# BLUEPRINT — Baek, *Optimality of Gerver's Sofa*, Chapters 3–8 (+ final assembly)

Source: arXiv:2411.19826v1 (author's PDF, uploaded 2026-09-17), read in full 2026-09-17 by Opus 5
(printed pp. 43–110).  Companion of `ch1-2.md`.  "Lean" = planned declaration / file.
Difficulty: **S** (≤ 1 day), **M** (days), **L** (weeks), **XL** (a project of its own).
Status flags as in `ch1-2.md`; everything below was *planned* at the time of writing unless marked.
The flags were not updated afterwards; the final state is described in `README.md`.

---

## 0. Architecture of the whole proof (what must be formalized, in order)

Main theorem (Thm 1.1.1, proof on p.110):

1. **Reduction to area ≥ 2.2** — Thm 1.5.1 `[PROOF-C-local]` + Hammersley lower bound `[PROOF-C-local]`.
2. **Monotone sofas** (Ch.2) — `[PROOF-C-local]`: `|S| ≤ |I(S)| = A_ω(C(I(S)))`.
3. **Existence of a balanced maximum cap `K_ω`** for each ω (Ch.3, Thm 3.5.2/3.5.5/3.5.6):
   `A_ω(K_ω) = max A_ω`, `N(K_ω) ⊆ K_ω`, `K_ω` is a Hausdorff limit of *maximum polygon caps*
   (which are *balanced*, Thm 3.4.9).
4. **ω = π/2** (Ch.4, Thm 1.5.2): if `A_ω(K_ω) ≥ 2.2` and ω < π/2, a rotated copy of `S_ω` has a
   movement with angle π/2; hence `max A_ω ≤ max A_{π/2}`.
5. **Injectivity condition** for balanced maximum caps with ω = π/2 (Ch.6, needs Ch.5).
6. **Upper bound** `A(K) ≤ Q(K, B_K, D_K)` for caps with injectivity (Ch.8.1–8.2, needs Ch.7).
7. **Q is concave** on the convex domain `L` (Ch.8.3, Mamikon, Ch.7.4).
8. **Gerver's cap is a critical point** of Q: `DQ ≤ 0` (Ch.8.4–8.5, needs Romik's ODEs for G),
   hence a global maximizer (Thm 7.1.5), and `Q(G) = |G|` (Thm 8.4.6).

Chain: `|S| ≤ max A_{π/2} = A(K*) ≤ Q(K*, B, D) ≤ Q(K_G, B_G, D_G) = |G|`.
The lower bound `|G| ≤ α` needs `IsMovingSofa gerversSofa` (upstream `sorry`, not in Baek; proved
downstream in round 38, §41).

## 1. Chapter 3 — Balanced maximum sofas and caps

### 3.1 Simple Nef polygons
| # | Statement | Lean plan | Deps | Diff. |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Def 3.1.1–3.1.2 | boolean functions; monotone ones | `Bool`-valued `Fin n → Prop → Prop` with monotonicity | — | S |
| Prop 3.1.1 | and/or-combinations are monotone | induction on a small syntax tree (`NefExpr`) | — | S |
| Def 3.1.3–3.1.4 | Nef polygon `E(H₁,…,Hₙ)`; simple = monotone `E` + distinct boundary lines | `nefSet (E) (H : Fin n → HalfPlane)` | — | S |
| **Thm 3.1.2** | pushing one defining half-plane by δ changes the area by `H¹(∂X ∩ lᵢ)·δ + O(δ²)` | cell decomposition by the other lines; in each open cell `X` is ∅, everything, or `Hᵢ`; Cavalieri + Lipschitz slice length | Mathlib `μH[1]`, Fubini | **L** |

*Formalization note.* Only two Nef polygons occur (`C_Θ(h)`, `N_Θ(h)`), and the theorem is used only
through its derivative form in Lemma 3.4.7.  A cheaper route: prove directly that
`δ ↦ |X_δ|` is differentiable at 0 with derivative = length of the boundary part on `lᵢ`, using
that on a small neighbourhood `X_δ ∖ X` (or `X ∖ X_δ`) is a union of thin trapezoids.  The
`H¹(∂X ∩ lᵢ)` can be replaced by an explicit "edge length" defined through 1-D Lebesgue measure on
the line.  Avoid general `μH[1]`.

### 3.2 Polygon caps and niches
| # | Statement | Lean plan | Deps | Diff. |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Def 3.2.1–3.2.2 | angle set `Θ = (ω, X)`, `Θ^⋄ = Θ ∪ (Θ+π/2) ∪ {ω, π/2}` | `structure AngleSet` (`Finset ℝ` ⊆ `(0,ω)`, nonempty) | — | S |
| Def 3.2.3 | polygon caps `K^c_Θ` | `IsPolyCap Θ K := IsCap K ω ∧ K = ⋂_{t ∈ Θ^⋄ ∪ {ω+π,3π/2}} H_K(t)` | Ch.2 | S |
| Def 3.2.4, Prop 3.2.1 | `C_Θ(K) = P_ω ∩ ⋂_{t∈Θ} Q⁺_K(t)` is a polygon cap ⊇ K; fixes polygon caps | as `Ccap` with finite index | Ch.2 | S |
| Def 3.2.5 | `N_Θ(K) = P_ω ∩ ⋃_{t∈Θ} Q⁻_K(t)` (**NB** printed with `P_ω`; Def 3.3.3 and §1.4.2 use the fan — see note) | `nicheΘ` | — | — |
| Prop 3.2.2 | `N_Θ(K) = N_Θ(C_Θ K) ⊆ N(K)` | | | S |
| Def 3.2.6, Thm 3.2.3 | `A_Θ(K) = |C_Θ K| − |N_Θ K|`; `A_ω ≤ A_Θ` | | Ch.2 | S |

*Note (possible typo).* Def 3.2.5 uses `P_ω` but Def 3.3.3/Prop 3.3.5 and the overview use
`F_h` (the fan).  `A_ω ≤ A_Θ` needs `N_Θ(K) ⊆ N(K)`, true either way; balancedness (Lemma 3.4.5)
is computed with the fan version.  **Formalize with the fan** (`fan ω`), which makes Prop 3.3.5
literally true; check where the difference could matter (only if `Q⁻_K(t) ∩ F_ω ⊄ P_ω`).

| # | Statement | Lean plan | Deps | Diff. |

| Def 3.5.1–3.5.2 | uniform angle sets `Θ_{ω,n}`; balanced maximum cap = `d_H`-limit of max polygon caps with `n_i` increasing powers of 2 | | | S |

| **Thm 3.5.4** | `N(K_ω) ⊆ K_ω` | `N_{Θ_j}(K_i) → N_{Θ_j}(K)` in `d_H` (!) | 3.4.10, 3.5.3 | M |
| **Thm 3.5.5** | `K_ω` maximizes `A_ω` | continuity of area; `|N_{Θ_m}(K_i)| → |N_{Θ_m}(K)|` | | M |

*Gap to watch (Thm 3.5.4).* "`N_{Θ_j}(K_i) → N_{Θ_j}(K)` in `d_H` (or `N_{Θ_j}(K)` empty)" is stated
without proof; the niche is a relatively open set, so Hausdorff convergence of the *closures* is the
right statement.  Formalize with closures (`X ⊆ Y` for `Y` compact survives).

| Def 2.1.13 / Thm 2.1.1 | surface area measure `σ_K(X) = H¹(⋃_{t∈X} e_K(t))` | **Proposed definition** (planar shortcut): `σ_K := ` total variation-type measure from `v⁺_K` — or directly `σ_K := ⟪v_t, d v⁺_K⟫` using `BoundedVariationOn.vectorMeasure` (Mathlib 2026 ✅) and prove the edge-length property | Mathlib vector measures | **L** |
| Def 5.1.1–5.1.4 | LS measure `df` for right-continuous BV `f`, `df({a}) = 0` | `BoundedVariationOn.vectorMeasure` restricted to `(a,b]` ✅ exists | | S–M |
| Prop 5.1.1 | linearity of `d` | | | S |
| Lemma 5.1.2 | integration by parts (Revuz–Yor Prop 4.5) | not in Mathlib (as far as checked) | | **M–L** |
| Lemma 5.1.3 | product rule if one factor continuous | from 5.1.2 | | S |
| Prop 5.1.4 | `f` AC ⇔ `df = r dt` | Mathlib `AbsolutelyContinuousOnInterval` + FTC | | M |
| Lemma 5.2.1 | `v⁺_K` has bounded variation | monotone coordinates on quarter intervals | | S–M |
| **Thm 5.2.2** | `d v⁺_K(t) = v_t σ_K` on `(a,b]` | polygons (telescoping) + approximation (Schneider 8.3.3) + weak convergence + Portmanteau (Mathlib ✅) — **or** automatic if σ_K is *defined* from `dv⁺_K` | | **L** (or S with the definition above) |

*Design decision to take before Ch.5.* Defining `σ_K` from `dv⁺_K` makes Thm 5.2.2 nearly
definitional; the costs move to (a) `σ_K ≥ 0`, (b) `σ_K({t}) = |e_K(t)|`, (c) weak continuity
`K ↦ σ_K` (used in 4.1.3, 6.4.2, 6.4.3), (d) `|K| = ½∫h dσ` (Thm 7.1.3) and mixed volume
(Thm 8.5.1), (e) convex-linearity (Thm 7.1.2(3)).  In the plane (c)–(e) have elementary proofs
via `v⁺_K`.  Recommended.

### 8.2 Definition of Q

## 8. Risk register (new, from the full reading)

* **R-J (Jordan/Green).** Thm 7.2.3, Lemma 7.3.5, Lemmas 8.2.2–8.2.3, Thm 8.4.6 use the Jordan curve
  theorem and Green's theorem for rectifiable curves; neither is in Mathlib.  Mitigation: every
  region involved is bounded by graphs (injectivity makes the core x-monotone; tails are convex
  arcs; `∂K'` is a convex curve), so areas can be computed with Fubini/1-D integrals.  This must be
  designed carefully before Ch.7–8 (a separate design note).
* **R-G (Gerver's sofa).** Thm 8.4.1 (G is a monotone sofa with the stated boundary structure) is
  explicitly *not* proved in the paper (Rem 8.4.1); Thm 8.4.2 is imported from [Rom18].  A full
  formalization must prove these from explicit formulas (Romik's closed forms).  Upstream's
  `isMovingSofa_gerversSofa` is related.
* **R-6.1.2.** Thm 6.1.2 (`C(G)` satisfies injectivity) is proved by citing "Theorem 2 of [Ger92]
  explicitly constructs a sequence of maximum polygon sofas converging to G".  Taken literally this
  would make G a balanced *maximum* sofa, i.e. already optimal among ω = π/2 sofas — the claim needs
  checking against [Ger92].  Rem 6.1.1 gives the alternative: verify (1)–(3) of Def 6.1.2 for G
  directly from Romik's formulas (numerically `f_K > 1` is plotted in Fig 1.10).  **Plan: use the
  direct route.**  (Not yet checked against [Ger92]; 🟡.)
* **R-BM.** Thm 8.1.1 uses Brunn–Minkowski to keep `|K| ≥ 2.2` under convex combinations.  The
  constraint is only used in Lemmas 8.1.4–8.1.5 (for caps in the image of `K ↦ (K,B_K,D_K)`),
  not in 8.3/8.5.  Plan: define `L` without it; check every use.
* **R-σ.** Weak continuity of `K ↦ σ_K` (Schneider 4.2.1) is used in 4.1.4 (only point masses),
  6.4.2 and 6.4.3 (Portmanteau for open sets and u.s.c./l.s.c. integrands).  Needs the planar
  definition first.
* **R-num.** Numerical lemmas: 4.2.2, 4.2.4 (values at `sec⁻¹ 2.2`, `tan⁻¹ 2.2`), 6.5.3 (π),
  8.1.4 (`2 sec φ + 2 tan φ < 2.2` with `φ ≤ 0.040`), Def 8.1.2 (`φ ∈ [0.039, 0.040]`).
* **R-up.** The final statement is about upstream `gerversSofa` (defined via `ABφθSpec`); it must
  be identified with Romik's G (same curves, same φ, θ).
* **R-3.2.5.** `P_ω` vs fan in Def 3.2.5 (see §1).

## 9. Suggested milestone order after M2

* **M3a (convex-body infrastructure):** vertices + Thm 2.1.3, mirror symmetry `M_ω` (Prop 2.5.4),
  Hausdorff = sup-norm of support functions, Blaschke, continuity of area.
* **M3b (Ch.3 polygon part):** angle sets, polygon caps, Nef formulas, Thm 3.1.2 (derivative form),
  balancedness 3.4.9, 3.4.10, existence 3.4.3, limits 3.5.x.
* **M4 (Ch.4):** mostly elementary + numerics; needs only point-mass u.s.c. of edge lengths.
* **M5 (planar surface area measure):** definition via `v⁺_K`, Thm 5.2.2, weak continuity,
  `|K| = ½∫h dσ`, integration by parts.
* **M6 (Ch.6):** heavy analysis; numerics of 6.5.3.
* **M7–M8:** first a design note for R-J; then Q, concavity, Gerver (R-G is the largest single
  item and may deserve its own milestone "MG").

## 10. Formalization design notes for Chapter 3 (2026-09-17, after M3a)

**Status of the skeleton.** `Sofa/Main.lean` proves `Interfaces G → sofaConstant ≤ volume G`
(axiom-clean for an arbitrary target set `G`).  The interface fields are exactly Thm 3.5.2 (I1a),
Thm 3.5.5 (I1c), Thm 1.5.2 (I2), and the Chapter 5–8 bound (I3).  Chapter 3 must discharge
(I1a), (I1c) (and (I1b) = Thm 3.5.4, used inside (I2)).

**D1 (slice lengths instead of `H¹`).**  For a line family `l(t, s)` parametrized by
`r ↦ s u_t + r v_t`, the slice of a finite boolean combination `X` of half-planes is a finite
boolean combination of half-lines whose endpoints are affine in `s` and in all offsets.
Lemma: `|len(X ∩ l(t,s)) − len(X' ∩ l(t,s'))| ≤ Σ_j |r_j − r_j'|` (the symmetric difference lies in
the union of the intervals between corresponding endpoints; parallel defining lines contribute
nothing as long as their offset differs from `s`).  Hence slice lengths are jointly continuous.

**D2 (area derivative, replaces Thm 3.1.2).**  For monotone `E` and `X_δ` obtained by pushing one
defining half-plane by `δ > 0`, `X ⊆ X_δ` and `X_δ ∖ X = strip_δ ∩ (F ∖ G)` where `F`, `G` are `E`
with the `i`-th input set to true/false (boolean combinations of the *other* half-planes).
Cavalieri + D1 give `|X_δ| − |X| = δ · len((F ∖ G) ∩ l_i) + o(δ)`.  Only `o(δ)` is needed for
Thm 3.4.9.  (Multi-offset version by telescoping + joint continuity, if needed.)

**D3 (polylines as graphs).**  Both the upper boundary `δK` of a polygon cap and the polyline `p_K`
are graphs of explicit piecewise-linear functions of `x`:
* `F_ω ∖ X` (with `X = ⋃_{t∈Θ} Q⁻_K(t)`) is `{y ≥ g(x)}` inside the fan, where
  `g = max(f_ω, max_{t∈Θ} min(L^b_t, L^d_t))`, `f_ω(x) = max(0, −x cot ω)` (fan bottom), and
  `L^b_t(x) = (h(t) − 1 − x cos t)/sin t`, `L^d_t(x) = (h(t+π/2) − 1 + x sin t)/cos t`;
* `K = {lower(x) ≤ y ≤ upper(x)}` with `upper = min_{s ∈ Θ^⋄} (h(s) − x cos s)/sin s`.
Displacement identities (`C⁺_K(ω) − A⁻_K(0) = Σ τ_K(t) v_t = Σ σ_K(t) v_t`) follow from the
fundamental theorem of calculus with *right* derivatives (Mathlib's
`integral_eq_sub_of_hasDeriv_right_of_le`) and a partition of `[C₀, A₀]` into the sets where a given
affine piece is active; each active piece with normal `t` contributes `(length) · v_t`.
Define `τ_K(t)` as that length (graph length of the active piece); relate it to the slice
length of D2 on the wall `b_K(t)` (projection along `v_t` has Jacobian `1/sin t`).

**D4 (Lemma 3.4.6 / Thm 3.4.10).**  With D3, Lemma 3.4.6 is `⟨Σ(σ−τ)v_t, u₀⟩ = 0`, and
Thm 3.4.10 follows the printed proof with partial displacements `c_t ∈ [0, τ_K(t)]` read off the
graph between `x_p` and `A₀`.  (Alternative for 3.4.6: translation invariance of `|C_Θ(h)|` and
`|N_Θ(h)|` in the direction `v₀` plus the multi-offset derivative.)

**D5 (typo).**  In the proof of Lemma 3.4.7, case `t ∈ {ω, π/2}`, the displayed formula should
read `|N_Θ(h⁺)| = |N_Θ(h)| − (σ_K(t+π) − τ_K(t)) ε + O(ε²)` (pushing the fan's bottom line up
shrinks the niche); the lemma's conclusion is unaffected.  (risk R16, harmless)

**D6 (Thm 3.4.3 existence).**  Paper route: continuity of `A_Θ` in `d_H` + Blaschke
(`exists_tendsto_hausdorffDist` ✅) on `B_Θ`; continuity of `|K|` needs Mathlib's
`Convex.addHaar_frontier` (module `Mathlib.Analysis.Convex.Measure`, available with full Mathlib).
For polygon caps, `|N_Θ(K)|` is continuous in the finitely many support values (D1 + Cavalieri).

**Planned files.** `Sofa/Nef.lean` (D1, D2), `Sofa/Envelope.lean` (D3), `Sofa/PolyBalance.lean`
(3.4.4–3.4.9), `Sofa/PolyMax.lean` (3.4.3, 3.4.10), `Sofa/BalancedMax.lean` (§3.5 → (I1)).

---

## 11. Revised route for §3.3–3.5: the graph representation (2026-09-17, Opus 5, after Nef.lean)

**Observation.** All normal angles in `Θ^⋄` lie in `(0, π)`, so every defining line of `C_Θ(h)` and
`N_Θ(h)` is the graph of an affine function of `x`:
`ℓ_{s,c}(x) = (c − x cos s)/sin s` (slope `−cot s`, strictly increasing in `s`).  With `D = Θ^⋄`,
`F₀ = {ω, π/2}`:
* `U_h = min_{s∈D} ℓ_{s,h(s)}` (concave), `f_h = max_{s∈F₀} ℓ_{s,h(s)−1}` (fan bottom),
  `R_h = max_{t∈Θ} min(ℓ_{t,h(t)−1}, ℓ_{t+π/2,h(t+π/2)−1})` (niche roof);
* `C_Θ(h) = {f_h ≤ y ≤ U_h}`, `N_Θ(h) = {f_h ≤ y < R_h}`,
  `A_Θ(h) = ∫ ((U_h − f_h)⁺ − (R_h − f_h)⁺) dx` (Cavalieri, `volume_eq_lintegral_slice` at `t = 0`).

**G1 (piecewise-affine calculus).** A continuous selection `g` of finitely many pairwise distinct
affine functions is locally one of them off the finite tie set; hence (FTC off a countable set,
`integral_eq_of_hasDerivAt_off_countable_of_le`)
`g(b) − g(a) = Σ_i slope_i · |{g = ℓ_i} ∩ [a,b]|`, and the active sets partition `ℝ` a.e.

**G2 (derivative, replaces Thm 3.1.2 and Lemma 3.4.7).** Pushing `h(t)` (`t ∈ D`) by `ε` moves
exactly the lines `ℓ_{t,h(t)}` and `ℓ_{t,h(t)−1}` by `ε/sin t`.  Dominated differentiation
(`hasDerivAt_integral_of_dominated_loc_of_lip`; Lipschitz bound `4/sin t` on a bounded window)
gives `d/dε A_Θ(h + ε e_t) |₀ = σ(t) − τ(t)` with (`I = {f < U}`)
* `σ(t) sin t = |{x ∈ I : U = ℓ_{t,h(t)}}|` (= edge length of the cap),
* `τ(t) sin t = |{f < R, R = ℓ_{t,h(t)−1}}| + |{x ∈ I : f = ℓ_{t,h(t)−1}}| − |{f < R, f = ℓ_{t,h(t)−1}}|`
  (a single formula for all `t ∈ D`; two of the three terms vanish depending on `t ∈ F₀`).
Both sides are two-sided derivatives, so the inward push of `H⁺(t, h(t)−1)` for `t ∈ F₀`
(risk D5) needs no separate treatment.

**G3 (Lemma 3.4.6 becomes a partition identity).** By G1,
`Σ_t σ(t) sin t = |I|` and `Σ_t τ(t) sin t = |{f<R}| + |I| − |{f<R}| = |I|`,
so `Σ_t (σ(t) − τ(t)) sin t = 0` — no polyline `p_K`, no Thm 3.4.4, no Lemma 3.4.5.
`τ ≥ 0` uses the gap theorem 2.5.5: `{f < R} ⊆ I = (x_C, h(0))`.

**G4 (Thm 3.4.10 by a rearrangement inequality, no mirror).** Let `g = max(f, R)`.  For a balanced
polygon cap and `s ∈ D`, pick `x*` with `U(x*) = ℓ_s(x*)` (edge point).  For `t < s` the pieces
`{U = ℓ_t} ∩ I` lie in `[x*, h(0)]`, for `t > s` in `(−∞, x*]`.  Applying G1 to `g − ℓ_s` on
`[x, h(0)]` and to `U − ℓ_s` on `[x*, h(0)]`, termwise comparison (balancedness) gives
`g(x) ≤ ℓ_s(x)` for all `x ∈ I`; hence `g ≤ U` on `I` and `N_Θ(K) ⊆ K`.

**G5 (existence, Thm 3.4.3, in h-space).** Maximize over polygon caps with `o_ω ∈ K`,
`A_Θ ≥ 0`; supports lie in a compact box (Lemma 3.4.2 via an explicit rectangle inside the
wedge `T_K(t)`); take a convergent subsequence of `(h_n, bottom points)`; the limit `h*` gives the
polygon cap `C_Θ(h*)` with `A_Θ(h_{C_Θ(h*)}) ≥ A_Θ(h*)` (Prop 3.3.7).  `A_Θ` is Lipschitz in `h`.

**G6 (§3.5 without continuity of area).** Thm 3.5.4: niche membership is an open condition in
the support values, so `N_{Θ_j}(K) ⊆ K` follows pointwise from `N_{Θ_j}(K_i) ⊆ K_i`.
Thm 3.5.5 needs only *upper* semicontinuity of `|K_i|` (cthickening) and Fatou's lemma for
`|N_{Θ_m}(K_i)|`; `Convex.addHaar_frontier` is only used to get `int K ≠ ∅` for `IsCap K`
(alternatively `addHaar_affineSubspace`).

**Files.** `Sofa/Graph.lean` (G1, Cavalieri for graph regions, inf'/sup' calculus),
`Sofa/PolyGraph.lean` (U, f, R, `A_Θ(h)`, G2, G3), `Sofa/PolyBalance.lean` (Lemma 3.4.8,
Prop 3.3.7, Thm 3.4.9, G4), `Sofa/PolyMax.lean` (G5), `Sofa/BalancedMax.lean` (G6 → (I1)).
`Sofa/Nef.lean` (D1–D2) stays as a verified alternative but is no longer on the critical path.
(Correction, 2026-09-24: its Cavalieri lemma `volume_eq_lintegral_slice`, with `slice`, is still
used, through `Sofa/Graph.lean`; only its Nef-polygon area derivative is not.)

---

## 12. §3.5 as formalized (2026-09-17, Opus 5, `Sofa/BalancedMax.lean`)

The three theorems of §3.5 are proved; `Sofa/Main.lean` no longer states (I1a)–(I1c) as
interfaces.  Route actually taken (it differs from G6 in one place: Thm 3.5.4 is *not* proved by
a "`N_{Θ_j}(K) ⊆ K` for each fixed `j`" argument but by the same eventual-membership lemma that
Thm 3.5.5 uses, which avoids Lemma 3.5.3 entirely).

**Key observation used throughout.** For a polygon cap `K` (`IsPolyCap ω Θ K`) one has
`C_Θ(K) = K`: indeed `C_Θ(K) = capH ω Θ (h_K)` (`polyCap_eq_capH`) and `capH ω Θ (h_K) = K`
(`capH_supportFn`), because `h_K(ω) = h_K(π/2) = 1` turns the fan floor `f` into the two
constraints `⟪p, u_ω⟫ ≥ 0`, `p₂ ≥ 0` coming from `h_K(ω+π) = h_K(3π/2) = 0`.  Hence
`A_Θ(K) = |K| − |N_Θ(K)| ≤ |K|`.

**Thm 3.5.2 (`exists_isBalancedMaxCap`).**
`n_i = 2^{i+1}`, `Θ_i = Θ_{ω,n_i}`, `K_i` a maximum polygon cap for `Θ_i` (`exists_isMaxPolyCap`).
1. `A_{Θ_i}(K_i) ≥ A_{Θ_i}(C_{Θ_i}(1)) ≥ 1/4` (`quarter_le_polySofaArea_one`: the unit square
   region `0 ≤ x ≤ 1/2`, `0 ≤ y ≤ 1/2` is inside `C_Θ(h ≡ 1)` and the niche is empty).
2. `ω/2 ∈ Θ_i` (`half_mem_uniformAngles`), so the *uniform* ball of `exists_ball` (Lemma 3.4.2,
   with `t₁ = ω/2`) applies to every `i`: `K_i ⊆ B(0, R)` with `R` independent of `i`.
3. Blaschke (`exists_tendsto_hausdorffDist`) gives a subsequence `K_{φ(i)} → L`.
4. `|L| ≥ 1/4`: upper semicontinuity `limsup |K_i| ≤ |L|`
   (`le_volume_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist`, via `tendsto_measure_cthickening_of_isCompact`).
5. `L` is a cap (`isCap_of_tendsto`): the four support values pass to the limit by
   `abs_supportFn_sub_le_hausdorffDist`; `|L| ≠ 0` + `Convex.interior_nonempty_iff_affineSpan_eq_top`
   + `Measure.addHaar_affineSubspace` give an interior point `q`, whence a uniform margin
   `⟪q, u_s⟫ + δ/2 ≤ h_L(s)`; then for `p` in the half-plane intersection the segment points
   `p_m = (1 − 1/(m+1)) p + (1/(m+1)) q` lie in `K_i` for large `i`, hence in `L`, hence `p ∈ L`.

**Density lemma (`eventually_mem_polyNiche`).** For `t ∈ (0, ω)` and `ε > 0`,
`⌊t·m/ω⌋ + 1` produces `t' ∈ Θ_{ω,m}` with `t < t' ≤ t + ω/m`, so
`∀ᶠ m, ∃ t' ∈ Θ_{ω,m}, t < t' < t + ε` (`exists_mem_uniformAngles`).  Combining
* `|⟪p, u_{s'}⟫ − ⟪p, u_s⟫| ≤ ‖p‖ |s' − s|`  (`norm_u_sub_u_le`),
* `|h_K(s') − h_K(s)| ≤ R |s' − s|`  (`abs_supportFn_sub_le`),
* `|h_{K_i}(s') − h_K(s')| ≤ d_H(K_i, K)`  (`abs_supportFn_sub_le_hausdorffDist`),
with `ε = g/(2(‖p‖+R+1))` and `d_H < g/2`, where `g` is the strict slack of `p ∈ N(K)`, gives
`∀ p ∈ N(K), ∀ᶠ i, p ∈ N_{Θ_i}(K_i)` — one lemma serving both Thm 3.5.4 and Thm 3.5.5.

**Thm 3.5.4 (`IsBalancedMaxCap.niche_subset`).** `N_{Θ_i}(K_i) ⊆ K_i` (Thm 3.4.9 + Thm 3.4.10),
so `p ∈ N(K)` lies in `K_i` for large `i`, so `infDist(p, K) ≤ d_H(K_i, K) → 0` and `K` is closed.

**Thm 3.5.5 (`IsBalancedMaxCap.isMax`).** For an arbitrary cap `K'`:
`A_ω(K') ≤ A_{Θ_i}(K')` (Thm 3.2.3) `= A_{Θ_i}(C_{Θ_i}(K')) ≤ A_{Θ_i}(K_i)`, where the middle
equality holds because `h_{C_Θ(K')} = h_{K'}` on `Θ^⋄` (`supportFn_polyCap`) and both `C_Θ` and
`N_Θ` depend only on those values (`polySofaArea_congr`), and `C_{Θ_i}(K')` is a polygon cap
(`isPolyCap_polyCap`, from `isPolyCap_capH` — the touching points come from the four support
values of a cap, using `u_{s+π} = −u_s`).  Then, for every `ε > 0` and large `i`,
* `|K_i| ≤ |K| + ε/2`  (`eventually_volume_le_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist`),
* `|N_{Θ_i}(K_i)| ≥ |N(K)| − ε/2`  (`eventually_le_volume_of_eventually_mem`: with
  `A_m = ⋂_{i ≥ m} N_{Θ_i}(K_i)`, `N(K) ⊆ ⋃_m A_m` by the density lemma, and
  `tendsto_measure_iUnion_atTop` for the increasing family),

so `A_ω(K') ≤ A_{Θ_i}(K_i) = |K_i| − |N_{Θ_i}(K_i)| ≤ A_ω(K) + ε`.

**No longer needed.** Lemma 3.5.3 (`subset_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist`) and the continuity of the
area functional under Hausdorff convergence; `Sofa/Nef.lean` and `Sofa/Mirror.lean` remain
verified but off the critical path.

## 13. Chapter 4 as formalized (2026-09-17, Opus 5, `Sofa/SideLength.lean`, `Sofa/RightAngle.lean`)

### 13.1 §4.1 without the surface area measure

The paper states Thm 4.1.2 / 4.1.4 as `w°_K ≤ σ_K(π/2)` and deduces from them that the top edge of
`K` reaches `σ_K(π/2)` to the left of `o_ω`.  We state that conclusion **directly**:

> **Thm 4.1.2 / 4.1.4 (as formalized).** For a maximum polygon cap (resp. a balanced maximum cap)
> `K` with `ω < π/2` and every `g ∈ [0, w°_K]`, the point `o_ω − g u₀ = (c_ω − g, 1)` lies in `K`.

This removes the surface area measure from Chapter 4 entirely, and with it **Thm 4.1.3** (weak
convergence `σ_{K_n} → σ_K`, quoted from Schneider): the limit in Thm 4.1.4 is taken on the
*points* `o_ω − g u₀`, using only that `K` is closed (`mem_of_eventually_mem`).

* `gapWinf K ω = sInf (w_K '' (0,ω))`, `gapZinf` (Def 4.1.1); `gapWinf_mirror` relates them.
* Lemma 4.1.1 is only needed one-sidedly: `gapWinf K ω − (1 + sec ω) d_H(K,K') ≤ gapWinf K' ω`.
* `gapWinf_le_supportFn_zero`: `w°_K ≤ h_K(0)`, by `w_K(t) → h_K(0)` as `t → ω⁻`.
* **Thm 4.1.2** in the graph representation.  Write `h = h_K`.
  - `actF(π/2) = [0,∞)` and `floorF ≥ 0` (because `h(ω) = h(π/2) = 1`), so the bottom edge of the
    cap is `[0, h(0)]` and `|actF(π/2) ∩ posU| = h(0)`.
  - `actR(π/2) ∩ posR = ∅`, and `[0,∞) ∩ posR ⊆ [0, h(0) − w°_K)` (on `posR` some
    `ℓ_{t,h(t)−1}(x) > 0`, i.e. `x < (h(t)−1)/cos t = h(0) − w_K(t)`).
  - Hence `τ_K(π/2) = h(0) − |[0,∞) ∩ posR| ≥ w°_K` — this is the paper's "segment `s` of length
    `≥ w°_K` inside `e_K(3π/2)` disjoint from `N_Θ(K)`", done with measures instead of `H¹`.
  - Balancedness (Thm 3.4.9) gives `σ_K(π/2) = τ_K(π/2) ≥ w°_K`.
  - `A' = {U = 1} ∩ [x_C, c_ω]` is closed, ordConnected and contains `c_ω`; the top edge
    `E = actU(π/2) ∩ posU` is contained in `A' = [a₀, c_ω]`, so `σ_K(π/2) = |E| ≤ c_ω − a₀` and
    `c_ω − σ_K(π/2) ∈ A'`, i.e. `(c_ω − g, 1) ∈ K` for `g ≤ σ_K(π/2)`.
    (`x_C ≥ −tan ω` because `h_K(ω+π/2) ≤ sec ω` for a cap, which is `supportFn_left_le`.)

### 13.2 §4.2

* **Cavalieri with a restricted `x`-range** (`measureReal_between_restrict`): replace `lo` by
  `S.piecewise lo (hi + 1)` and reuse `measureReal_between_Icc`.
* `|P_ω ∩ {⟪p,u₀⟫ > c}| = (sec ω − c)(1 − c cos ω)/(2 sin ω)` for `c_ω ≤ c ≤ sec ω` (one interval
  integral), `|P_ω ∩ {⟪p,u₀⟫ ≤ c_ω}| = tan ω/2 + c_ω` (two), hence **`|P_ω| = sec ω`**.
* **Lemma 4.2.2.**  The two corner triangles `T_r = P_ω ∩ {⟪p,u₀⟫ > D}` and
  `T_t = P_ω ∩ {⟪p,v_ω⟫ > D}` are mirror images (`mirror ω '' T_r = T_t`, so `|T_t| = |T_r|` for
  free) and disjoint when `D ≥ c_ω`; a cap with `h_K(0), h_K(ω+π/2) < D` misses both, so
  `|K| ≤ sec ω − (sec ω − D)(1 − D cos ω)/sin ω = c_ω + 2d − d² cot ω` for `D = d + c_ω`.
  With `d = d_{ω,min}` this is `< 2.2` — both cases reduce to polynomial inequalities in
  `s = sin ω`, `c = cos ω` under `s²+c²=1`, `c ≤ 5/11` (and `s < 2.2c` resp. `s ≥ 2.2c`).
  `d_{ω,min}` is defined by the *trigonometric* condition `sin ω < 2.2 cos ω` (equivalent to
  `ω < arctan 2.2` on `(0,π/2)`), which avoids `arctan` entirely.
* **Lemma 4.2.4** is replaced by two polynomial facts: `d_{ω,min} sin ω > 1`
  (`one_lt_dmin_mul_sin`) and `d² cos ω + 4 cos ω sin²ω < 2 d sin ω` for `d = d_{ω,min}`
  (`dmin_ineq`), the latter being exactly `(1 − d cot ω)² + 4cos²ω < 1` cleared of denominators.
  The paper's convexity argument (Lemma 4.2.3 + four endpoint evaluations) is replaced by two
  `nlinarith` certificates after establishing `sin ω ≥ 0.8907` (resp. `≥ 0.9103`) and
  `cos²ω ≤ 25/146`.  (The statement of Lemma 4.2.4 says `ω ∈ [arctan 2.2, π/2)`, but its proof —
  and its use in Thm 4.2.5 — cover `ω ∈ [arccos(5/11), π/2)`; we prove the latter.)
* **Mirror stability of polygon caps** (needed for the WLOG in Thm 4.2.5): for a `t ↦ ω−t`
  symmetric angle set (in particular `Θ_{ω,n}`), `polyCap`, `polyNiche` and `A_Θ` are
  mirror-equivariant, `IsPolyCap ω Θ K ↔ polyCap ω Θ K = K` (for a cap), `mirror ω (o_ω) = o_ω`,
  hence `IsMaxPolyCap` and `IsBalancedMaxCap` are stable under `M_ω`.
* **Thm 4.2.5.**  `r = (h_K(0), r_y)` with `⟪r, u_ω⟫ = 1`; for `t ∈ (0,ω)` the cone decomposition
  `u_t = α u_0 + β u_ω` (`inner_u_decomp_cone`, `α,β ≥ 0`) gives `h_K(t) ≤ ⟪r, u_t⟫`, and with
  `g² + r_y² = 1` and Cauchy–Schwarz `⟪(g, r_y), u_t⟫ ≤ 1` one gets `g ≤ w_K(t)` for every `t`,
  i.e. `g ≤ w°_K` (`le_gapWinf_of_sq`).  Then `q₁ = o_ω − g u₀ ∈ K` by Thm 4.1.4, and with
  `t = π/2 − ω` the three vertices of `Δ_ω` satisfy the two closed half-plane conditions, the
  binding ones being `d sin ω ≥ 1` and `g cos ω ≥ 2cos²ω` (Lemma 4.2.4).  The other case
  (`h_K(ω+π/2) ≥ d_{ω,min} + c_ω`) follows by applying the same result to `M_ω K` and transporting
  with `t ↦ 2ω − π/2`; `Δ_ω` is mirror-invariant.

**Still open in Chapter 4**: Thm 1.5.2 itself — the construction of the movement with rotation
angle `π/2` (rotate `S_ω` by `π/2 − ω` inside the horizontal hallway using that `P_ω \ Δ_ω` has
width `≤ 1` in the directions `t ∈ [ω, π/2]`, then concatenate with the original movement).

### 13.3 The geometric input to Thm 1.5.2 (`Sofa/Width.lean`)

Write `p = a u₀ + b v_ω` (a basis for `ω < π/2`), i.e.
`a = coA ω p = ⟪p, u_ω⟫ / cos ω`, `b = coB ω p = p₂ / cos ω`; then

* `⟪p, u_t⟫ = a cos t + b sin(t − ω)`  (`inner_eq_coord`),
* `P_ω = {0 ≤ a, b ≤ sec ω}` — a *square* in these coordinates,
* `Δ_ω = {a, b ≥ 0, a + b ≤ c_ω}` — the standard simplex.

**`mem_niche_of_coord_lt`.**  If `a, b ≥ 0` and `a + b < c_ω` then `p ∈ N(K)`.  Indeed `p ∈ F_ω`
(from `a, b ≥ 0`), and for the angle `t ∈ (0,ω)` of Thm 4.2.5, with `M = h_K(t) − 1`,
`M' = h_K(t+π/2) − 1`:
`⟪p, u_t⟫ = a cos t + b sin(t−ω) ≤ a cos t < c_ω cos t ≤ M` (because `sin(t−ω) ≤ 0 ≤ b` and
`0 < cos t`), and `⟪p, u_{t+π/2}⟫ = −a sin t + b cos(t−ω) ≤ b cos(t−ω) < c_ω cos(t−ω) ≤ M'`.

**`width_le_one`.**  For `p, q ∈ P_ω` with `a_q + b_q ≥ c_ω` and `t ∈ [ω, π/2]`, put
`α = cos t ≥ 0`, `β = sin(t−ω) ≥ 0`.  Then
`⟪p − q, u_t⟫ ≤ sec ω (α+β) − c_ω min(α,β)`, and the two cases are *identities*:

* `min = α`:  `sec ω (α+β) − c_ω α = (β + α sin ω)/cos ω = sin t ≤ 1`;
* `min = β`:  `sec ω (α+β) − c_ω β = (α + β sin ω)/cos ω = cos(t − ω) ≤ 1`.

Combining with Thm 3.5.4 (`N(K) ⊆ K`) gives, for any `S ⊆ K ∖ N(K)` (in particular the monotone
sofa `S_ω`), `h_S(t) + h_S(t+π) ≤ 1` for all `t ∈ [ω, π/2]`
(`supportFn_add_supportFn_le_one`) — i.e. `S_ω` fits in the horizontal hallway after any rotation
by `−φ` with `φ ∈ [0, π/2 − ω]`.

**What is left for Thm 1.5.2** is only the *movement*: take `S' = R_{π/2−ω}(S_ω)` placed far to
the left in `H_L`, (i) rotate it clockwise by `π/2 − ω` — legal at every intermediate angle by the
width bound, with a continuous vertical correction given by `−h(·)(3π/2)` — (ii) translate right
to the initial position of `S_ω` (a horizontal translation inside `H_L`), (iii) follow the original
movement of `S_ω`.  The total rotation angle is `−(π/2 − ω) + (−ω) = −π/2`.

### 13.4 The construction for Thm 1.5.2 (formalized: `Sofa/RotateSofa.lean`, `Sofa/Thm152.lean`)

Let `φ = π/2 − ω ∈ (0, π/4)`, and let `A = tr c S_ω` be the sofa in the position where
`IsMovingSofa A m` holds, with `m t p = rot (θ t) p + m t 0`, `θ 0 = 0`, `θ 1 = −ω`.
Write `h_A` for its support function and `R` for a bound `|h_A| ≤ R`.

Two facts about rotations (both one-liners):
`⟪rot α p, u_s⟫ = ⟪p, u_{s−α}⟫`, hence `h_{rot α '' A}(s) = h_A(s − α)`.

**New sofa.** `S' := rot φ '' A`, `c₀ := pt x₀ (h_A(3π/2 − φ))` with `x₀ := −R − 1`.

**New motion** (`ψ = θ'`, `w` the translation; `m' t p = rot (ψ t) (p − c₀) + w t`, so that
`m' t p = rot (ψ t) p + m' t 0` and `m' 0 = id`):

| `t` | `ψ t` | `w t` | placed set |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[0, 1/3]` | `−3tφ` | `pt x₀ (h_A(3π/2 − ψ t − φ))` | `rot (ψ t + φ) '' A` shifted |
| `[1/3, 2/3]` | `−φ` | `(2 − 3t) • pt x₀ (h_A(3π/2))` | `A` shifted |
| `[2/3, 1]` | `−φ + θ(3t−2)` | — (`m' t := m (3t−2) ∘ m' (2/3)`) | `m(3t−2) '' A` |

* Phase 1 lands in `horizontalHallway`: the `y`-range of `rot (ψ+φ) '' A` shifted by
  `h_A(3π/2 − ψ − φ)` is `[0, h_A(π/2 − α) + h_A(3π/2 − α)]` with `α = ψ + φ ∈ [0, φ]`, i.e.
  `[0, h_A(t) + h_A(t+π)]` with `t = π/2 − α ∈ [ω, π/2]` — at most `1` by
  `supportFn_add_supportFn_le_one`; the `x`-range is `≤ x₀ + R ≤ −1 ≤ 1`.
* Phase 2 is a straight-line translation of `A` from `pt x₀ (h_A(3π/2))` to `0`; since
  `A ⊆ horizontalHallway`, its `y`-range is `[−h_A(3π/2), 1]` and every intermediate shift
  `s · h_A(3π/2)`, `s ∈ [0,1]`, keeps it inside `[0, 1]`; the `x`-shift only moves it left.
* Phase 3 is the original movement, so `m' t '' (tr c₀ S') = m (3t−2) '' A ⊆ hallway`, and
  `m' 1 '' (tr c₀ S') = m 1 '' A ⊆ verticalHallway`.
* `ψ 1 = −φ + θ 1 = −(π/2 − ω) − ω = −π/2`, so `IsSofaWithAngle S' (π/2)`; and `|S'| = |A| = |S_ω|
  = A_ω(K)`.  Feeding `S'` through `stdPos_tr_stdVec` and `Imono` gives the monotone sofa with
  rotation angle `π/2` required by interface (I2).

**As formalized** (`Sofa/RotateSofa.lean`, `isSofaWithAngle_rot_image`): the rigid motion is
`rigid α c w := rotateTranslate (α : Real.Angle) (−c + rot (−α) w)`, so that
`rigid α c w q = rot α (q − c) + w`, `rigid 0 c c = id` and continuity is
`continuous_rigid` (from `continuous_motion_of_continuous_apply`).  The three phases are glued
with `Continuous.if_le` at `t = 1/3` and `t = 2/3`, and phase 3 reparametrizes the original
motion by `Set.projIcc 0 1 _ (3t − 2)` (`continuous_projIcc`).  The membership computations use
`coord_rot_add_zero`/`coord_rot_add_one` (`(rot α q + v) 0 = ⟪q, u_{−α}⟫ + v 0` and
`(rot α q + v) 1 = ⟪q, u_{π/2−α}⟫ + v 1`), which turn "the placed set is in the hallway" into
three support-function inequalities.

Interface (I2) itself is `Sofa/Thm152.lean`
(`IsBalancedMaxCap.exists_monotoneSofa_pi_div_two`): Thm 3.5.4 → Thm 2.5.9 gives the monotone
sofa `S` with `C(S) = K`; Thm 2.4.3 gives `S = K \ N(K)`; Thm 2.5.10 gives `|S| = A_ω(K)`;
`Sofa/Width.lean` gives the width bound; `isSofaWithAngle_rot_image` rotates it; `volume_rot_image`
(rotations are `LinearIsometryEquiv`s, hence measure-preserving) keeps the area; and
`stdPos_tr_stdVec` + `Imono_props` produce the monotone sofa of rotation angle `π/2`.

## 14. Chapter 5 as formalized (2026-09-18, Opus 5, `Sofa/SurfaceArea.lean`, `Sofa/SurfaceThm.lean`)

### 14.1 `σ_K` as a Lebesgue–Stieltjes measure (design decision)

Mathlib v4.33.1 has `StieltjesFunction` (monotone + right-continuous `ℝ → ℝ`, with its measure,
`measure_Ioc`, `measure_singleton`) but **no vector-valued LS measure for BV functions**.  So the
blueprint's option "define `σ_K` from `d v⁺_K`" is realized in the following concrete way.

The product rule says that if `d v⁺_K = v_t σ_K(dt)`, then, since `dv_t/dt = −u_t` and
`⟪v⁺_K(t), u_t⟫ = h_K(t)`,

    `d ⟪v⁺_K(t), v_t⟫ = σ_K(dt) − h_K(t) dt`.

So we **define**

    `arcFn K t := ⟪v⁺_K(t), v_t⟫ + ∫_0^t h_K`,      `σ_K := (arcFn K).measure`,

which gives `σ_K((a,b]) = ⟪v⁺_K(b), v_b⟫ − ⟪v⁺_K(a), v_a⟫ + ∫_a^b h_K` *by construction*.
`arcFn K = edgeMax K + (primitive of h_K)`, and since `edgeMax K t = ⟪v⁺_K(t), v_t⟫`, the same
identity reads `edgeMax K t = arcFn K t − ∫_0^t h_K`, i.e. **`edgeMax K` is (monotone) − (continuous)**
— this gives its measurability and interval integrability for free (used in §14.3).

The two hypotheses of `StieltjesFunction`:

* **right-continuity** is Thm 2.1.3 (`tendsto_vtxP_right`, already proved in `Sofa/Vertex.lean`);
* **monotonicity** follows from a one-line geometric fact.  Writing `d = v⁺_K(b) − v⁺_K(a)`,
  `⟪v⁺_K(b), u_a⟫ ≤ h_K(a) = ⟪v⁺_K(a), u_a⟫` gives, after expanding `u_a` in the frame `(u_b, v_b)`,

      `cos(b−a) ⟪d, u_b⟫ ≤ sin(b−a) ⟪d, v_b⟫`         (`cos_mul_inner_vtxP_sub_le`)

  and `⟪d, u_b⟫ ≥ 0` always (`inner_vtxP_sub_u_nonneg`).  For `0 ≤ b − a < π/2` this yields
  `⟪d, v_b⟫ ≥ 0` and `⟪d, u_b⟫ ≤ tan(b−a) ⟪d, v_b⟫`.  Monotonicity on intervals of length `≤ 1`
  then follows from `∫_a^b h_K ≥ ∫_a^b ⟪v⁺_K(a), u_s⟫ ds = ⟪v⁺_K(a), v_a − v_b⟫`, and
  `monotone_of_locally` chains it to all of `ℝ`.

Mathlib's `measure_singleton` plus `tendsto_vtxP_left` immediately give the atom formula
**`σ_K({t}) = |e_K(t)|`** (`sigmaK_singleton`) — the defining property of the surface area measure
in Thm 2.1.1.

### 14.2 Theorem 5.2.2

With the definition above, `σ_K((a,b]) − ⟪d, v_b⟫ = ∫_a^b (h_K(s) − ⟪v⁺_K(a), u_s⟫) ds ≥ 0`, and the
integrand is `⟪v⁺_K(s) − v⁺_K(a), u_s⟫ ≤ tan(s−a) σ_K((a,s])`.  Hence the two-sided estimate

    `0 ≤ ⟪d, u_b⟫ ≤ tan δ · σ`,   `0 ≤ σ − ⟪d, v_b⟫ ≤ δ tan δ · σ`      (`δ = b − a`).

Since `t ↦ ⟪v_t, e⟫` is `‖e‖`-Lipschitz, the defect
`⟪d, e⟫ − ∫_{(a,b]} ⟪v_t, e⟫ σ_K(dt)` is at most `‖e‖(tan δ (1+δ) + δ) σ ≤ 5‖e‖δσ` for `δ ≤ 1`
(`abs_defect_le`).  The defect is additive in `(a,b]`, so chaining over a partition of mesh `δ`
keeps the bound `5‖e‖δ σ_K((a,b])`, and `δ → 0` gives

    **`⟪v⁺_K(b) − v⁺_K(a), e⟫ = ∫_{(a,b]} ⟪v_t, e⟫ σ_K(dt)`**   (`inner_vtxP_sub_eq_setIntegral`)

and, by `integral_inner`, the vector form `v⁺_K(b) − v⁺_K(a) = ∫_{(a,b]} v_t σ_K(dt)`
(`vtxP_sub_eq_setIntegral`).

**What this buys**: §5.1 of the paper (the general LS calculus: Def 5.1.1–5.1.4, Prop 5.1.1,
Lemma 5.1.2 integration by parts, Lemma 5.1.3 product rule, Prop 5.1.4) is **not needed** as a
general theory — the only two instances Chapter 6 uses are handled directly (§14.3).  Lemma 5.2.1
(`v⁺_K` has bounded variation) is also not needed separately: `arcFn` *is* the variation.

### 14.3 Chapter 6 §6.2 as formalized (`Sofa/ArmLength.lean`)

`y_K(t) = h_K(t) u_t + h_K(t+π/2) v_t` (`outerCorner`, already in `Sofa/Support.lean`),
`A⁺_K(t) = v⁺_K(t)`, `C⁺_K(t) = v⁺_K(t+π/2)` (Def 2.5.1), and

    `f⁺_K(t) = ⟪y_K(t) − A⁺_K(t), v_t⟫ = h_K(t+π/2) − ⟪v⁺_K(t), v_t⟫`,
    `g⁺_K(t) = ⟪y_K(t) − C⁺_K(t), u_t⟫ = h_K(t) + ⟪v⁺_K(t+π/2), v_{t+π/2}⟫`.

* **Prop 6.2.1** is the frame decomposition `p = ⟪p,u_t⟫u_t + ⟪p,v_t⟫v_t` plus
  `⟪y_K(t), u_t⟫ = h_K(t) = ⟪A⁺_K(t), u_t⟫`.
* **Lemma 6.2.4** `g⁺_K(t) = ∫_{(t,t+π/2]} sin(s−t) σ_K(ds)` is Thm 5.2.2 with `e = u_t`, using
  `−⟪v_s, u_t⟫ = sin(s−t)`.
* **FTC for `h_K`**: `h_K(b) − h_K(a) = ∫_a^b ⟪v⁺_K(s), v_s⟫ ds`.  Mathlib's
  `integral_eq_sub_of_hasDeriv_right_of_le` needs only the *right* derivative
  (`hasDerivWithinAt_supportFn_Ioi`, Thm 2.1.3) plus interval integrability of `edgeMax K`, which
  §14.1 supplies.  So **no absolute-continuity theory is needed**.
* **Thm 6.2.5** `d f⁺_K = g⁺_K dt − σ_K` becomes, in integrated form,

      `σ_K((a,b]) = ∫_a^b g⁺_K(s) ds − (f⁺_K(b) − f⁺_K(a))`      (`sigmaK_Ioc_toReal_eq`),

  which is pure algebra from `g⁺_K(t) = h_K(t) + edgeMax K (t+π/2)`, the FTC above (shifted by
  `π/2`) and `arcFn K t = edgeMax K t + ∫_0^t h_K`.

---

## 15. Chapter 6 §6.1–§6.2 and the weak convergence of `σ_K` (2026-09-18, Opus 5)

### 15.1 Theorem 6.2.3 (`Sofa/ArmLength.lean`)

The support function has a *right* derivative everywhere, `∂⁺h_K(t) = ⟪v⁺_K(t), v_t⟫ = edgeMax K t`
(Thm 2.1.3, already in `Sofa/Vertex.lean`).  Differentiating
`y_K(t) = h_K(t) u_t + h_K(t+π/2) v_t` with `u' = v`, `v' = −u` gives

    `∂⁺y_K(t) = (edgeMax K t − h_K(t+π/2)) u_t + (h_K(t) + edgeMax K (t+π/2)) v_t`
              `= −f⁺_K(t) u_t + g⁺_K(t) v_t`,
    `∂⁺x_K(t) = (1 − f⁺_K(t)) u_t + (g⁺_K(t) − 1) v_t`,

since `f⁺_K(t) = h_K(t+π/2) − edgeMax K t` and `g⁺_K(t) = h_K(t) + edgeMax K (t+π/2)`.  The
vector-valued derivatives of `u` and `v` are obtained from `u t = cos t • e₀ + sin t • e₁` and
`HasDerivAt.smul_const`.

**Consequence for Def 6.1.2**: condition (3) `⟪x'_K(t), u_t⟫ < 0 < ⟪x'_K(t), v_t⟫` is literally
`f⁺_K(t) > 1` and `g⁺_K(t) > 1`, and condition (2) (`x_K ∈ C¹`) is the continuity of `f⁺_K`, `g⁺_K`.
`Sofa/Injective.lean` therefore states `IsInjectiveCap` directly in those terms (plus (1), the
absolute continuity of `σ_K` on `[0,π/2)` and `(π/2,π]`), which is what Chapters 7–8 consume.
`Sofa/Injective.lean` also has Def 6.3.4 (`k₀`, `m₀`) with `m₀` monotone, `m₀ ≤ 1`, and its
piecewise-linear values (`3x/2 − 1` on `[0,1]`, `x/2` on `[1,2]`, `1` on `[2,∞)`).

### 15.2 Weak convergence `σ_{K_i} → σ_K` (`Sofa/SigmaLimit.lean`)

Needed for Lemma 6.4.2 / Thm 6.4.3 (taking the limit of the polygon inequality) and again in
Chapter 7.  With `σ_K` defined as the LS measure of `arcFn K`, weak convergence *is* pointwise
convergence of `arcFn K_i` at the continuity points of `arcFn K`, i.e. wherever
`σ_K({t}) = |e_K(t)| = 0`.  Since `arcFn K t = edgeMax K t + ∫_0^t h_K` and `h_{K_i} → h_K`
uniformly, everything reduces to

    **`|e_K(t)| = 0` ⟹ `v⁺_{K_i}(t) → v⁺_K(t)`**.

Proof (no subsequences): write `q = v⁺_K(t)`; then `e_K(t) = {q}`, so on the compact set
`S = K \ B(q, ε/2)` the functional `⟪·, u_t⟫` attains a value `≤ h_K(t) − c` with `c > 0`.  If
`d_H(K', K) < min(ε/2, c/3)` and `p ∈ e_{K'}(t)`, then `⟪p, u_t⟫ = h_{K'}(t) ≥ h_K(t) − d` and the
nearest point `q' ∈ K` to `p` has `⟪q', u_t⟫ ≥ h_K(t) − 2d > h_K(t) − c`, so `q' ∈ B(q, ε/2)` and
`dist(p, q) ≤ d + ε/2 < ε`.

Formalized: `edge_eq_singleton`, `exists_hausdorffDist_edge_subset_ball`,
`tendsto_vtxP_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist`, `tendsto_edgeMax_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist`,
`tendsto_primitive_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist`, `tendsto_arcFn_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist`,
`tendsto_sigmaK_Ioc`.

### 15.3 Risk analysis for Chapter 7: Theorem 7.1.3 (`|K| = ½∫h_K dσ_K`)

This is the one place where the *actual area* enters Chapter 8 (it converts `|K|` into the convex
curve area functional `J(u^{0,π}_K)` in Lemma 8.3.5; everything after that is Mamikon/quadratic
bookkeeping).  Baek's proof route is Thm 7.2.3 (Green's theorem for a rectifiable Jordan curve),
which needs the **Jordan curve theorem** — not in Mathlib, and out of scope here.  Options, in
decreasing order of expected feasibility:

1. **Polygons + limit.**  For a convex polygon `P` with `0 ∈ P`, `|P| = ½ Σ_i h_i ℓ_i` (triangulate
   from the origin: each edge contributes the triangle `conv{0, A_i, B_i}` of area `½ h_i ℓ_i`).
   Then pass to the limit with §15.2 (weak convergence of `σ`), uniform convergence of `h`, and the
   continuity of `|·|` under Hausdorff convergence (already available from `Sofa/BalancedMax.lean`).
   The only new ingredient is the polygon triangulation.
2. **Chaining/Riemann sums** on the sectors `Sec(a,b) = conv({0} ∪ arc_K(a,b))`, exactly like the
   proof of Thm 5.2.2 in §14.2: `|Sec(a,b)| = ½|v⁺(a) × v⁺(b)| + O(σ((a,b])·mesh)` and
   `v⁺(a) × v⁺(b) ≈ h_K(b) σ((a,b])`.  Additivity of `Sec` along a partition is the fiddly part.
3. **First variation.**  `|K + εL| = |K| + ε ∫ h_L dσ_K + O(ε²)` with `L = K` and
   `K + εK = (1+ε)K` gives `2|K| = ∫ h_K dσ_K`.  Needs the planar mixed-volume theorem, which is
   also not in Mathlib.

Recommended: **route 1**.  Note that route 1's limit step needs `σ_{K_i}(S¹)` bounded (true: it is
the perimeter, `≤ π·diam`) and the atoms of `σ_K` to be avoidable (a monotone function has at most
countably many jumps, so a.e. `t` is a continuity point).

### 15.4 The area of a triangle (`Sofa/Triangle.lean`)

The atom for route 1/2 of §15.3.  `cross p q := p₀q₁ − p₁q₀`, with `p × u_t = −⟪p, v_t⟫` and
`p × v_t = ⟪p, u_t⟫`.

* `stdTri = {(x,y) : 0 ≤ x, 0 ≤ y, x + y ≤ 1}` is described as `{lo(p₀) ≤ p₁ ≤ hi(p₀)}` with
  `lo`, `hi` *piecewise* (`1` resp. `0` outside `[0,1]`, so that the slice is empty there); then
  `Sofa/Graph.lean`'s `volume_between_Icc` gives `|stdTri| = 1/2` by a one-dimensional
  computation.
* `triLin A B : (x,y) ↦ xA + yB` has `LinearMap.det = A × B` (computed in the standard
  orthonormal basis via `LinearMap.det_toMatrix` + `Matrix.det_fin_two`).
* `tri A B := triLin A B '' stdTri` and Mathlib's `Measure.addHaar_image_linearMap` give

      **`|tri A B| = ½ |A × B|`**,   and `|triAt P Q R| = ½ |(Q−P) × (R−P)|`.

* `tri_subset_of_convex`: `0, A, B ∈ S` convex ⟹ `tri A B ⊆ S`.
* `cross_nonneg_of_mem_tri`: `tri A B` lies in the wedge `{A × q ≥ 0} ∩ {B × q ≤ 0}` (for
  `A × B ≥ 0`).

**Route 2 for Thm 7.1.3** (sectors by normal angle, chaining as in §14.2): with `0 ∈ int K`, set
`A = v⁺_K(a)`, `B = v⁺_K(b)` and `sector K A B := K ∩ wedgeC A B` where
`wedgeC A B := {q : A × q ≥ 0 ∧ B × q ≤ 0}`.  Then

1. *additivity* `|sector K A C| = |sector K A B| + |sector K B C|` — **done**
   (`volume_sector_add`, hypotheses `B ≠ 0`, `A×B ≥ 0`, `B×C ≥ 0`, `A×C > 0`).  The `⊇`
   inclusion of the wedges is the trivial dichotomy on the sign of `B × q`; the `⊆` inclusions come
   from **Cramer's rule** `(A×C)(B×q) = (B×C)(A×q) + (A×B)(C×q)` (`cross_cramer_mul`); the
   degenerate cases `A×B = 0` / `B×C = 0` give wedges inside a line, which is null
   (`volume_setOf_cross_eq_zero`, via `Measure.addHaar_submodule`); and the overlap
   `wedgeC A B ∩ wedgeC B C` is inside the line `{B × q = 0}`;
2. *lower bound* `tri A B ⊆ sector K A B` — `tri_subset_of_convex` + `cross_nonneg_of_mem_tri`;
3. *upper bound* `Sec(a,b) \ tri A B ⊆` the chord–arc cap, of area `≤ ½σ((a,b])² tan(δ/2)`
   (the cap is inside the triangle `A, Y, B` with `Y` the intersection of the two supporting
   lines); summing over a partition gives `≤ ½ tan(δ/2) · (perimeter)² → 0`;
4. `A × B = A × (B − A)` and `B − A = p u_b + q v_b` with `q ≈ σ((a,b])`, `p ≤ tan δ · q`
   (§14.1), so `A × B = −p ⟪A, v_b⟫ + q ⟪A, u_b⟫ ≈ h_K(b) σ((a,b])` — **done**
   (`Sofa/SectorArea.lean`, `cross_eq_frame` and `abs_cross_vtxP_sub_le`:
   `|A × B − σ_K((a,b]) h_K(b)| ≤ 2(b−a) σ_K((a,b]) (2R + σ_K((a,b]))` for `b − a ≤ 1`);
5. chaining as in §14.2.

So **steps 1–4 are done**; what is left is step 5, the global assembly:

* the chaining itself is the same pattern as §14.2 (`abs_defect_le` + induction on a partition of
  mesh `δ` + `δ → 0`);
* plus one extra combinatorial fact that §14.2 did not need: a *full turn* is not a wedge, so
  `|K| = Σ_j |sector K A_{t_j} A_{t_{j+1}}|` has to be established for a partition of `[a, a+2π]`
  into three or four pieces of turn `< π`, i.e. "three wedges with rays in cyclic order cover the
  plane".  For a **cap with rotation angle `π/2`** this is avoidable: Baek's Lemma 8.3.5 already
  reduces to `|K| = ½∫_{(0,π)} h_K dσ_K` because `σ_K` vanishes on `(π, 2π) \ {3π/2}` and
  `h_K(3π/2) = 0`, and `(0, π)` is a half-turn — so the wedge formulation suffices there with one
  extra split at `π/2`.

### 15.5 Step 5 of Thm 7.1.3, worked out (not yet formalized)

Fix `K` compact convex with `0 ∈ int K`, so `h_min := inf_t h_K(t) > 0`, and `‖·‖ ≤ R` on `K`.
Write `A_t := v⁺_K(t)`, `σ(a,b] := arcFn K b − arcFn K a`, `δ := b − a`, and

    `F(a,b) := |sector K A_a A_b|`,    `G(a,b) := ½ ∫_{(a,b]} h_K dσ_K`.

**(a) Nondegeneracy** — **formalized** (`vtxP_eq_of_arcFn_eq`, `cross_vtxP_nonneg`).  `A_a × A_b = A_a × (A_b − A_a) = −p ⟪A_a, v_b⟫ + q ⟪A_a, u_b⟫` with
`p = ⟪A_b − A_a, u_b⟫ ∈ [0, tan δ · q]`, `q = ⟪A_b − A_a, v_b⟫ ∈ [0, σ(a,b]]` (§14.1).  Since
`⟪A_a, u_b⟫ = h_K(b) − p ≥ h_min − p` and `|⟪A_a, v_b⟫| ≤ R`,

    `A_a × A_b ≥ q (h_min − p − R tan δ)`,

so for `δ` small (say `tan δ ≤ h_min/(4(R+1))` and `2δ σ_total ≤ h_min/4`) one gets
`A_a × A_b ≥ 0`, with strict inequality exactly when `q > 0`.  When `q = 0` then `p = 0`,
`A_a = A_b`, and **both** `F(a,b)` and `G(a,b)` vanish (the wedge is a line and
`σ(a,b] ≤ q/(1 − δ tan δ) = 0`), so the degenerate case is handled separately and additivity is
only ever invoked with `A × C > 0`.

**(b) Local estimate.**  `volume_sector_squeeze` + `abs_cross_vtxP_sub_le` + Lipschitzness of
`h_K` (constant `R`, `Sofa/SupportLipschitz.lean`) give

    `|F(a,b) − G(a,b)| ≤ ½(λ²−1)(A_a × A_b) + δ σ (2R + σ) + ½ R δ σ`,
    `λ = h_K(b)/(h_K(b) − p)`,  `λ² − 1 = p(2h_K(b) − p)/(h_K(b) − p)² ≤ 16 δ σ R / h_min²`

(using `p ≤ 2δσ ≤ h_min/2` and `h_K(b) ≤ R`), and `A_a × A_b ≤ σ R + δσ(2R+σ)`.  All together
`|F(a,b) − G(a,b)| ≤ C δ (σ + σ²)` with `C = C(R, h_min)`.

**(c) Chaining.**  `F` and `G` are both additive over `a ≤ b ≤ c` (`volume_sector_add`, resp.
`setIntegral` additivity over `Ioc`), so the defect `D(a,b) := F(a,b) − G(a,b)` is additive and
the induction of §14.2 gives `|D(a,b)| ≤ C δ (σ(a,b] + σ(a,b]²)` for every mesh `δ`; letting
`δ → 0` gives `F = G`.

**(d) Global assembly.**  A full turn is not a wedge.  For a **cap with rotation angle `π/2`**
Baek's Lemma 8.3.5 only needs the half-turn `(0, π)`, so it suffices to apply (c) on `(0, π/2]`
and `(π/2, π]` and to check `K = sector K A_0 A_{π/2} ∪ sector K A_{π/2} A_π` up to a null set —
which for a cap follows from `K ⊆ P_{π/2}` (the unit square strip) plus `h_K(3π/2) = 0`.
For a general convex body one instead splits `[a, a+2π]` into four quarters and uses that four
wedges with rays in cyclic order cover the plane.

### 15.6 Theorem 7.1.3 as formalized so far (`Sofa/AreaThm.lean`)

Standing hypotheses (`AreaSetup K hm R`): `K` compact, nonempty, convex, `0 ∈ K`,
`hm ≤ h_K(t)` for all `t` (so `0` is interior), `‖·‖ ≤ R` on `K`.  Two quantities:

    `secR K a b := |K ∩ wedgeC (v⁺_K a) (v⁺_K b)|`,    `curveG K a b := ½ ∫_{(a,b]} h_K dσ_K`.

* `curveG_add` (no side conditions) and `abs_curveG_sub_le`
  (`|2 curveG − σ h_K(b)| ≤ R δ σ`, from the `R`-Lipschitzness of `h_K`);
* `secR_add` (real form of `volume_sector_add`) and `secR_add'`, which also covers the degenerate
  case `σ_K((x,z]) = 0` — there all three vertices coincide and all three sectors are null;
* `local_geom`: the seven facts about `v⁺_K(a)`, `v⁺_K(b)` on a short interval
  (`0 ≤ p ≤ 2δσ`, `σ/2 ≤ q ≤ σ`, `|⟪v⁺(a), v_b⟫| ≤ R`, and the frame formula for `A × B`);
* `cross_pos_arith` / `cross_vtxP_pos_of`: with the single smallness hypothesis
  `16 δ (R + Sg + 1) ≤ hm` (and `δ ≤ 1/2`, `σ ≤ Sg`) one gets `p ≤ hm/8`, `h_K(b) − p ≥ hm/2` and
  `A × B ≥ σ hm/8 > 0`;
* `area_local_arith` + **`abs_secR_sub_curveG_le`**: `|secR − curveG| ≤ C δ σ` whenever
  `71 M³ ≤ hm² (2C)` and `1 ≤ M`, `R ≤ M`, `Sg ≤ M`.  (The three error terms are
  `hm²(2 secR − A×B) ≤ 64 δσM³`, `hm²|A×B − σh_K(b)| ≤ 6 δσM³`, `hm²|σh_K(b) − 2curveG| ≤ δσM³`.)
* **`secR_eq_curveG`**: chaining over a partition of mesh `δ` and letting `δ → 0` gives

      **`secR K a b = curveG K a b`** for `b − a ≤ 1/2` with `16(b−a)(R + Sg + 1) ≤ hm`.

**Step 5(d) (the global assembly) is done** — see §15.7.

---

## 16. Theorem 7.1.3: the global assembly (2026-09-18, Opus 5, `Sofa/WedgeCover.lean`)

### 16.1 The obstruction, and the way round it

`|K| = ½∫_{S¹} h_K dσ_K` needs `|K| = Σ_j secR K t_j t_{j+1}` for a partition of a *full turn*.
Iterated binary additivity cannot give this: a full turn is not a wedge, and `secR_add` needs
`A × C > 0`; indeed `secR K t₀ (t₀+2π) = 0`, because `wedgeC A A` is a line.

The plan recorded in §15.6 was to argue with the cyclic sign sequence `s_j = sign(A_j × q)` and the
unimodality of `t ↦ ⟪v⁺_K(t), u_ψ⟫` (proved in `Sofa/VertexOrder.lean`).  That works, but the
cyclic combinatorics is unpleasant.  The route actually taken is better, and removes the need for
`VertexOrder`'s sign windows entirely:

> **Lift the argument explicitly.**  Put
>
>     `vtxAngle K t := t + arcsin ( ⟪v⁺_K(t), v_t⟫ / ‖v⁺_K(t)‖ )`.
>
> Because `⟪v⁺_K(t), u_t⟫ = h_K(t) ≥ hm > 0`, the angle `φ(t) := vtxAngle K t − t` satisfies
> `cos φ(t) = h_K(t)/‖v⁺_K(t)‖ > 0`, hence `φ(t) ∈ (−π/2, π/2)` and
>
>     `v⁺_K(t) = ‖v⁺_K(t)‖ • u (vtxAngle K t)`   (`vtxP_eq_polar`).
>
> Since `v⁺_K` and `v` are `2π`-periodic, `vtxAngle K (t + 2π) = vtxAngle K t + 2π`
> **on the nose** (`vtxAngle_add_two_pi`).

So the winding number is `1` by construction — no covering-space theory, no lifting lemma, no
`Real.Angle`.  The wedges become honest angular intervals and the whole argument is interval
arithmetic.

### 16.2 The angular increment lies in `[0, π)`  (`vtxAngle_step`)

Write `Δ = vtxAngle K b − vtxAngle K a = (b − a) + φ(b) − φ(a)`, so `Δ ∈ (b − a − π, b − a + π)`.

* *Upper bound.*  `cos φ(t) = h_K(t)/‖v⁺_K(t)‖ ≥ hm/R`, and `cos φ = sin(φ + π/2) < φ + π/2` by
  `Real.sin_lt`, so `φ(t) > hm/R − π/2` (`vtxAngle_sub_gt`); also `φ(t) < π/2`
  (`vtxAngle_sub_lt`, because `cos φ(t) > 0`).  Hence `Δ < (b − a) + π − hm/R`, and the standing
  smallness hypothesis `16(b−a)(R + Sg + 1) ≤ hm` gives `(b − a) < hm/R`, so `Δ < π`.
* *Lower bound.*  `A × B = ‖A‖‖B‖ sin Δ ≥ 0` by `cross_vtxP_nonneg` (repackaged as
  `cross_vtxP_nonneg_of` with the same smallness hypothesis), so `sin Δ ≥ 0`; with `Δ ∈ (−π, π)`
  this forces `Δ ≥ 0`.

`PartData K T N` bundles what the tiling argument needs: `0 < N`, the increments of
`Θ_j := vtxAngle K (T j)` are in `[0, π)` for `j < N`, and `Θ_N = Θ_0 + 2π`.

### 16.3 Wedge membership is an angular interval  (`sin_sandwich`, `angle_mem_of_mem_wedgeC`)

`wedgeC A B = {q : 0 ≤ A × q ∧ B × q ≤ 0}`, and for `q = ‖q‖ u_θ` one has
`A_m × q = ‖A_m‖‖q‖ sin(θ − Θ_m)`.  So membership in the `m`-th wedge says
`sin(θ − Θ_m) ≥ 0 ≥ sin(θ − Θ_{m+1})`.  The elementary lemma

> `sin_sandwich`: `0 < Δ`, `x < 2π`, `x − Δ > −2π`, `sin x ≥ 0`, `sin(x − Δ) ≤ 0` ⟹ `0 ≤ x ≤ Δ`

(four cases: `x > π`, `−π < x < 0`, `x ≤ −π`, and the conclusion; each is one application of
`sin_pos_of_pos_of_lt_pi`) converts this into `Θ_m ≤ θ ≤ Θ_{m+1}`, once `θ` is normalised to
`[Θ_0, Θ_0 + 2π)` (`exists_polar_Ico`, via `exists_angle` built from `arccos`).  The two strictness
requirements are supplied by assuming `q` is **off every partition ray**, i.e.
`A_k × q ≠ 0` for all `k ≤ N`; the union of the `N+1` rays is null (`volume_rays_eq_zero`).

### 16.4 Tiling

* *Covering* (`exists_mem_wedgeC`): for `q ≠ 0` take `θ ∈ [Θ_0, Θ_0+2π)` and let `j` be least with
  `θ < Θ_{j+1}` (`Nat.find`; `j ≤ N−1` because `Θ_N = Θ_0 + 2π > θ`).  Then `Θ_j ≤ θ < Θ_{j+1}` and
  `Δ_j < π` give the two sign conditions.  `q = 0` lies in every wedge.  Hence
  `K = ⋃_{j<N} sector K A_j A_{j+1}` (`eq_iUnion_sector`).
* *A.e. disjointness* (`sector_aeDisjoint`): for `i < j < N` and `q` off every ray, §16.3 gives
  `θ ≤ Θ_{i+1} ≤ Θ_j ≤ θ`, so `θ = Θ_{i+1}` and `A_{i+1} × q = 0` — contradiction.
* Therefore `|K| = Σ_{j<N} |sector_j|` (`measure_biUnion_finset₀`), i.e.
  `volume.real K = Σ_{j<N} secR K (T j) (T (j+1))` (`volumeReal_eq_sum_secR`).

### 16.5 The theorem

Combining with `secR_eq_curveG` on each piece and telescoping `curveG_add`:

> `volumeReal_eq_curveG_of_part`: `|K| = curveG K (T 0) (T N)` for any partition satisfying
> `PartData` and the per-step smallness hypotheses.
>
> **`volumeReal_eq_curveG`** `(h : AreaSetup K hm R) (t₀ : ℝ) : |K| = ½ ∫_{(t₀, t₀+2π]} h_K dσ_K`,
> with the uniform partition `T j = t₀ + j·(2π/N)` and
> `N := ⌈4π⌉₊ + ⌈32π(R + Sg + 1)/hm⌉₊ + 1`, `Sg := arcFn K (t₀+2π) − arcFn K t₀`.

This is **Theorem 7.1.3** for a convex body with the origin in its interior.

### 16.6 Removing the position assumption (`Sofa/Translate.lean`)

For a **cap** the origin is a *boundary* point (`h_K(3π/2) = 0`), so `AreaSetup.hm_le` fails.  One
translation removes the restriction, because both sides of Thm 7.1.3 are translation-covariant.
Write `tr c K = K + c` (`Sofa/StdPos.lean`).

* `edge_tr`, `edgeMax_tr` (`m⁺_{K+c}(t) = m⁺_K(t) + ⟪c, v_t⟫`) and
  **`vtxP_tr`** (`v⁺_{K+c}(t) = v⁺_K(t) + c`, from `decomp_u_v`).
* **`arcFn_tr`**: `arcFn (K+c) t = arcFn K t + c₁`.  The cancellation is exact and needs no
  trigonometry: `∫_0^t ⟪c, u_s⟫ ds = ⟪c, v_0⟫ − ⟪c, v_t⟫` (already in `Sofa/SurfaceArea.lean` as
  `integral_inner_u`), and the `−⟪c, v_t⟫` kills the `+⟪c, v_t⟫` coming from the vertex, leaving
  the constant `⟪c, v_0⟫ = c₁`.
* **`sigmaK_tr`**: `σ_{K+c} = σ_K`, by `Measure.ext_of_Ioc` and `sigmaK_Ioc`.
* **`setIntegral_inner_u_sigmaK`**: `∫_{(a,b]} ⟪c, u_r⟫ dσ_K(r) = ⟪v⁺_K(b) − v⁺_K(a), R_{π/2} c⟫`.
  No vector-valued integral is needed: `⟪c, u_r⟫ = ⟪v_r, R_{π/2} c⟫` pointwise
  (`R_{π/2} c = (−c₁, c₀)`), so this is the *scalar* Thm 5.2.2 `inner_vtxP_sub_eq_setIntegral`
  applied to `e = R_{π/2} c`.  Over a full turn it vanishes by `vtxP_add_two_pi`.
* Hence **`curveG_tr`**: `curveG (K+c) t₀ (t₀+2π) = curveG K t₀ (t₀+2π)`.

Finally `exists_areaSetup_of_mem_interior`: for `c ∈ interior K` pick `r > 0` with
`ball c r ⊆ K`; then `(r/2)·u_t ∈ K − c` gives `h_{K−c}(t) ≥ r/2`, and compactness gives `R`.
Together with `volume_tr`:

> **`volumeReal_eq_curveG_of_interior_nonempty`**
> `(hK : IsCompact K) (hconv : Convex ℝ K) (hint : (interior K).Nonempty) (t₀ : ℝ) :`
> `|K| = ½ ∫_{(t₀, t₀+2π]} h_K dσ_K`.

This is **Theorem 7.1.3 in full**.

---

## 17. Chapter 7 §7.1 as formalized (2026-09-18, Opus 5, `Sofa/Quadratic.lean`, `Sofa/Minkowski.lean`)

### 17.1 `ConvexDomain` instead of Baek's Def 7.1.1

Baek's Def 7.1.1 defines a convex domain as a space with a barycentric operation `c_λ` **together
with an embedding** into a convex subset of a vector space.  Only three consequences are ever
used, so `Sofa/Quadratic.lean` asks exactly for them:

```
structure ConvexDomain (V) where
  carrier : Set V
  bary    : ℝ → V → V → V
  mem     : x ∈ carrier → y ∈ carrier → 0 ≤ l → l ≤ 1 → bary l x y ∈ carrier
  bary_zero, bary_one
```

This is *weaker* than Baek's definition (no embedding is demanded), so the theorems proved from it
are stronger; and it applies verbatim both to a convex subset of a vector space
(`ConvexDomain.ofConvex`) and to a family of planar convex bodies under Minkowski combination,
which is **not** a vector space (`Set ℝ²` has no additive inverses).

`ConvexLinearOn` (Def 7.1.2), `ConvexBilinearOn` (Def 7.1.3), `IsQuadraticOn` (Def 7.1.4),
`ConcaveOnDomain` (Def 7.1.6) and `EqUpToLinear` (Def 7.1.7) follow the paper.

### 17.2 The directional derivative and Theorem 7.1.5

`dirDeriv D f K K' := derivWithin (fun l => f (D.bary l K K')) (Ici 0) 0` — a **right** derivative,
because `c_λ` is only defined for `λ ∈ [0,1]`; Baek writes `d/dλ|_{λ=0}` without comment.

* `quad_along`: `f(c_λ(K,K')) = f K + L λ + Q λ²` with `L = g(K,K') + g(K',K) − 2g(K,K)` and
  `Q = g(K,K) + g(K',K') − (g(K,K') + g(K',K))` — Baek's Equation (7.1), normalized.
* `dirDeriv_eq` (**Lemma 7.1.4**): `Df(K;K') = L`.  The agreement of `f ∘ c_·` with the polynomial
  only on `[0,1]` is handled by `HasDerivWithinAt.congr_of_eventuallyEq` on `𝓝[Ici 0] 0`.
* **`isMaxOn_iff_dirDeriv_nonpos` (Theorem 7.1.5)**.  `⇒` is `linear_coeff_nonpos`: if
  `Lλ + Qλ² ≤ 0` for all `λ ∈ (0,1]` then `L ≤ 0` (take `λ = min(1, L/(2(|Q|+1)))`).  `⇐` uses
  concavity at `λ = 1/2` to get `Q ≤ 0`, and then `f K' − f K = L + Q ≤ 0`.

### 17.3 Planar convex bodies (Thm 7.1.1–7.1.3, `Sofa/Minkowski.lean`)

`mix l A B := (1−l) • A + l • B` (pointwise/Minkowski).  The chain is entirely mechanical once
the support function is handled:

* **`supportFn_mix`** (Thm 7.1.1 / 7.1.2 (1)): `h_{c_λ} = (1−λ)h_A + λh_B`, by `le_antisymm` from
  `supportFn_le_iff` and `exists_supportFn_eq`.
* **`vtxP_mix`** (Thm 7.1.2 (2)): `v_K(a,b)` is *literally linear* in `(h_K(a), h_K(b))`
  (Def 2.1.14), so `vtx2_mix` is algebra; then `v⁺_K(t) = lim_{s→t⁺} v_K(t,s)` (Thm 2.1.3) and
  uniqueness of limits give `vtxP_mix`.  `edgeMax_mix` follows from `edgeMax = ⟪v⁺, v_t⟫`.
* **`arcFn_mix`**, **`sigmaK_mix`** (Thm 7.1.2 (3)): `arcFn` is convex-linear because
  `arcFn K t = ⟪v⁺_K(t), v_t⟫ + ∫_0^t h_K`; then `σ_{c_λ} = ofReal(1−λ)·σ_A + ofReal(λ)·σ_B` by
  `Measure.ext_of_Ioc`.
* **`areaB t₀ A B := ½ ∫_{(t₀,t₀+2π]} h_A dσ_B`** is convex-bilinear (`areaB_mix_left` uses
  `supportFn_mix` + `integral_add`; `areaB_mix_right` uses `sigmaK_mix` +
  `integral_add_measure`/`integral_smul_measure`), and `areaB t₀ K K = curveG K t₀ (t₀+2π)` by
  definition.  With **Theorem 7.1.3** (`Sofa/Translate.lean`) this gives
  **`isQuadraticOn_volumeReal`**: on any family of compact convex bodies with nonempty interior
  closed under `mix`, `K ↦ |K|` is a quadratic functional.

Nothing here needs Schneider's Thm 1.7.5 or Rem 1.7.7; the embedding `K ↦ h_K` is used only
through the four `*_mix` identities above.

---

## 18. Chapter 6 §6.5 as formalized (2026-09-18, Opus 5, `Sofa/ArmBound.lean`)

§6.5 is **self-contained real analysis** once Theorem 6.5.1 is granted, so it is formalized
*conditionally*: `ArmPair f G` packages exactly what the section uses, and everything else is
unconditional.

### 18.1 The operator and the iterates

`armF f x = 1 + ∫_0^x m₀(f(π/2 − u)) du` (Def 6.5.1), `armIter 0 = 0`,
`armIter (n+1) = max (armIter n) (armF (armIter n))` (Def 6.5.2), `jFn c x = max (1−x) c`
(Def 6.5.3).  Continuity of `armIter n` needs continuity of `x ↦ ∫_a^x G` for continuous `G`,
obtained from FTC-2 (`intervalIntegral.integral_hasDerivAt_right`) rather than looking for a
primitive-continuity lemma.

`one_sub_le_armF`: `armF f ≥ 1 − x` because `m₀ ≥ −1` on `[0,∞)` — this is Baek's one-line remark
and it disposes of the `1 − x` branch of `j_c` in both cases of Lemma 6.5.3.

### 18.2 Lemma 6.5.3, with the two quadratic inequalities made explicit

`jFn c (π/2 − u) = max (u + 1 − π/2) c`, which lies in `[0,1]` for `u ∈ [0,x] ⊆ [0,π/2]` and
`c ∈ [0,2/3]`, so `m₀` there is the affine branch `3y/2 − 1`.  The `max` integral splits at
`a = π/2 − 1 + c`:

* `x ≤ a`: `∫ = c x`, and the goal `1 − (1 − 3c/2)x ≥ c + 1/12` follows from `x ≤ a` and the
  completed square

      `1 − (1−3c/2)(π/2−1+c) − c − 1/12 = (3/2)(c − (7/6 − π/4))² + (−1/8 + 3π/8 − 3π²/32)`,

  whose constant is positive iff `π < 2 + √(8/3) ≈ 3.633`.
* `x > a`: `∫ = c a + ((x+1−π/2)² − c²)/2`, and

      `armF(j_c)(x) − c − 1/12 = (3/4)(x − (π/2 − 1/3))² + (3/4)(c − (5/3 − π/2))²
                                 + (−1/2 + 3π/4 − 3π²/16)`,

  whose constant is positive iff `π < 2 + √(4/3) ≈ 3.1547` — **a margin of only 0.013**, so
  `Real.pi_lt_d2` (`π < 3.15`) is exactly strong enough and `pi_gt_three` is not.

Both are discharged by `nlinarith` with the two squares and `Real.pi_gt_d2`/`Real.pi_lt_d2`
as hints.

### 18.3 The iteration and Theorem 6.5.6

`armIter 1 = jFn 0` by direct computation (`armF 0 = 1 − x`), and `iter_ge` proves
`jFn (k/12) ≤ armIter (k+1)` on `[0,π/2]` for `k ≤ 9` by induction (Lemma 6.5.3 needs
`k/12 ≤ 2/3`, i.e. `k ≤ 8`, at the inductive step).  Hence `armIter 10 ≥ 3/4 > 2/3`, so
`m₀(armIter 10 (π/2−u)) ≥ m₀(3/4) = 1/8` and `armIter 11 x ≥ 1 + x/8 > 1` on `(0, π/2]`.

> **Typo in the paper.**  Lemma 6.5.5 is stated as "`f₁₁(x) > 1` on `x ∈ (0,1]`", but its proof
> gives — and Theorem 6.5.6 uses — `x ∈ (0, π/2]`.  Formalized as `(0, π/2]`.

`ArmPair f G` (with `f = f_K`, `G = t ↦ g_K(π/2 − t)`) is **symmetric** in `f` and `G`; that is
precisely Baek's "apply the inductive hypothesis to the mirror image `K^m`".  `Lemma 6.5.2`
(`armIter_le_of_armPair`) and `Theorem 6.5.6` (`one_lt_of_armPair`) then follow by a two-line
induction.

**What remains for Theorem 6.1.1**: Theorem 6.5.1 itself, i.e. §6.3 (Thm 6.3.3) and §6.4
(Thm 6.4.3, Cor 6.4.4, Prop 6.4.5–6.4.6).

---

## 19. Theorem 6.3.3: analysis before formalizing (2026-09-18, Opus 5)

Theorem 6.3.3 (`σ_K(t) ≤ k₀(g⁺_K(t))·δ + O(δ²)` for a maximum polygon cap with `n` steps) is the
last unformalized ingredient of Theorem 6.1.1, and Baek calls it "the most important part of the
analysis".  This section records the computations worked out before implementation, and one
**edge case that needs care**.

### 19.1 Notation in the graph representation

With `ω = π/2`, `Θ = Θ_n = {jδ : 1 ≤ j ≤ n−1}`, `δ = (π/2)/n` and `h = h_K`:

* inner walls `b_s(x) = lineFn s (h s − 1) x`, `d_s(x) = lineFn (s+π/2) (h(s+π/2) − 1) x`;
* niche roof `R(x) = max_{s∈Θ} min(b_s(x), d_s(x))` (`roofR`), fan floor `f ≡ h(π/2) − 1`
  (constant, because `fanFin (π/2) = {π/2}`);
* `X = actR(t) ∩ posR`, and `τ(t) = |X| / sin t` — the two `actF` correction terms in `tauH`
  vanish for `t ∈ Θ` because `actF(t)` is a single point (`b_t` has nonzero slope).

`X ⊆ ⃗b_K(t)`: on `X` the niche roof equals `b_t`, hence `b_t ≤ d_t` there, and parametrizing
`b_t` by `p = x_K(t) + s(−v_t)` gives `⟪p, v_t⟫ = h(t+π/2) − 1 − s`, so `b_t ≤ d_t ⟺ s ≥ 0`.

### 19.2 The two exact lengths

Write `x_K(t) = (h(t)−1)u_t + (h(t+π/2)−1)v_t` (the intersection of `b_t` and `d_t`).  With
`p = x_K(t) − s v_t` and `⟪v_t, u_θ⟫ = sin(θ−t)`:

    `H¹(⃗b_K(t) ∩ H^d_K(t+δ)) = max(0, [−sin δ·(h(t)−1) + cos δ·(h(t+π/2)−1)
                                        − h(t+π/2+δ) + 1] / cos δ)`

and symmetrically for `t−δ`.  Baek's Lemma 6.3.2 rewrites these as
`tan δ · max(0, 1 − g⁺_K(t) + tan(δ/2))` and `tan δ · max(0, g⁻_K(t) − 1 + tan(δ/2))`, using
`g^±_K(t) = h(t) − ⟪C^±_K(t), u_t⟫` and the fact that the *adjacent* polygon angles let one
identify `C^+_K(t)` with `c_K(t) ∩ c_K(t+δ) = a_K(t+π/2) ∩ a_K(t+π/2+δ)`.

### 19.3 The `S`-side: `β = 2 tan(δ/2) − σ_K(t)`

On the line `a_t`, with foot `P = h(t)u_t` and `p = P + s v_t`, the intersections with
`a_{t±δ}` are at

    `s_+ = (h(t+δ) − h(t) cos δ)/sin δ`,   `s_- = −(h(t−δ) − h(t) cos δ)/sin δ`,

so the polygon edge length is the classical

    `σ_K(t) = s_+ − s_- = (h(t+δ) + h(t−δ) − 2h(t) cos δ) / sin δ`.

Replacing `h` by `h − 1` (which is what passing from `a` to `b` does) subtracts
`2(1−cos δ)/sin δ = 2 tan(δ/2)`:

    `s'_+ − s'_- = σ_K(t) − 2 tan(δ/2)`,

and `b_t ∩ S = {p = P' + s v_t : s'_+ ≤ s ≤ s'_-}` (above `b_{t±δ}` means `s ≥ s'_+` resp.
`s ≤ s'_-`).  Hence `H¹(b_K(t) ∩ S) = max(0, 2 tan(δ/2) − σ_K(t))`, Baek's Equation (6.2).

### 19.4 An edge case that needs care

> **Correction (2026-09-24).** There is no gap here; the analysis below is wrong in its
> conclusion. The proof only needs `H₀ ∩ Q⁻_K(u) ⊆ N_Θ(K)` for `u ∈ {t−δ, t, t+δ}`, where
> `H₀ = {y ≥ 0}`. At `t = δ` the quadrant `Q⁻_K(0)` lies in `y < 0` (because `h_K(π/2) = 1`), so
> this holds trivially for `u = 0`; likewise for `Q⁻_K(π/2)` at `t = (n−1)δ`. The line `l_K(π)`
> (item 1, `t = (n−1)δ`) and the line `l_K(0)` (used for `β = 2 tan(δ/2) − σ_K(t)` at `t = δ`,
> §19.3) contain no edge of the polygon, but they pass
> through the vertices `C⁺_K(t)` and `A⁻_K(δ)` that the proof needs, because `π` and `0` lie in the
> normal cones of these vertices. So Baek's Theorem 6.3.3 holds at both extreme angles as printed.
> The formalization still uses the different bound of §28, which is valid in any case.

Two steps of Baek's proof use that `t − δ` and `t + δ` are themselves angles of the polygon:

1. Lemma 6.3.2 identifies `r = c_K(t±δ) ∩ c_K(t)` with `C^±_K(t) = v^±_K(t+π/2)`, which requires
   `t + π/2` and `t + π/2 ± δ` to be **adjacent** normals of `polyCap`.  The normal set is
   `Θ ∪ (Θ+π/2) ∪ {π/2, 3π/2}`, whose largest element below `3π/2` is `π − δ`; so for
   `t = (n−1)δ` the angle `t + π/2 + δ = π` is **not** a normal of the polygon and the
   identification fails.
2. Theorem 6.3.3 uses `⋃_{u ∈ {t−δ,t,t+δ}} Q⁻_K(u) ⊆ N_Θ(K)`, which needs `t ± δ ∈ Θ`; for
   `t = δ` one has `t − δ = 0 ∉ Θ` and for `t = (n−1)δ` one has `t + δ = π/2 ∉ Θ`.

So the argument as printed is valid for `2δ ≤ t ≤ π/2 − 2δ`.  This is harmless for
Theorem 6.4.3 on intervals `(a,b)` with `a > 0` (for `δ` small every angle of `(a,b) ∩ Θ` is
interior), but Baek also needs `I = [0,b)`, where `t = δ` is always involved.  **Plan**:
formalize Theorem 6.3.3 for interior `t` first, then handle `σ_K({0})` and the two extreme
angles separately — for a polygon cap `σ_K(0) = 0` (no normal at `0`), and the two extreme
angles contribute at most `O(δ)` each to `σ_{K_n}([0,b))`, which vanishes in the limit.

### 19.5 What has to be built first

* the dictionary between `sigmaH`/`tauH` (the `x`-projections used in Chapter 3) and the
  plane-geometric edge lengths — in particular the formula of §19.3 for `sigmaH`;
* `innerCorner`/`outerCorner` in terms of `h` (already in `Sofa/Support.lean`);
* the identification of `C^±_K(t)` for a polygon cap with the intersection of adjacent
  supporting lines.

---

## 20. The geometric core of Theorem 6.3.3 as formalized (2026-09-18, Opus 5, `Sofa/NicheBound.lean`)

### 20.1 Line comparison as interval arithmetic

For `sin s₁, sin s₂ > 0`,

    `lineFn s₁ c₁ x ≤ lineFn s₂ c₂ x  ⟺  x · sin(s₁ − s₂) ≤ sin s₁ · c₂ − sin s₂ · c₁`,

so every comparison set is a half-line (`setOf_lineFn_le_pos`/`neg`) and every equality set is a
single point (`volume_setOf_lineFn_eq`).  With `wall h s x := lineFn s (h s − 1) x` the niche roof
is `roofR = sup'_{s∈Θ} min (wall h s) (wall h (s+π/2))`.

### 20.2 The covering — Baek's topological step becomes one line

For `x` with `roofR x = wall h t x`, either

* `wall h (t+π/2) x < wall h t x` — then the `sup'` is attained at some `s ≠ t` and
  `wall h s x = wall h t x` or `wall h (s+π/2) x = wall h t x`, i.e. `x ∈ crossSet`, a **finite**
  union of points (null); or
* `wall h t x ≤ wall h (t+π/2) x` and then, by trichotomy against `d_{t∓δ}`, `x` lies in
  `pieceL`, `pieceR`, or — when `wall h t x < wall h (t±δ+π/2) x` — in `pieceS`, because
  `min (wall h (t±δ)) (wall h (t±δ+π/2)) x ≤ roofR x = wall h t x < wall h (t±δ+π/2) x`
  **forces** `wall h (t±δ) x ≤ wall h t x`.

This last implication is Baek's `X \ R ⊆ S`; in the plane he needs `∂N_Θ(K)`, the quadrants
`Q⁻_K(u)` and the openness of the niche, and in the graph representation it is immediate from
`Finset.le_sup'`.  (This is also why the argument needs `t ± δ ∈ Θ` — see §19.4.)

### 20.3 The three interval lengths

With `cornerX h t = cos t·(h t −1) − sin t·(h(t+π/2)−1)` (the `x`-coordinate of the inner corner
`x_K(t)`):

    `pieceL h t δ = Icc (cornerX h t) (cornerX h t + sin t · lenL h t δ)`
    `pieceR h t δ = Icc (cornerX h t) (cornerX h t + sin t · lenR h t δ)`
    `pieceS h t δ = Icc c (c + sin t · lenS h t δ)`

where

    `lenL = ( sin δ·(h t −1) + cos δ·(h(t+π/2)−1) − (h(t−δ+π/2)−1) ) / cos δ`
    `lenR = (−sin δ·(h t −1) + cos δ·(h(t+π/2)−1) − (h(t+δ+π/2)−1) ) / cos δ`
    `lenS = ( 2(1−cos δ) − (h(t+δ) + h(t−δ) − 2 h(t) cos δ) ) / sin δ`.

`lenS` is Baek's `2 tan(δ/2) − σ_K(t)` written out, and `lenL`, `lenR` are his
`tan δ·(g⁻_K(t) − 1 + tan(δ/2))` and `tan δ·(1 − g⁺_K(t) + tan(δ/2))` — the identification of the
`g`'s with the support values is the remaining step.

**`volumeReal_actR_le`**: `|{x : roofR x = wall h t x}| ≤ sin t·(lenL⁺ + lenR⁺ + lenS⁺)`.

Note the *sum* replaces Baek's `max` over `u ∈ {t−δ, t+δ}`: harmless, because `g⁻ ≤ g⁺` makes
`(g⁻−1)⁺ + (1−g⁺)⁺ ≤ |1 − g⁺|` exactly as his `M ≤ |1 − g⁺_K(t)|` does.

### 20.4 What is left

1. `tauH ω Θ _ h t = |{x : roofR x = wall h t x} ∩ posR| / sin t` for `ω = π/2`, `t ∈ Θ`
   (the two `actF` corrections vanish because `floorF` is constant and `wall h t` is not);
2. `lenL`, `lenR` in terms of `g^∓_K(t)`, and `lenS` in terms of `σ_K(t)`, for a polygon cap;
3. the arithmetic of Baek's two cases (`s ≤ δ` and `s > δ`) with explicit `O(δ²)` constants.

---

## 21. Theorem 6.3.3 (interior angles) as formalized (2026-09-18, Opus 5)

`sigmaH_le_k0_armGp`: for a maximum polygon cap `K` with rotation angle `π/2`, angle set `Θ`, and
`t, t ± δ ∈ Θ` with `0 < t − δ`, `t + δ < π/2`,

    `σ_K(t) ≤ k₀(g⁺_K(t))·tan δ + ( 2 tan δ · tan(δ/2) + (tan(δ/2) − (tan δ)/2)⁺ )`,

with `tan δ = sin δ/cos δ` and `tan(δ/2) = (1−cos δ)/sin δ` written out.  The bracket is the
`O(δ²)` of the paper, made **exact**.

### 21.1 The three inputs

* **`volumeReal_subset_actR_le`** (§20): `|X| ≤ sin t·(lenL⁺ + lenR⁺ + lenS⁺)`, with
  `X = actR(t) ∩ posR` via `tauH_eq_pi_div_two` (the two `actF` corrections vanish because
  `floorF (π/2)` is a constant function and `wall h t` is not).
* **`sigmaH_le_lenU`**: `σ_K(t) ≤ (lenU)⁺` where `lenU = (h(t+δ)+h(t−δ)−2h(t)cos δ)/sin δ`.
  This is the classical polygon edge-length formula, but only as the **inequality** that needs no
  adjacency argument: `actU(t)` lies in the interval cut out by the two neighbouring supporting
  lines (`Finset.inf'_le` twice).  Note `lenS = 2 tan(δ/2) − lenU` exactly.
* **`lenL_le` / `lenR_le`**: `lenL ≤ tan δ·(g⁻_K(t) − 1 + tan(δ/2))` and
  `lenR ≤ tan δ·(1 − g⁺_K(t) + tan(δ/2))`.  Baek's Lemma 6.3.2 gets these as *equalities* by
  identifying `C^±_K(t)` with the intersection of adjacent supporting lines — which needs the
  polygon's adjacency structure (and fails at the extreme angles, §19.4).  Here only the easy
  direction is used: `v^+_K(s) ∈ K ⊆ H_K(s+δ)` gives `m^+_K(s) ≤ (h(s+δ) − h(s)cos δ)/sin δ`, and
  `v^-_K(s) ∈ K ⊆ H_K(s−δ)` gives the reverse bound for `m^-_K(s)` — **valid for every compact
  convex body**, and pointing in exactly the right direction.

### 21.2 The arithmetic (`thm633_arith`)

Purely real; it needs neither `0 ≤ σ` nor any geometry:

* `lenU < 0` ⇒ `σ ≤ 0` ⇒ trivial (the cap side is empty);
* otherwise `σ ≤ lenU` gives `lenS⁺ ≤ (2 tan(δ/2) − σ)⁺`, and
  `lenL⁺ + lenR⁺ ≤ tan δ·((g⁻−1)⁺ + (1−g⁺)⁺ + 2 tan(δ/2)) ≤ tan δ·(|1−g⁺| + 2 tan(δ/2))`
  because `g⁻ ≤ g⁺` (`armGm_le_armGp`);
* the two cases `σ ≤ 2 tan(δ/2)` and `σ > 2 tan(δ/2)` use
  `k₀(x) ≥ (|x−1|+1)/2` and `k₀(x) ≥ |x−1|` respectively.

### 21.3 Still to do for Theorem 6.4.3

1. the two **extreme angles** `t = δ` and `t = (n−1)δ`, and `σ_K({0})` (§19.4);
2. `tan δ ≤ δ + δ³`, `tan(δ/2) ≤ (tan δ)/2 + δ³/8` to convert the exact error term into
   `C·δ²` with an explicit `C` (needs a bound on `g⁺`, e.g. Lemma 6.3.1's diameter ≤ 5);
3. the limit `δ → 0` (Thm 6.4.3), using the weak convergence of `σ_K` (`Sofa/SigmaLimit.lean`)
   and Lemma 6.4.1/6.4.2.

---

## 22. Theorem 6.3.3 in measure form (2026-09-18, Opus 5)

Baek states Theorem 6.3.3 as a bound on the *side length* `σ_K(t)`, which in our development is
the atom `σ_K({t}) = |e_K(t)| = edgeLength K t` (Thm 2.1.1, `sigmaK_singleton`), while the
balancedness theorem of Chapter 3 is stated for `sigmaH` (an `x`-projection).  The two are linked
by:

* **`ofReal_edgeLength_mul_sin_le`**: the geometric edge `e_K(t) = [v⁻_K(t), v⁺_K(t)]` projects
  onto `Ioo (v⁺_K(t))₀ (v⁻_K(t))₀`, of length `|e_K(t)|·sin t`; every point of it lies in
  `actU(t)` (because `roofU ≤ ℓ_t` always, and `≥` on the edge), and in `posU` except where
  `floorF` meets `ℓ_t` — a single point, since `floorF (π/2)` is the *constant* `h(π/2) − 1`.
* hence **`edgeLength_le_sigmaH`**, and with §21:

      **`edgeLength_le_k0_delta`**:  `|e_K(t)| ≤ k₀(g⁺_K(t))·δ + (k₀(g⁺_K(t)) + 5)·δ²`
      **`sigmaK_singleton_le_k0_delta`**: the same as `σ_K({t}) ≤ ofReal(…)`

for every interior angle `t` of a maximum polygon cap with rotation angle `π/2` and step
`0 < δ ≤ 1`.  This is exactly the input Theorem 6.4.3 needs.

**Remaining for Theorem 6.4.3**: the two extreme angles and `σ_K({0})` (§19.4); a uniform bound
on `g⁺_{K_n}` (available from Hausdorff convergence — the `K_n` lie in a fixed bounded set — so
Lemma 6.3.1's diameter ≤ 5 is *not* needed); Lemma 6.4.1 (`g^±` between adjacent angles);
Lemma 6.4.2 (`∫|g⁺_{K_n} − g⁺_K| → 0`, from the weak convergence in `Sofa/SigmaLimit.lean` and
`armGp_eq_setIntegral`); and the `lim inf` step.

---

## 23. Plan for §6.4 (2026-09-18, Opus 5) — what is actually missing

Theorem 6.4.3 takes the limit of Theorem 6.3.3.  Two steps need facts about *polygons* that our
Chapter 3 route (the graph representation) never needed, so they have to be built:

    (i)  `σ_{K_n}(I) = Σ_{t ∈ Θ_n ∩ I} σ_{K_n}({t})`  — `σ` is purely atomic on the normals;
    (ii) `Σ_{t ∈ Θ_n ∩ I} k₀(g⁺_{K_n}(t))·δ ≤ ∫_I k₀(g_{K_n}) + O(δ)`  — Lemma 6.4.1.

### 23.1 The face lemma (the foundation)

> Let `K` be compact convex with `K = ⋂_{r ∈ A} hpLe r (h_K r)`, `A` finite, and let `s` be an
> angle with `u_r ≠ u_s` for every `r ∈ A`, and with positive width `h_K(s) + h_K(s+π) > 0`.
> Then `|e_K(s)| = 0`.

*Proof.*  If the edge were nondegenerate, its midpoint `m` would have some active constraint
`r ∈ A` (otherwise a ball around `m` is inside `K` and `⟪·,u_s⟫` is not maximal at `m`).  Both
endpoints then satisfy `⟪·,u_r⟫ = h_K(r)` (a midpoint of a segment attains the max only if the
whole segment does), so `⟪v⁺ − v⁻, u_r⟫ = 0`, i.e. `sin(r−s) = 0`, i.e. `u_r = ±u_s`.  `u_r = u_s`
is excluded by hypothesis; `u_r = −u_s` forces `K` into the line `⟪·,u_s⟫ = h_K(s)`, contradicting
positive width.  ∎

### 23.2 From the face lemma to (i)

* the maximizer is unique off `A`, so `t ↦ v⁺_K(t)` is continuous on each gap and takes values in
  the (finite) extreme-point set of the polytope, hence is **constant** on each gap;
* with `v⁺_K ≡ P` on the gap `(a,b)`, `h_K(t) = ⟪P, u_t⟫` there, and
  `∫_a^b ⟪P, u_t⟫ dt = ⟪P, v_a − v_b⟫` (`integral_inner_u`, already in `Sofa/SurfaceArea.lean`),
  so `arcFn K b − arcFn K a = ⟪P, v_b⟫ − ⟪P, v_a⟫ + ⟪P, v_a − v_b⟫ = 0` and `σ_K((a,b]) = 0`.

### 23.3 From the face lemma to Lemma 6.4.1

With `v⁺_K(t' + π/2) = C` constant for `t' ∈ [t, t+δ)` (same gap), `armGp_eq_add` gives
`g⁺_K(t') = h_K(t') − ⟪C, u_{t'}⟫`, so for `t, t' ` in the same gap

    `|g⁺_K(t') − g⁺_K(t)| ≤ |h_K(t') − h_K(t)| + ‖C‖·‖u_{t'} − u_t‖ ≤ 2R·δ`,

using the `R`-Lipschitz continuity of `h_K` (`Sofa/SupportLipschitz.lean`).  This replaces Baek's
Thales-circle argument **and** removes the need for Lemma 6.3.1 (diameter ≤ 5): the constant `2R`
is uniform over the `K_n` because Hausdorff convergence puts them all in one bounded set.

### 23.4 Lemma 6.4.2 and the limit

`g⁺_K(t) = ∫_{(t, t+π/2]} sin(s−t) dσ_K(s)` (`armGp_eq_setIntegral`), so the weak convergence
`σ_{K_n} → σ_K` of `Sofa/SigmaLimit.lean` gives `g⁺_{K_n}(t) → g⁺_K(t)` at every `t` where
`σ_K({t}) = σ_K({t+π/2}) = 0` — all but countably many `t`.  Dominated convergence (the `g⁺` are
uniformly bounded) then gives `∫|g⁺_{K_n} − g⁺_K| → 0`, and `k₀` is 1-Lipschitz, so the same holds
for `k₀ ∘ g⁺`.  Finally `σ_K(I) ≤ liminf σ_{K_n}(I)` for open `I` (portmanteau, or directly from
`tendsto_sigmaK_Ioc`) gives Theorem 6.4.3 on `(a,b) ⊆ (0, π/2)`.

The two extreme angles and `σ_K({0})` (§19.4) are still needed for the half-open interval
`[0, b)`.

### 23.5 The face lemma as formalized (`Sofa/NicheBound.lean`, round 19)

* `u_frame s r : u r = cos (r−s) • u s + sin (r−s) • v s` (from `decomp_u_v` and the two inner
  products `⟪u s, u r⟫ = cos (r−s)`, `⟪v s, u r⟫ = sin (r−s)`);
* `supportFn_congr_of_u_eq`: `h_K` depends only on the *direction* `u t`;
* **`edgeLength_eq_zero_of_no_normal`**: `K = ⋂_{r ∈ A} H_K(r)` with `A : Finset ℝ`,
  `u r ≠ u s` for every `r ∈ A`, and positive width in direction `s` ⟹ `|e_K(s)| = 0`;
* `volume_setOf_inner_eq`: a line `{⟪p, u s⟫ = c}` is null (translate of the kernel of a nonzero
  functional, `Measure.addHaar_submodule` + `volume_tr`);
* `width_pos_of_volume_ne_zero`: a body of positive area has positive width in every direction —
  so the width hypothesis of the face lemma is automatic for any cap with `|K| > 0`.

### 23.6 `σ_K` gives no mass to a gap (round 20)

* **`vtxP_mem_vtx2_image`**: for a polygon `K = ⋂_{r∈A} H_K(r)` with positive widths and a
  non-normal direction `t`, the vertex `v⁺_K(t)` equals `v_K(r, r')` for two normals with
  `sin(r'−r) ≠ 0`.  Proof: at least one constraint is active at `Q = v⁺_K(t)` (else `Q` is
  interior and `⟪·,u_t⟫` can be increased — `inner_dir_nonpos_of_max`); every active normal is
  non-parallel to `u_t` (positive width rules out the antipode); and if all active normals were
  parallel to each other, pushing along `±v_{r₀}` would increase `⟪·,u_t⟫` — so two active
  normals are non-parallel, and they determine `Q`.
* **`vtxP_const_on_gap`**: on a closed interval of non-normal directions, `v⁺_K = v⁻_K` (the face
  lemma), so `v⁺_K` is two-sided continuous there (Thm 2.1.3 in both directions); its image lies
  in the finite set `{v_K(r,r') : r, r' ∈ A}`, and a continuous real function on an interval with
  finite image is constant (`const_of_continuousOn_of_finite_image`, by the intermediate value
  theorem — `Set.Icc_infinite`).  Testing against `u_0` and `u_{π/2}` gives constancy of `v⁺_K`.
* **`sigmaK_gap_eq_zero`**: with `v⁺_K ≡ Q` on `[a,b]`, `h_K(t) = ⟪Q, u_t⟫` there, so
  `∫_a^b h_K = ⟪Q, v_a⟫ − ⟪Q, v_b⟫` (`integral_inner_u`) and
  `arcFn K b − arcFn K a = ⟪Q, v_b⟫ − ⟪Q, v_a⟫ + ⟪Q, v_a⟫ − ⟪Q, v_b⟫ = 0`.

So for a polygon cap the surface measure is concentrated on the normals — item (i) of §23.

## 24. §6.4 formalized (2026-09-18, Opus 5, round 21)

### 24.1 Lemma 6.4.1 without the Thales circle (and without Lemma 6.3.1)

Baek's proof of Lemma 6.4.1 observes that for `t' ∈ I = (t, t+δ)` the outer corner `y_K(t')` runs
along an arc of the circle with diameter `AC`, where `A = A⁺_K(t)` and `C = C⁺_K(t)` are the two
vertices that stay *constant* along the gap; the arc has central angle `2δ`, and `|AC| ≤ 5` by
Lemma 6.3.1 (`diam K ≤ 5`), so the arc has length `≤ 5δ`.

Round 20 gives the constancy of the vertex directly (`vtxP_const_on_gap`), and with it the whole
circle argument collapses.  Theorem 6.2.5's *algebraic* form (`armGp_eq_add`, already in
`Sofa/ArmLength.lean`) says

    `g⁺_K(s) = h_K(s) + ⟪v⁺_K(s + π/2), v_{s+π/2}⟫`,

and `v_{s+π/2} = −u_s`, so with `v⁺_K ≡ C` on the gap containing `s + π/2`,

    `g⁺_K(s) = h_K(s) − ⟪C, u_s⟫`        (`armGp_eq_sub_inner`).

Both terms are `R`-Lipschitz in `s` (`abs_supportFn_sub_le` for the first; `‖C‖ ≤ R` and
`‖u_s − u_{s'}‖ ≤ |s − s'|` for the second), so

    `|g⁺_K(s') − g⁺_K(s)| ≤ 2R |s' − s|`    (`abs_armGp_sub_le_gap`).

Note what this *gains*: Baek's `5δ` needs `diam K ≤ 5`, a genuine theorem about *caps*; `2Rδ`
needs only a bound on the bodies, which along a Hausdorff-convergent sequence is free.  So
**Lemma 6.3.1 is not on the critical path for Theorem 6.4.3 at all**.

The integrated form, which is what Equation (6.4) uses, is

    `k₀(g⁺_K(t))·δ ≤ ∫_t^{t+δ} k₀(g⁺_K) + 2R δ²`   (`k0_armGp_mul_le_integral`),

using that `k₀` is 1-Lipschitz (`abs_k0_sub_le`, from `k₀(x) = (3|x−1| + 1 + ||x−1| − 1|)/4`, the
`max a b = (a+b+|a−b|)/2` rewriting of Def 6.3.4).  The bound on the integrand only has to hold
a.e. on `(t, t+δ)`, which is what lets the *open* gap `(t+π/2, t+δ+π/2)` suffice: at the right
endpoint `v⁺_K` jumps, but a single point is null.

### 24.2 Lemma 6.4.2 without Portmanteau

Baek proves `g⁺_{K_n}(t) → g⁺_K(t)` a.e. by writing `g⁺_K(t) = ∫ s dσ_K` for an upper
semicontinuous `s` (Lemma 6.2.4), applying the Portmanteau theorem to get
`limsup g⁺_{K_n}(t) ≤ g⁺_K(t)`, and running the mirror argument with `g⁻` and a lower
semicontinuous integrand for the reverse inequality.

The same algebraic form makes this unnecessary.  `g⁺_K(t) = h_K(t) + e_K^{max}(t + π/2)` and

* `h_{K_n}(t) → h_K(t)` from `|h_S(t) − h_T(t)| ≤ d_H(S,T)` (`Sofa/Hausdorff.lean`);
* `e_{K_n}^{max}(s) → e_K^{max}(s)` whenever `|e_K(s)| = 0`, which is exactly
  `tendsto_edgeMax_of_tendsto_hausdorffDist` (`Sofa/SigmaLimit.lean`, round 9).

The exceptional set is `{t : |e_K(t + π/2)| ≠ 0}`, countable because `arcFn K` is monotone and

    `arcFn K s → arcFn K t − |e_K(t)|` as `s ↑ t`   (`tendsto_arcFn_nhdsLT`),

i.e. the jump of `arcFn K` at `t` *is* `|e_K(t)|`, so an atom is a discontinuity point and
`Monotone.countable_not_continuousAt` applies (`countable_edgeLength_ne_zero`).  Dominated
convergence (bound `k₀(g⁺_{K_n}(t)) ≤ 1 + 2R`, from `k₀(x) ≤ 1 + |x|` and `|g⁺_K| ≤ 2R`) then
gives

    `∫_a^b k₀(g⁺_{K_n}) → ∫_a^b k₀(g⁺_K)`   (`tendsto_integral_k0_armGp`).

### 24.3 Equations (6.4) and (6.5)

`σ_K` in this formalization is a Stieltjes measure, so intervals are half-open on the *right*
(`Ioc`), where Baek writes `[t, t+δ)`.  The translation is `sigmaK_Ioc_eq_singleton_of_gap`:

> if `(a, b)` contains no normal direction, then `σ_K((a, b]) = σ_K({b})`

(proved by `arcFn_const_on_Ico_gap` plus the jump formula above).  So a step's whole mass sits at
its *right* endpoint, and Equation (6.4) becomes, for `t = (j+1)δ`,
