    `σ_K((jδ, (j+1)δ]) ≤ ∫_{(j+1)δ}^{(j+2)δ} k₀(g⁺_K) + (6 + 4R) δ²`   (`sigmaK_step_le_integral`),

where `(6 + 4R)δ² = (k₀(g⁺_K(t)) + 5)δ² + 2Rδ²` bounds Theorem 6.3.3's error plus Lemma 6.4.1's,
using `k₀(g⁺_K(t)) ≤ 1 + 2R`.  It needs `1 ≤ j` and `j + 3 ≤ n`: Theorem 6.3.3 needs
`t − δ, t, t + δ ∈ Θ` (see §19.4), which for `Θ_{π/2,n} = {iδ : 1 ≤ i ≤ n−1}` is exactly that.

The two gap hypotheses are supplied by `uniform_gap_base` and `uniform_gap_shift`: the normal set
of a polygon cap is `Θ ∪ (Θ + π/2) ∪ {ω, π/2, ω+π, 3π/2}` (`polyCapFin`, `IsPolyCap.eq_iInter_fin`),
and for `ω = π/2` neither `(jδ, (j+1)δ) ⊆ (0, π/2)` nor its `π/2`-shift meets it — every case is a
one-line application of `u_ne_u_of_sub_lt` (`cos(r−s) = 1` and `|r−s| < 2π` force `r = s`).

Summing (finite subadditivity along the cut points, `measure_Ioc_le_sum`, plus
`intervalIntegral.sum_integral_adjacent_intervals`) and shifting the window back by `δ`:

    `σ_K((mδ, (m+N)δ]) ≤ ∫_{mδ}^{(m+N)δ} k₀(g⁺_K) + N(6+4R)δ² + (1+2R)δ`
                                                          (`sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_poly`),

and `Nδ ≤ π/2`, so the total error is `O(δ)` — Baek's `µ_{K_n}(I) + O(δ)` in Equation (6.5).

### 24.4 What is left for Theorem 6.4.3

`sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_of_limit'` already takes the limit: with `K_n → K` in Hausdorff distance,
all inside `ball 0 R`, and the discrete bound on `(a', b']`,

    `σ_K((a,b]) ≤ ∫_{a'}^{b'} k₀(g⁺_K)`  for `[a,b] ⊆ [a',b']` with `a, b` continuity points,

and `integral_k0_armGp_widen_le` shrinks `∫_{a'}^{b'}` back to `∫_a^b` at a cost
`(1+2R)((a−a') + (b'−b))`.  Note the two limits are *genuine limits*, not `liminf`s.

Three items remain:

1. **`hwidth` for maximum polygon caps**: `0 < h_K(s) + h_K(s+π)` for every `s`.  By
   `width_pos_of_volume_ne_zero` (round 19) it suffices that `|K_n| > 0`, which should follow from
   `IsCap` (the cap contains the unit horizontal segment and reaches height 1) — to be proved.
2. **A uniform `R`**: all `K_n` lie in a fixed ball.  Available from `IsCap` bounds or from
   Hausdorff convergence.
3. **Matching the dyadic cut points with the continuity points of `arcFn K`**: given continuity
   points `a < b` of `arcFn K` inside `(0, π/2)`, pick dyadic `a' < a`, `b < b'` in `Θ_n` for
   large `n`, apply `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_of_limit'` and then let `a' ↑ a`, `b' ↓ b` with
   `integral_k0_armGp_widen_le`.  Then extend from continuity points to all Borel sets
   (countably many atoms + outer regularity), which is Baek's last paragraph.

After that: Cor 6.4.4 / Prop 6.4.5–6.4.6 and the packaging into `ArmPair` (`Sofa/ArmBound.lean`)
to close Theorem 6.1.1.

## 25. Theorem 6.4.3 and Theorem 6.5.1 proved (2026-09-18, Opus 5, round 22)

### 25.1 The three items of §24.4, settled

1. **`hwidth` for maximum polygon caps.** `quarter_le_polySofaArea_one` (`Sofa/BalancedMax.lean`)
   says the all-ones polygon cap has polygon sofa area `≥ 1/4`; a *maximum* polygon cap beats it,
   and `ofReal_le_volume_of_polySofaArea` turns that into `|K| ≥ 1/4 > 0`.  Then
   `width_pos_of_volume_ne_zero` (round 19) gives positive width in every direction
   (`volume_ne_zero_of_isMaxPolyCap`, `width_pos_of_isMaxPolyCap`), so the face lemma applies and
   the `hwidth` hypothesis disappears from `sigmaK_step_le_integral` and
   `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_poly`.
2. **A uniform `R`.**  `exists_eventually_norm_le`: if `d_H(K_n, K) → 0` with `K` compact, then
   `K ⊆ ball 0 R₀` and eventually `d_H < 1`, so eventually `K_n ⊆ ball 0 (R₀+1)`
   (`IsCompact.exists_infDist_eq_dist` + `infDist_le_hausdorffDist_of_mem`).  *Lemma 6.3.1 is not
   used anywhere.*  Accordingly `tendsto_integral_k0_armGp` and the two limit theorems now take
   the bound only `∀ᶠ n in atTop` (`tendsto_integral_filter_of_dominated_convergence`).
3. **Dyadic cut points vs. continuity points.**  `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_dyadic` proves Theorem
   6.4.3 when the *outer* interval `[A, B]` has dyadic endpoints `A = p(π/2)/2^k`,
   `B = q(π/2)/2^k`: for `n_i ≥ 2^{k+1}` (and `n_i` a power of two, `n` strictly monotone) we have
   `2^k ∣ n_i`, so `A = m δ_i` and `B = (m+N) δ_i` with `m ≥ 1` and `m + N + 2 ≤ n_i`, and
   `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_poly` applies with error `≤ ((6+4R)(π/2) + (1+2R)) δ_i → 0`.
   `exists_dyadic_sandwich` (floor/ceiling at scale `2^k`, `2^{-k} < min(ε/(π/2), α, 1−β)`) then
   sandwiches an arbitrary `[a, b] ⊂ (0, π/2)` between dyadic endpoints within `ε`, and
   `integral_k0_armGp_widen_le` pays `(1+2R')·2ε` for the widening.  Letting `ε → 0`
   (`le_of_forall_pos_le_add`) gives

       `σ_K((a, b]) ≤ ∫_a^b k₀(g⁺_K)`   (`sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_balanced`)

   for every `0 < a ≤ b < π/2` with `|e_K(a)| = |e_K(b)| = 0`.

### 25.2 The left endpoint `0`

Baek's Equation (6.3) is also needed for `I = [0, b)`, and this is where his §6.3 endpoint problem
(§19.4) would bite.  It does not arise here: the dyadic outer interval always has `A > 0`, so it
is enough to let `a ↓ 0` in `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_balanced`.  The atoms are countable, so the
continuity points are dense (`Set.Countable.dense_compl`); choosing continuity points
`x_j ∈ (0, min(T, 1/(j+1)))` and using the *right*-continuity of `arcFn K`
(`tendsto_arcFn_nhdsGT`, from `continuousWithinAt_vtxP`) gives

    `σ_K((0, T]) ≤ ∫_0^T k₀(g⁺_K)`   (`sigmaK_Ioc_zero_le_integral`)

for every continuity point `T ∈ (0, π/2)`; the boundary term `∫_0^{x_j} k₀(g⁺_K) ≤ (1+2R')x_j → 0`.

### 25.3 Theorem 6.5.1

`armFp_ge_of_sigmaK_le` (round 15) already converts that measure inequality into the differential
inequality on the arm lengths, because Theorem 6.2.5 is an *identity*.  Hence

    `f⁺_K(0) + ∫_0^T m₀(g⁺_K(s)) ds ≤ f⁺_K(T)`   (`armFp_ge_balanced`)

for every balanced maximum cap and every continuity point `T ∈ (0, π/2)`.  This is Baek's
Theorem 6.5.1 — with **no** Radon–Nikodym, **no** density `r_K`, **no** Prop 5.1.4, **no**
Corollary 6.4.4.

### 25.4 What is left in Chapter 6

* Extend `armFp_ge_balanced` from continuity points to *all* `T ∈ [0, π/2)`.  `f⁺_K` is
  right-continuous (`edgeMax K` is), and `∫_0^T m₀(g⁺_K)` is continuous in `T` (the integrand is
  bounded by `1 + 4R`), so approximating `T` from the *right* by continuity points suffices.
* Package the result as an `ArmPair` (`Sofa/ArmBound.lean`) — this needs `f⁺_K`, `g⁺_K`
  continuous and the bounds `0 ≤ f, g ≤ 2`, i.e. Prop 6.4.6 (2) and Lemma 6.3.1-style estimates —
  and conclude `1 < f⁺_K(π/2)`, i.e. the injectivity condition Theorem 6.1.1.
* Theorem 6.1.2 (Gerver's sofa satisfies the injectivity condition); later proved in
  `Sofa/GerverInj.lean`.

## 26. `σ_K` has no atoms in `(0, π/2)` (2026-09-18, Opus 5, round 23)

Baek's Corollary 6.4.4 deduces from Theorem 6.4.3, *via the Radon–Nikodym theorem*, that `σ_K` is
absolutely continuous on `[0, π/2)` and hence that `A⁺_K = A⁻_K`, `C⁺_K = C⁻_K` there
(Prop 6.4.5), which is what makes `f_K`, `g_K` well defined and continuous (Prop 6.4.6 (1)).

The atom-freeness needs no Radon–Nikodym at all.  The continuity points of `arcFn K` are dense
(the atoms are countable), so for any `t ∈ (0, π/2)` and any `ε > 0` there are continuity points
`a ∈ (t − ε, t)` and `b ∈ (t, t + ε)` inside `(0, π/2)`, and then

    `σ_K({t}) ≤ σ_K((a, b]) ≤ ∫_a^b k₀(g⁺_K) ≤ (1 + 2R)(b − a) < (1 + 2R)·2ε`

by Theorem 6.4.3 and `k₀(g⁺_K) ≤ 1 + 2R`.  Letting `ε → 0` gives `|e_K(t)| = σ_K({t}) = 0`
(`edgeLength_eq_zero_of_balanced`).

Consequently every continuity hypothesis in §25 can be discharged:

* `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_balanced'` — Theorem 6.4.3 for **all** `0 < a ≤ b < π/2`;
* `sigmaK_Ioc_zero_le_integral'` — the `I = [0, T)` case for **all** `T ∈ (0, π/2)`;
* `armFp_ge_balanced` — **Theorem 6.5.1** for **all** `T ∈ (0, π/2)`:

      `f⁺_K(0) + ∫_0^T m₀(g⁺_K(s)) ds ≤ f⁺_K(T)`.

It also gives `v⁺_K = v⁻_K` on `(0, π/2)`, i.e. Prop 6.4.5 on that interval, so `A_K` and `C_K`
are well defined there and (by Thm 2.1.3 in both directions) continuous.

### 26.1 What is left to close Theorem 6.1.1

`ArmPair f G` (`Sofa/ArmBound.lean`) needs, with `f t = f_K(t)` and `G t = g_K(π/2 − t)`:

1. `Continuous f`, `Continuous G` on `[0, π/2]`.  Atom-freeness on `(0, π/2)` gives it on the
   open interval; `armGp K t = h_K(t) + e^{max}_K(t + π/2)` also needs atom-freeness of `σ_K` on
   `(π/2, π)`, which follows by the mirror symmetry Prop 3.5.1 (`Sofa/Mirror.lean`: the mirror of
   a balanced maximum cap is one), plus the two endpoints.
2. `0 ≤ f`, `0 ≤ G` — immediate from `e^{min}_K ≤ ⟪p, v_t⟫ ≤ e^{max}_K` on the edge and the
   definition of the outer corner.
3. `bound_f` with the constant `1`: this is `f_K(0) ≥ 1`, i.e. `e^{max}_K(0) ≤ 0` for a cap in
   standard position — part of Prop 6.4.6 (2).
4. `bound_G` — Theorem 6.5.1 for the mirror cap, transported by Prop 6.2.2
   (`g_{K^m}(t) = f_K(π/2 − t)`).

Then `one_lt_of_armPair` (round 14) gives `f_K(t) > 1` on `(0, π/2]`, i.e. Theorem 6.5.6, hence
the injectivity condition Theorem 6.1.1.

## 27. The mirror cap, and where `f_K(0) = 1` actually comes from (2026-09-18, Opus 5, round 24)

### 27.1 Proposition 6.2.2

The reflection `M_ω` of `Sofa/Mirror.lean` acts on the moving frame by
`M_ω u_s = u_{ω+π/2−s}` and `M_ω v_s = −v_{ω+π/2−s}` (`mirror_u_eq`, `mirror_v_eq`), hence
`y_{M K}(t) = M_ω y_K(ω − t)` (`outerCorner_mirror`) and, with Prop 2.5.4 for the vertices,

    `f⁺_{M K}(t) = g⁻_K(ω − t)`,   `g⁺_{M K}(t) = f⁻_K(ω − t)`

(`armFp_mirror`, `armGp_mirror`).  Note the sign swap `+ ↦ −`: the reflection reverses the
rotational order, so the counterclockwise-most contact point becomes the clockwise-most one.

### 27.2 The mirror of a balanced maximum cap at `ω = π/2`

`Sofa/RightAngle.lean` already has `IsBalancedMaxCap.image_mirror` for `ω < π/2`; the restriction
is an artifact of `mirror_oω`, whose proof divides by `cos ω`.  At `ω = π/2` the fan apex is
`o_{π/2} = (0,1)` (`oω_pi_div_two`) and `M_{π/2}` is `(x,y) ↦ (−x,y)`, so it is fixed
(`mirror_oω_pi_div_two`); `mirror_oω_le`, `IsMaxPolyCap.image_mirror_le` and
`IsBalancedMaxCap.image_mirror_le` then cover `0 < ω ≤ π/2`.

Two consequences:

* **No atoms on `(π/2, π)` either** (`edgeLength_eq_zero_of_balanced_upper`), since
  `|e_{M K}(t)| = |e_K(π − t)|`.  Hence `f⁻_K = f⁺_K` and `g⁻_K = g⁺_K` on `(0, π/2)`
  (`armFm_eq_armFp_of_balanced`, `armGm_eq_armGp_of_balanced`) — Baek's Prop 6.4.5.
* **Theorem 6.5.1 for the mirror** (`armGm_ge_balanced`), which is Baek's `bound_G`:

      `g⁻_K(π/2) + ∫_0^T m₀(f⁻_K(π/2 − s)) ds ≤ g⁻_K(π/2 − T)`.

Also `0 ≤ f^±_K`, `0 ≤ g^±_K` for any compact body (`armFp_nonneg` etc.), which is the easy half
of Prop 6.4.6 (2).

### 27.3 **Finding: `f_K(0) = 1` is not proved in the paper, and the §19.4 endpoint gap is what it needs**

> **Correction (2026-09-24).** The paper justifies `f_K(0) = 1` only informally (§1.7.3), but no new
> argument is needed: `σ_K({0}) = 0` is Corollary 6.4.4, whose proof (Theorem 6.4.3 on `[0, b)`)
> is valid at the endpoint angle, because the "gap" of §19.4 does not exist (see the correction
> there). Together with `e^{min}_K(0) = 0` (§28.1) this gives `f_K(0) = 1`. The conclusion below,
> that a genuinely new argument is needed, is wrong.

Baek's Lemma 6.5.2 (and hence Theorem 6.5.6 and the injectivity condition Theorem 6.1.1) uses
`f_K(0) = 1`.  The only justification in the paper is the informal sketch §1.7.3: *"We have
`f(0) = 1` because the point `A(0)` should be on the x-axis."*  Chapter 6 never returns to it.
Here is what it actually takes.

`f⁺_K(0) = h_K(π/2) − e^{max}_K(0) = 1 − e^{max}_K(0)`, so `f⁺_K(0) ≥ 1 ⟺ e^{max}_K(0) ≤ 0`.

* `e^{min}_K(0) = 0` **is** provable.  For a maximum polygon cap `K_n` the direction `0` is not a
  normal (the normal set is `Θ ∪ (Θ+π/2) ∪ {π/2, 3π/2}`), so by the face lemma the `x`-maximizing
  face is a single vertex, namely the intersection of the bottom line `y = 0` (normal `3π/2`,
  `h_{K_n}(3π/2) = 0`) with the line of normal `δ = min Θ`: it is `(h_{K_n}(0), 0)`.  Passing to
  the Hausdorff limit, `(h_K(0), 0) ∈ K`, whence `e^{min}_K(0) ≤ 0 ≤ e^{max}_K(0)`, and with
  `K ⊆ {y ≥ 0}`, `e^{min}_K(0) = 0`.
* Therefore `f⁺_K(0) ≥ 1 ⟺ e^{max}_K(0) = e^{min}_K(0) ⟺ **σ_K({0}) = 0**`.
* `σ_K({0}) = 0` is exactly the content of Theorem 6.4.3 on the half-open interval `I = [0, b)`
  — the case whose proof sums Equation (6.4) over `t ∈ [0,b) ∩ ({0} ∪ Θ_n)`.  The term `t = 0` is
  free (`|e_{K_n}(0)| = 0`), but the term `t = δ` needs Theorem 6.3.3 at the endpoint angle `δ`,
  where `t − δ = 0 ∉ Θ_n` — **precisely the gap recorded in §19.4**.
* And that term cannot be bounded crudely: `|e_{K_n}(δ_n)|` is the length of the almost-vertical
  right edge of `K_n`, and it *converges to* `|e_K(0)|`.  So any `O(δ)` bound on it already
  contains the conclusion.

Conclusion: the endpoint restriction of §19.4 is **load-bearing**, not cosmetic.  Closing it needs
a genuinely new argument — the natural candidates are the *balanced* condition (`σ_{K_n}(δ) =
τ_{K_n}(δ)`, Def 3.4.x) or a direct geometric argument that a balanced maximum cap cannot have a
vertical right edge.  Until then `1 ≤ f⁺_K(0)` has to be carried as an explicit hypothesis, and
everything downstream of Lemma 6.5.2 (Theorem 6.5.6, Theorem 6.1.1) is conditional on it.

Note that one lemma suffices for both halves: `g⁻_K(π/2) = f⁺_{M K}(0)` by Prop 6.2.2, so
`1 ≤ f⁺_{K'}(0)` for every balanced maximum cap `K'` gives both `bound_f` and `bound_G`.

## 28. **The endpoint-angle gap is closed**, and `f_K(0) = 1` (2026-09-22, Opus 5, round 25)

> **Correction (2026-09-24).** There was no gap in the paper (see the correction to §19.4). This
> section is an alternative route at the extreme angles, and it is the one the formalization uses.

§27.3 identified `f_K(0) = 1` as an unproved input of Baek's Lemma 6.5.2, and traced it to the
endpoint-angle gap of §19.4.  Both are now closed.  The key is that the *balanced* condition gives
a bound on `σ(t)` that needs **no neighbouring angles at all**.

### 28.1 `e^{min}_K(0) = 0` for every cap

For `ω = π/2`, `capAngles (π/2) = [0, π] ∪ {3π/2}`.  Let `P = v⁺_K(0)` and `Q = h_K(0)·u_0 =
(h_K(0), 0)`.  In the frame at `0`, `⟪P, u_r⟫ = h_K(0)·cos r + e^{max}_K(0)·sin r` and
`⟪Q, u_r⟫ = h_K(0)·cos r`, so `⟪Q, u_r⟫ ≤ ⟪P, u_r⟫ ≤ h_K(r)` whenever `sin r ≥ 0` — which covers
all of `[0, π]` — and at `r = 3π/2` one checks `⟪Q, u_{3π/2}⟫ = 0 = h_K(3π/2)` directly.  Hence
`Q ∈ K`, `Q ∈ e_K(0)` and `e^{min}_K(0) ≤ ⟪Q, v_0⟫ = 0`; the reverse holds because `K ⊆ {y ≥ 0}`.
(`edgeMin_zero_of_isCap`; consequences `armFm_zero_of_isCap`: `f⁻_K(0) = 1`, and
`armFp_zero_eq`: `f⁺_K(0) = 1 − σ_K({0})`.)

### 28.2 The crude niche-side bound — Theorem 6.3.3 at *any* angle

Let `K` be a polygon cap with `ω = π/2` and `t ∈ Θ`.  In the graph representation the niche side
with normal `t` is `actR(t) ∩ posR`, where `roofR = max_{s∈Θ} min(b_s, d_s)`.

* If `roofR(x) = b_t(x)` but `b_t(x) > d_t(x)`, the maximum is attained at some `s ≠ t` and
  `b_t(x)` equals `b_s(x)` or `d_s(x)` — a crossing of two distinct lines.  So off the null set
  `crossSet Θ h t` we have `b_t(x) ≤ d_t(x)` (`actR_subset_cross_or_le`).
* The fan floor is `floorF (π/2) h ≡ h(π/2) − 1 = 0` for a cap, so on `posR`, `b_t(x) > 0`.

The two conditions `0 < b_t(x)` and `b_t(x) ≤ d_t(x)` are half-lines in `x`, and

    `actR(t) ∩ posR ⊆ crossSet ∪ [cos t·(h(t)−1) − sin t·(h(t+π/2)−1),  (h(t)−1)/cos t]`,

an interval of length `sin t·((h(t)−1)·tan t + (h(t+π/2)−1))`.  Dividing by `sin t`:

    `τ(t) ≤ ((h(t) − 1)·tan t + (h(t+π/2) − 1))⁺`     (`tauH_le_crude`)

and, by the balanced condition (Thm 3.4.9, `IsMaxPolyCap.balanced`),

    `|e_K(t)| ≤ σ(t) = τ(t) ≤ ((h(t) − 1)·tan t + (h(t+π/2) − 1))⁺`   (`edgeLength_le_crude`)

for **every** `t ∈ Θ` of a maximum polygon cap.  **This is the bound Theorem 6.3.3 does not give at
`t = δ` and `t = (n−1)δ`** (§19.4).  It is weaker than Theorem 6.3.3 in general (it does not see
`k₀(g⁺)`), but it is `O(δ)` exactly where it is needed.

At `t = δ`: `h(δ) ≤ R`, `tan δ ≤ δ + δ³ ≤ 2δ`, and `h(δ+π/2) − 1 = h(δ+π/2) − h(π/2) ≤ Rδ` by the
Lipschitz bound, so `|e_K(δ)| ≤ (3R+2)·δ` (`edgeLength_delta_le`).

### 28.3 `σ_K({0}) = 0`

For a maximum polygon cap, `0` is not a normal direction, so `|e_K(0)| = 0`
(`edgeLength_zero_of_maxPolyCap`) and with §28.1, `arcFn K 0 = e^{max}_K(0) = 0`
(`arcFn_zero_of_maxPolyCap`).  Splitting `(0, cδ]` into `(0, δ]` (bounded by §28.2) and
`(δ, cδ]` (Equation (6.5), `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_poly` with `m = 1`):

    `arcFn K_n (c δ) ≤ (1 + 2R)·(c δ) + ((3R+2) + (6+4R)(π/2))·δ`   (`arcFn_le_of_maxPolyCap`).

Now let `K_n → K` be a balanced maximum cap and `b = (π/2)/2^k`.  Every `b ∈ (0, π/2)` is a
continuity point of `arcFn K` (round 23), and for `n_i ≥ 2^{k+1}` we have `b = c_i δ_i` with
`c_i = n_i/2^k ≥ 2` and `c_i + 2 ≤ n_i`, so taking `i → ∞`:

    `arcFn K b ≤ (1 + 2R)·b`.

Since `arcFn K` is monotone, `arcFn K 0 ≤ arcFn K b ≤ (1+2R)b` for every `k`, hence
`arcFn K 0 ≤ 0`.  But `arcFn K 0 = e^{max}_K(0) = |e_K(0)|` by §28.1, so

    **`σ_K({0}) = |e_K(0)| = 0`**   (`edgeLength_zero_of_balanced`)

and therefore

    **`f⁺_K(0) = 1`**   (`armFp_zero_of_balanced`),

    **`1 + ∫_0^T m₀(g⁺_K(s)) ds ≤ f⁺_K(T)`**   (`one_add_integral_le_armFp`),

for every balanced maximum cap and every `T ∈ (0, π/2)`.  This is Baek's Theorem 6.5.1 with the
constant `1` that Lemma 6.5.2 needs — **unconditional**.

### 28.4 What is left

`ArmPair f G` (`Sofa/ArmBound.lean`) now needs only its *continuity* component: `f` and `G` must be
continuous on all of `ℝ` (the iteration `armIter` only looks at `[0, π/2]`, so any continuous
extension will do).  `f⁺_K = f⁻_K` and `g⁺_K = g⁻_K` on `(0, π/2)` (round 24), `f⁻_K` is
left-continuous and `f⁺_K` right-continuous, so

    `f(t) := if t ≤ 0 then f⁺_K(0) else f⁻_K(min t (π/2))`

is continuous, equals `f⁺_K` on `[0, π/2)`, and takes the correct limit value at `π/2`; similarly
for `G(t) := g⁻_K(π/2 − ·)`.  With `bound_f` = §28.3 and `bound_G` = `armGm_ge_balanced` (round 24)
together with `g⁻_K(π/2) = f⁺_{K^m}(0) = 1`, `one_lt_of_armPair` then gives Theorem 6.5.6 and the
injectivity condition **Theorem 6.1.1**.

## 29. Theorem 6.5.6: `f_K, g_K > 1` (2026-09-22, Opus 5, round 26)

`Sofa/ArmBound.lean` (round 14) reduced Theorem 6.5.6 to the one-dimensional statement
`one_lt_of_armPair`, which consumes an `ArmPair f G`:

    `Continuous f`, `Continuous G`, `0 ≤ f`, `0 ≤ G`,
    `bound_f : ∀ t ∈ [0, π/2], 1 + ∫_0^t m₀(G(π/2 − u)) du ≤ f t`,
    `bound_G : ∀ t ∈ [0, π/2], 1 + ∫_0^t m₀(f(π/2 − u)) du ≤ G t`.

### 29.1 The functions

Baek's `f_K` is defined (Def 6.4.1) only on `[0, π/2)` and extended continuously; the extension
is `f⁻_K`, because `f⁺_K = f⁻_K` on `[0, π/2)` (no atoms — rounds 23 and 25) while at `π/2` the
*left* limit is `f⁻_K(π/2)`.  So we take

    `F_K(t) := f⁻_K(max 0 (min t (π/2)))`        (`armFK`),
    `G := F_{K^m}`,

and Prop 6.2.2 in its `f⁻` form, `f⁻_{M K}(t) = g⁺_K(ω − t)` (`armFm_mirror`, new), gives

    `G(π/2 − u) = F_{K^m}(π/2 − u) = g⁺_K(u)`    on `[0, π/2]`   (`armFK_mirror_sub`),

so `bound_f` *is* `1 + ∫_0^t m₀(g⁺_K) ≤ F_K(t)`.

### 29.2 Continuity

`v⁻_K = v⁺_K` on `[0, π/2)`, `v⁻_K` is always left-continuous (Thm 2.1.3) and `v⁺_K` always
right-continuous, so `v⁻_K` is continuous on `[0, π/2]` (`continuousOn_vtxM_Icc`), hence so is
`f⁻_K` (`continuousOn_armFm_Icc`).  Clamping the argument into `[0, π/2]` gives a globally
continuous `F_K` (`continuous_armFK`).

### 29.3 The bound at the right endpoint

`bound_f` holds at `t = 0` because `F_K(0) = f⁻_K(0) = 1` (round 25's `edgeMin_zero_of_isCap`),
and on `(0, π/2)` because `F_K = f⁺_K` there and round 25 gives `1 + ∫_0^t m₀(g⁺_K) ≤ f⁺_K(t)`.
At `t = π/2` (and in fact for every `t > 0` uniformly) take the limit from the left: the primitive
`T ↦ ∫_0^T m₀(g⁺_K)` is continuous (`intervalIntegral.continuous_primitive`), and `f⁻_K` is
left-continuous (`tendsto_armFm_nhdsLT`), so the inequality passes to the limit.

### 29.4 `bound_G` for free

`G = F_{K^m}` and `(K^m)^m = K`, so `bound_G` for `(F_K, F_{K^m})` is literally `bound_f` for
`(F_{K^m}, F_{(K^m)^m}) = (F_{K^m}, F_K)`, i.e. the same lemma applied to the balanced maximum cap
`K^m` (round 24).  Hence `ArmPair (armFK K) (armFK (K^m))` (`armPair_balanced`), and
`one_lt_of_armPair` gives

    **`f⁺_K(t) > 1` and `g⁺_K(t) > 1` for every `t ∈ (0, π/2)`**

(`one_lt_armFp_of_balanced`, `one_lt_armGp_of_balanced`) — Baek's **Theorem 6.5.6**.

### 29.5 Proposition 6.4.6 (1), and a correction to our own Definition 6.1.2

`g⁺_K(r) = f⁻_{K^m}(π/2 − r)` *identically*, so `g⁺_K` is continuous on the whole closed interval
`[0, π/2]` (`continuousOn_armGp_Icc`).  But `f⁺_K` is continuous only on `[0, π/2)`
(`continuousOn_armFp_Ico`): its jump at `π/2` is `f⁺_K(π/2) − f⁻_K(π/2) = −σ_K({π/2}) =
−|e_K(π/2)|`, the length of the *top face* of the cap, which is positive for Gerver's sofa.

**This means our `Sofa/Injective.lean` encoding of Definition 6.1.2 is slightly too strong**: its
clause `continuousOn_armFp : ContinuousOn (armFp K) (Icc 0 (π/2))` should read `Ico 0 (π/2)` (or
be stated for the continuous extension `armFK`).  Baek's `f_K` on `[0, π/2]` is the extension, not
`f⁺_K`.  The fix is one line, but it rebuilds everything downstream of `Injective.lean`, so it is
batched with the next structural edit.

### 29.6 What is left for Theorem 6.1.1

Of `IsInjectiveCap`, clause (3) is now proved and clause (2) needs only the `Ico`/extension
correction of §29.5.  Clause (1) — `σ_K ≪ dt` on `[0, π/2)` and on `(π/2, π]` — still needs
upgrading Theorem 6.4.3 from intervals to all Borel sets: `σ_K((a,b]) ≤ (1+2R)(b−a)` on
`[0, π/2)` plus outer regularity of Lebesgue measure and the decomposition of an open subset of
`ℝ` into countably many disjoint intervals; the right half follows by the mirror.

---

## 30. Theorem 6.1.1: balanced maximum caps are injective (2026-09-22, Opus 5, round 27)

`Sofa/AbsCont.lean`, `Sofa/Injective.lean` (edited).

### 30.1 Lipschitz ⇒ absolutely continuous, by a difference Stieltjes function

The plan recorded in §29.6 was outer regularity plus the decomposition of an open subset of `ℝ`
into countably many intervals.  There is a shorter route that stays inside the Stieltjes
formalism and needs neither.

Fix `lo ≤ hi` and `C ≥ 0`, and suppose

    (L)   arcFn K b − arcFn K a ≤ C·(b − a)   whenever lo ≤ a ≤ b ≤ hi.

Let `clamp t := max lo (min t hi)` and define two functions on all of `ℝ`:

    A(t) := arcFn K (clamp t),        D(t) := C·t − A(t).

`A` is monotone and right-continuous because `clamp` is monotone, continuous, and maps `Ici x`
into `Ici (clamp x)` — so `A` is a `StieltjesFunction` (`clampSF`).  The point is that **(L) says
exactly that `D` is monotone**: for `a ≤ b`,

    A(b) − A(a) ≤ C·(clamp b − clamp a) ≤ C·(b − a),

the first inequality by (L) applied to `clamp a ≤ clamp b` (both lie in `[lo, hi]`), the second
because `clamp` is 1-Lipschitz (`clamp_sub_le`) and `C ≥ 0`.  `D` is right-continuous as a
difference of right-continuous functions, hence a `StieltjesFunction` (`lipDiffSF`).

Now for every `a < b`,

    A.measure (a,b] + D.measure (a,b]
      = ofReal(A b − A a) + ofReal(D b − D a)        (both differences are ≥ 0)
      = ofReal((A b − A a) + (D b − D a))
      = ofReal(C·(b − a)) = (ofReal C • volume) (a,b],

and both sides are finite on every `Ioc`, so `Measure.ext_of_Ioc'` gives

    A.measure + D.measure = ofReal C • volume,

whence `A.measure ≤ ofReal C • volume` (`clampSF_measure_le`) and, by
`Measure.absolutelyContinuous_of_le_smul`, `A.measure ≪ volume`.

The clamp is invisible inside the window: for `a ⊔ lo < b ⊓ hi` we have
`clamp (b ⊓ hi) = b ⊓ hi` and `clamp (a ⊔ lo) = a ⊔ lo`, and outside that case
`Ioc (a ⊔ lo) (b ⊓ hi)` is empty and both `ofReal`s vanish.  Hence

    (σ_K).restrict (Ioc lo hi) = A.measure.restrict (Ioc lo hi)     (`sigmaK_restrict_eq_clampSF`)

and therefore `(σ_K).restrict (Ioc lo hi) ≪ volume` (`sigmaK_restrict_ac`).

### 30.2 The Lipschitz constant on `[0, π/2)`

Theorem 6.4.3 in the form `sigmaK_Ioc_zero_le_integral'` / `sigmaK_Ioc_le_integral_balanced'`
(round 21–25) gives, for `0 ≤ a ≤ b < π/2`,

    arcFn K b − arcFn K a = σ_K((a,b]) ≤ ∫_a^b k₀(g⁺_K(t)) dt.

With `R` any radius bound for `K`, `|g⁺_K| ≤ 2R` (`abs_armGp_le`) and `k₀(x) ≤ 1 + |x|`
(`k₀_le_one_add_abs`), so the integrand is `≤ 1 + 2R` and

    arcFn K b − arcFn K a ≤ (1 + 2R)(b − a)        (`arcFn_lip_left`).

Note `b < π/2` is essential — `σ_K` has an atom at `π/2` (the cap's top face).  So §30.1 applies
with `lo = 0`, `hi = c` for any `c < π/2`, giving `(σ_K)|_{(0,c]} ≪ volume`.

To reach `Ico 0 (π/2)` we cover

    s ∩ [0, π/2) ⊆ {0} ∪ ⋃_n (s ∩ (0, π/2 − 1/(n+1)])

and use the round-25 endpoint theorem `σ_K({0}) = |e_K(0)| = 0`
(`edgeLength_zero_of_balanced`).  Countable subadditivity finishes: `sigmaK_ac_left`.

### 30.3 The right half by the mirror

Let `K^m := mirror(π/2) '' K`, which is again a balanced maximum cap
(`IsBalancedMaxCap.image_mirror_le`, round 24) and has the same radius bound (`mirrorL` is a
linear isometry).  From `edgeMax_mirror`, `supportFn_mirror` and
`∫_0^t h_K(π − s) ds = ∫_{π−t}^{π} h_K` (`intervalIntegral.integral_comp_sub_left`),

    arcFn K^m (t) = −e^min_K(π − t) + ∫_{π−t}^{π} h_K        (`arcFn_mirror_eq`).

On `(π/2, π]` there are no atoms: `edgeLength_eq_zero_of_balanced_upper` covers `(π/2, π)`, and
at `π` we use `|e_K(π)| = |e_{K^m}(0)| = 0` by `edgeLength_mirror` plus
`edgeLength_zero_of_balanced` applied to `K^m` (`edgeLength_pi_of_balanced`).  Hence
`e^max_K = e^min_K` throughout `(π/2, π]`, and for `π/2 < a ≤ b ≤ π`

    arcFn K b − arcFn K a = (e^max_K b − e^max_K a) + ∫_a^b h_K
                          = (e^min_K b − e^min_K a) + ∫_a^b h_K
                          = arcFn K^m (π − a) − arcFn K^m (π − b)     (`arcFn_sub_eq_mirror`),

and `0 ≤ π − b ≤ π − a < π/2`, so §30.2 applied to `K^m` bounds the right-hand side by
`(1 + 2R)(b − a)`: `arcFn_lip_right`.  Covering `Ioc (π/2) π = ⋃_n (π/2 + 1/(n+1), π]` — no
endpoint atom to worry about this time — gives `sigmaK_ac_right`.

### 30.4 The correction of §29.5, done

Baek's Definition 6.4.1 is explicit: `f_K(t) := f⁺_K(t) = f⁻_K(t)` for `t ∈ [0, π/2)` and
**`f_K(π/2) := f⁻_K(π/2)`**; dually `g_K(t) := g⁺_K(t) = g⁻_K(t)` for `t ∈ (0, π/2]` and
**`g_K(0) := g⁺_K(0)`**.  Proposition 6.4.6 (1) then asserts continuity of `f_K, g_K` on the
*closed* interval `[0, π/2]`.  So `f_K = f⁻_K = armFm` and `g_K = g⁺_K = armGp`, both on
`Icc 0 (π/2)`, and the clause of our `IsInjectiveCap` becomes

    continuousOn_armFm : ContinuousOn (armFm K) (Icc 0 (π/2))
    continuousOn_armGp : ContinuousOn (armGp K) (Icc 0 (π/2))

satisfied by `continuousOn_armFm_Icc` and `continuousOn_armGp_Icc` (round 26).  The old
`ContinuousOn (armFp K) (Icc 0 (π/2))` was false for Gerver's sofa.

### 30.5 Theorem 6.1.1

    isInjectiveCap_of_balanced : IsBalancedMaxCap K (π/2) → IsInjectiveCap K

assembling (1) = §30.2 + §30.3, (2) = §30.4, (3) = Theorem 6.5.6 (round 26).  **Chapter 6 is
complete.**

### 30.6 What is left

* **Theorem 6.1.2** (Gerver's sofa satisfies the injectivity condition) needs "`G` is a balanced
  maximum sofa", which Baek gets from Theorem 2 of [Ger92] — an external input, not proved in
  this paper.  Formalizing it means formalizing Gerver's construction of a convergent sequence of
  maximum polygon sofas.
* **Chapter 7** (§7.2–§7.4): Mamikon's theorem and the area identity.
* **Chapter 8**, including Theorem 8.4.1, which Baek does not prove.

### 30.7 Clause (1) in Baek's own words

Baek states clause (1) of Definition 6.1.2 as the existence of measurable densities
`r_K, s_K : [0, π/2] → ℝ≥0` with `σ_K = r_K(t) dt` on `[0, π/2)` and `σ_K = s_K(t − π/2) dt` on
`(π/2, π]`.  Our `IsInjectiveCap` states absolute continuity instead, which is the same thing by
Radon–Nikodym; `exists_density_of_ac` and `exists_densities_of_balanced` make that translation
machine-checked (the density is `Measure.rnDeriv`, and `Measure.withDensity_rnDeriv_eq` turns
`≪ volume` into `σ_K|_· = volume.withDensity r`).

---

## 31. Chapter 7 §7.2–§7.4: the curve area functional and Mamikon's theorem (2026-09-22, Opus 5.5, round 28)

`Sofa/StieltjesIBP.lean`, `Sofa/CurveArea.lean`, `Sofa/Mamikon.lean`.

### 31.1 What Chapter 8 actually needs from Chapter 7

Reading §8.2–§8.3 against §7.2–§7.4 shows that Chapter 8 feeds `J` only four kinds of curves:

* segments `J(p, q)`;
* convex arcs `J(u^{a,b}_K)` of `K`, `B`, `D`;
* the inner corner `x_K|[φ^R, φ^L]` and the outer corner `y_K = x_K + u_t + v_t` — Lipschitz,
  since `h_K` is;
* the parametrised supporting lines `l^t_K(s) = v_K(s, t)` — which move along the fixed line
  `l_K(t)`, so their `J` is a segment's (Thm 8.3.1).

So `J` is never needed for a BV curve with a singular (Cantor) part, and **absolutely continuous**
curves suffice.  Mathlib v4.33.1 has exactly the right tools for those:
`AbsolutelyContinuousOnInterval`, its FTC `integral_deriv_eq_sub`, and integration by parts
`integral_mul_deriv_eq_deriv_mul`.  Hence

    curveJ z a b := ½ ∫_a^b z(t) × z'(t) dt      (z' = deriv z)

(`curveJ`), agreeing with Baek's `½∫ z × dz` on absolutely continuous `z`.

### 31.2 The one new analytic tool: integration by parts against a Stieltjes measure

For a Stieltjes function `F` and `g` absolutely continuous on `[a, b]`:

    ∫_{(a,b]} g dF = g(b) F(b) − g(a) F(a) − ∫_a^b g'(s) F(s) ds        (`integral_Ioc_stieltjes`)

Proof: with `f(t, s) := 1_{s<t} g'(s)` on `(a,b]²`, which is integrable for `dF ⊗ ds` (dominated
by `|g'(s)|`),

    ∫_{(a,b]} (g(t) − g(a)) dF(t) = ∫∫ f dF ds = ∫_{(a,b]} g'(s) F((s,b]) ds = ∫_a^b g'(s)(F(b) − F(s)) ds

by Fubini (`integral_integral_swap`).  The inner integrals are the AC FTC on `(a,t)` (the endpoint
`t` is Lebesgue-null) and `F.measure_Ioc`.

### 31.3 The closed form of `½∫ h_K dσ_K`

With `h = h_K`, `e = e⁺_K = ⟪v⁺_K, v_t⟫`:

    2·curveG K a b = ∫_{(a,b]} h dσ_K = h(b) e(b) − h(a) e(a) + ∫_a^b (h² − e²) dt   (`two_mul_curveG_eq`)

1. `h` is `R`-Lipschitz, hence AC (`absolutelyContinuousOnInterval_supportFn`).
2. `h' = e` a.e.: at every `t` with `|e_K(t)| = 0` the one-sided derivatives `e⁺(t)`, `e⁻(t)` of
   `h` agree (`hasDerivAt_supportFn_of_edgeLength_eq_zero`), and the atoms of `σ_K` are
   countable (`ae_deriv_supportFn`).
3. §31.2 with `F = arcFn K = e + H`, `H(t) = ∫_0^t h`, gives
   `h(b)F(b) − h(a)F(a) − ∫ e(e + H)`.
4. `∫ e H = ∫ h' H = [hH] − ∫ h H' = [hH] − ∫ h²` (AC integration by parts; `H` is `C¹`).

Everything else cancels.  Removing the atom at `b` (`convJ_eq_curveG`):

    2·J(u^{a,b}_K) = h(b) e⁻(b) − h(a) e⁺(a) + ∫_a^b (h² − e²)                  (`two_mul_convJ_eq`)

where `J(u^{a,b}_K) := convJ K a b := ½∫_{(a,b)} h dσ_K` — Baek's Thm 7.3.2 taken as the
definition.  Consequences:

* **Lemma 7.3.4** (`convJ_concat`): `J(u^{a,c}) = J(u^{a,b}) + J(v⁻(b), v⁺(b)) + J(u^{b,c})`, the
  middle term being `h(b)|e_K(b)|/2` (`segJ_vtxM_vtxP`).
* **Lemma 7.3.3 / Thm 7.3.2 (quadratic)** (`convB`, `isQuadraticOn_convJ`):
  `B(K₁, K₂) = ½∫_{(a,b)} h_{K₁} dσ_{K₂}` is convex-bilinear, `J(u^{a,b}_K) = B(K, K)`.
* With Thm 7.1.3 (round 13): the classical **`|K| = ½∫_{S¹}(h_K² − h_K'²)`**
  (`volumeReal_eq_integral_sq_sub_sq`), an independent sanity check of both results.

### 31.4 Mamikon's theorem

Let `z(t) = h(t) u_t + w(t) v_t` (`tanCurve K w`; every curve with `z(t) ∈ l_K(t)` is of this
form, `eq_tanCurve`), `w` AC on `[a, b]`, `a < b`.  Then (`mamikonM_eq`)

    M_K(a, b; z) := J(v⁺(a), z(a)) + J(z) + J(z(b), v⁻(b)) − J(u^{a,b}_K) = ½ ∫_a^b (w − e)² dt.

Proof: where `h` and `w` are differentiable, `z' = (e − w) u_t + (h + w') v_t`
(`hasDerivAt_tanCurve`), so `z × z' = h² + w² + h w' − w e` (`cross_frame`).  One AC integration by
parts, `∫ h w' = [h w] − ∫ e w`, and the closed form §31.3 give

    2M = h(a)(w(a) − e(a)) + ∫(h² + w² − 2we) + [hw]_a^b + h(b)(e⁻(b) − w(b))
         − h(b)e⁻(b) + h(a)e(a) − ∫(h² − e²)  =  ∫ (w − e)².

No Lebesgue–Stieltjes calculus of vector measures (Baek's Lemma 5.1.3) is used.  Our statement
needs only `a < b`, not Baek's `b < a + π` (that bound matters for the geometric picture, not for the
identity).

**Theorem 7.4.2** (`mamikonM_mix_le`): if `w` is convex-linear in `K` then so is
`α = w − e⁺_K` (`edgeMax_mix`), and `K ↦ ½∫α²` is convex along `c_λ` because `x ↦ x²` is.

`mamikonM_eq'` restates Mamikon for a curve given pointwise on `[a,b]` only (`mamikonM_congr`,
`curveJ_congr`), which is the form Chapter 8 will use for `l^t_K` (defined only on `(t − π, t]`).

### 31.5 What is *not* formalized from Chapter 7, and why that is (so far) harmless

* **Thm 7.2.3** (Green's theorem: `J` = enclosed area for a rectifiable Jordan curve), **Prop 7.2.7**
  (orientation), **Lemma 7.3.1**, **Lemma 7.3.5**: Jordan-curve topology, absent from Mathlib.
  In Chapter 8 they are used *only* in Lemmas 8.2.2 and 8.2.3, to convert `J`-values into areas
  for the upper bound `A(K) ≤ Q(K, B_K, D_K)` (Thm 8.2.4).  Plan: prove those two lower bounds
  directly — the core region by `regionBetween` + substitution (for `K ∈ K_i`,
  `x'_K · e₀ = −(f−1)cos t − (g−1)sin t < 0` on `(0, π/2)`, so the core curve is a graph over the
  `x`-axis), the side regions by the sector-area machinery of rounds 11–12.
* **Thm 7.3.2, geometric part** (`u^{a,b}_K` is a rectifiable Jordan arc and `½∫h dσ` is its `J`):
  we *define* `J(u^{a,b}_K)` by the formula.  Its geometric content, for bodies with the origin in
  the interior, is `secR_eq_curveG` (round 12).
* **Def 7.2.6 for BV curves with a singular part**: never needed (§31.1).

### 31.6 A harmless typo in Baek's proof of Theorem 7.4.1

The last line of the chain reads `= αu_t × d(αu_t) = αu_t × (u_t dα + αv_t dt) = α² dt`, but
`z − v⁺_K = α v_t` (Thm 7.4.1's own statement), so it should be
`αv_t × d(αv_t) = αv_t × (v_t dα − α u_t dt) = α² dt`.  Both computations give `α² dt`, so the
theorem is unaffected; our proof does not go through this line at all.

## 32. Chapter 8 §8.3: concavity of `𝒬` (2026-09-22, Opus 5.5, round 29)

`Sofa/MamikonLine.lean`, `Sofa/CapHalf.lean`, `Sofa/ConcaveQ.lean`.

### 32.1 Statement

Fix `φ ∈ (0, π/4)` (`φ^R = φ`, `φ^L = π/2 − φ`).  `LEq φ K B D` records the four *equalities* of
Baek's Def 8.1.3 that §8.3 uses:

    h_K(φ) + h_B(π + φ) = 1,   h_K(π/2) + h_B(3π/2) = 1,   h_K(π/2) + h_D(3π/2) = 1,   h_K(π − φ) + h_D(2π − φ) = 1.

**Theorem 8.3.8** (`Qfun_concave`).  For caps `K₁, K₂` of rotation angle `π/2` with nonempty
interior, compact nonempty `B_i, D_i`, `LEq φ K_i B_i D_i`, and `λ ∈ [0, 1]`:

    (1 − λ) 𝒬(K₁, B₁, D₁) + λ 𝒬(K₂, B₂, D₂) ≤ 𝒬(c_λ(K₁, K₂), c_λ(B₁, B₂), c_λ(D₁, D₂)).

No injectivity (`K ∈ K_i`), no inequality of `𝓛`, and no containment `B ⊆ K` is used.

### 32.2 Supporting-line curves and Mamikon along a line (`MamikonLine.lean`)

`l^t_K(s) = v_K(s, t)` for `s < t` and `l^t_K(t) = v⁻_K(t)` (`lineCurve`).  On `l_K(s)`,
`v_K(s, t) = h(s) u_s + w(s) v_s` with `w(s) = lineW K t s = (h(t) − h(s)cos(t − s))/sin(t − s)`,
which is AC on `[a, b] ⊂ (t − π, t)` (`ac_lineW`).  So Mamikon applies:

    mamikonSeg K a b t := J(v⁺(a), l(a)) + J(l(a), l(b)) + J(l(b), v⁻(b)) − J(u^{a,b}_K)
                        = ½ ∫_a^b (lineW K t s − e⁺_K(s))² ds          (a < b < t, t − a < π; `mamikonSeg_eq`)

(the middle term is `J(l^t_K|[a,b])` by **Thm 8.3.1**, `curveJ_vtx2`).  **Convexity**
(`mamikonSeg_mix_le`): `lineW` is convex-linear in `K` (`lineW_mix`), so is `e⁺_K`, and `x ↦ x²` is
convex.  **The endpoint `b = t`** (needed for `M_K(π/2, π; l^π_K)`, `ℛ_B`, `ℒ_D`): there the
integrand blows up, so convexity is obtained from `b < t` by letting `b → t⁻`
(`tendsto_mamikonSeg_left`: `l^t_K(b) → v⁻_K(t)` by Thm 2.1.3, `v⁻_K` is left-continuous,
`convJ K a b → convJ K a t` from the left) — `mamikonSeg_mix_le_self`.

For the outer corner `y_K(t) = h(t)u_t + h(t + π/2)v_t = x_K(t) + u_t + v_t` (`w = h(· + π/2)`):
`mamikonM_outerCorner`, `mamikonM_outerCorner_mix_le`.  **Lemma 8.3.6 (1)** explicitly
(`curveJ_outer_sub_inner`, from `two_mul_curveJ_frame`: `2J(a u + b v) = ∫(a² + b² + ab' − ba')`):

    J(y_K|[p,q]) − J(x_K|[p,q]) = ∫_p^q (h(t) + h(t + π/2) − 1) dt
                                  + ((h(q + π/2) − h(q)) − (h(p + π/2) − h(p)))/2.

### 32.3 Caps (`CapHalf.lean`)

* **Plane separation** (`mem_of_forall_inner_le_supportFn`): for compact convex `S`,
  `x ∈ S ⟺ ∀ t, ⟪x, u_t⟫ ≤ h_S(t)` (Hahn–Banach, `geometric_hahn_banach_closed_point`).
* Consequences for a cap `K` (rotation angle `π/2`): the floor corners `h_K(0)u_0`, `h_K(π)u_π ∈ K`;
  `e⁺_K(π) = 0`, `e⁻_K(π) = −|e_K(π)|`, `e⁺_K(0) = |e_K(0)|`; on `[π, 3π/2]`, `h_K(t) = −h_K(π)cos t`,
  on `[3π/2, 2π]`, `h_K(t) = h_K(0)cos t`.
* **Lemma 8.3.5, core** (`volumeReal_cap`): start from `|K| = ½∫_0^{2π}(h² − (e⁺)²)`
  (`volumeReal_eq_integral_sq_sub_sq`, §31.3).  On `[π, 3π/2]` and `[3π/2, 2π]`, `h = c cos t`, so
  `∫(h² − h'²) = c²[sin t cos t]` vanishes between multiples of `π/2`
  (`integral_sq_sub_sq_of_eq_cos`).  The closed form `2J(u^{0,π}_K) = h(π)e⁻(π) − h(0)e⁺(0) +
  ∫_0^π(h² − e²)` with `e⁻(π) = −|e(π)|`, `e⁺(0) = |e(0)|` then gives

      |K| = J(u^{0,π}_K) + h_K(π)|e_K(π)|/2 + h_K(0)|e_K(0)|/2.

  Baek removes the two atoms using `K ∈ K_i`; we keep them (§32.5 absorbs them).
* **Caps are closed under Minkowski combinations** (`isCap_mix`), via plane separation; and
  `interior_mix_nonempty`.

### 32.4 Lemma 8.3.4 (`Qfun_eq`)

`𝒬 = 𝒫_K − ℛ_B − ℒ_D` on `LEq`.  The points: `W^R_K = sec φ (h_K(φ) − 1) u_0` (`ptWR`),
`Z^L_K = sec φ (h_K(π − φ) − 1) v_{π/2}` (`ptZL`).  From `LEq`: `v_B(π + φ, 3π/2) = W^R_K` and
`v_D(3π/2, 2π − φ) = Z^L_K` (`eq_of_inner_u_eq_two`: two points on the same two non-parallel lines
coincide); `x^R_K, X_B, W^R_K` lie on `l(φ, h_K(φ) − 1)`, `Z^L_K, Y_D, x^L_K` on
`l(π − φ, h_K(π − φ) − 1)`, and `W^R_K, W_B, Z_D, Z^L_K` on the floor `y = 0` through `O`.  On a
line `l(t, c)`, `J(p, q) = c⟪q − p, v_t⟫/2` (`segJ_on_line`), so the collinear chains telescope and
the floor segments vanish.

### 32.5 Lemma 8.3.7 as an explicit identity (`Sfun_add_Pfun`)

Write `𝒮_K = M₁ + M₂ + M₃ + M₄` (Def 8.3.2, with `l^{π/2−φ+π/2}_K(π/2 − φ) = y_K(π/2 − φ)`,
`vtx2_add_pi_div_two`) and expand `|K|` in `𝒫_K` by §32.3 and `convJ_concat` at `φ, π/2 − φ, π/2`.
All `J(u^{·,·}_K)` cancel; the rest groups into eight brackets:

| bracket | terms | value |
|---|---|---|
| (i) | `J(v⁺(0), v(0, π/2)) + h(0)|e(0)|/2` | `h(0)/2` |
| (ii) | `J(v(0, π/2), v(φ, π/2))` (top line `y = 1`) | `(v(0,π/2)₀ − v(φ,π/2)₀)/2` |
| (iii) | `J(v(φ,π/2), v⁻(φ)) + J(v⁻(φ), v⁺(φ)) + J(v⁺(φ), y(φ))` (on `l_K(φ)`) | `J(v(φ, π/2), y(φ))` |
| (iii′) | `J(v(φ,π/2), y(φ)) + J(x(φ), W^R)`, with `v(φ,π/2) = W^R + c_R`, `y(φ) = x(φ) + u_φ + v_φ` | `(W^R × (u_φ+v_φ) + c_R × x(φ) + c_R × (u_φ+v_φ))/2` |
| (iv) | `J(y|[φ, π/2−φ]) − J(x|[φ, π/2−φ])` | §32.2 |
| (v) | `J(y, v⁻) + J(v⁻, v⁺) + J(v⁺, y)` at `π/2 − φ` (a closed chain on one line) | `0` |
| (vi) | `J(y(π/2−φ), v(π/2, π−φ)) + J(Z^L, x(π/2−φ))`, with `v(π/2,π−φ) = Z^L + c_L` | `(x × c_L + (u+v) × Z^L + (u+v) × c_L)/2` |
| (vii) | `J(v(π/2,π−φ), v⁻(π/2)) + J(v⁻(π/2), v⁺(π/2)) + J(v⁺(π/2), v(π/2, π))` (top line) | `(v(π/2,π−φ)₀ − v(π/2,π)₀)/2` |
| (viii) | `J(v(π/2, π), v⁻(π)) + h(π)|e(π)|/2` | `h(π)/2` |

with the constant vectors `c_R = (sec φ (1 − sin φ), 1)` (`cvecR`, `vtx2_phi_eq`) and
`c_L = (sec φ (sin φ − 1), 1)` (`cvecL`, `vtx2_pi_sub_phi_eq`).  The sum of the right column is
`Λ(K)` (`Lamfun`), and every entry is convex-linear in `K` (`Lamfun_mix`: `h_K`, `v_K(a, b)`,
`x_K(t)`, `W^R_K`, `Z^L_K` are all convex-linear, `c_R, c_L, u_t + v_t` are constants).  Hence

    𝒮_K + 𝒫_K = Λ(K)        for every cap of rotation angle π/2 with nonempty interior.

The atoms of `σ_K` at `0`, `φ`, `π/2 − φ`, `π/2`, `π` are absorbed by (i), (iii), (v), (vii), (viii),
which is why `K ∈ K_i` is not needed.

### 32.6 Theorem 8.3.8

`Qfun_eq_Lam`: `𝒬 = Λ(K) − 𝒮_K − ℛ_B − ℒ_D`.  `Λ` is convex-linear; `𝒮_K` (four Mamikon regions),
`ℛ_B`, `ℒ_D` are convex (§32.2); `c_λ` preserves caps, nonempty interiors and `LEq` (`LEq.mix`).
Hence `𝒬` is concave along `c_λ`.

### 32.7 Two harmless typos in Baek §8.3

* **Def 8.3.3**: `ℛ_B` is printed as `M_B(π/2 + φ^R, 3π/2; l^{3π/2}_B)`.  The proof of Lemma 8.3.4
  needs `M_B(π + φ^R, 3π/2; l^{3π/2}_B)` (its terms are `J(X_B, W^R_K)`, `J(W^R_K, W_B)`, `J(b_B)` with
  `X_B = v⁺_B(π + φ^R)`, `b_B = u^{π+φ^R, 3π/2}_B`).  Formalized with `π + φ^R` (`Rfun`).
* **Proof of Lemma 8.3.7**: `J₄₂ = J(l^π_K(π/2), C_K(π/2))` is claimed to be `h_K(π/2)/2`; it is
  `J(v_K(π/2, π), v⁻_K(π)) = h_K(π)/2 − h_K(π)|e_K(π)|/2`, and together with the atom of `|K|` at `π`
  it gives `h_K(π)/2` — still linear, so the lemma stands (bracket (viii)).

### 32.8 What is left in Chapter 8

* **Quadraticity of `𝒬`** (the other half of Thm 8.3.8 / Prop 8.2.1), needed only for the
  directional-derivative argument of §8.5.
* **§8.1**: `𝓛` as a convex domain, `K ↦ (K, B_K, D_K)`, Thm 8.1.8.  Plan: drop the constraint
  `|K| ≥ 2.2` (it needs Brunn–Minkowski, absent from Mathlib) if §8.5 can do without it.
* **§8.2**: the upper bound `A(K) ≤ 𝒬(K, B_K, D_K)` (Lemmas 8.2.2–8.2.3, Thm 8.2.4) without Green's
  theorem — the main remaining risk of Chapter 8.
* **§8.4–§8.5**: Gerver's sofa via Romik's ODEs, the unproved Thm 8.4.1, directional derivatives.

## 33. Chapter 8 §8.1 and Lemma 8.2.2 without Green's theorem (2026-09-22, Opus 5.5, round 30)

`Sofa/TangentCap.lean`, `Sofa/NicheSide.lean`.

### 33.1 The classical area formula for every compact convex set

Round 28 proved `|K| = ½∫_{t₀}^{t₀+2π}(h_K² − (e⁺_K)²)` for convex bodies with nonempty interior
(via Thm 7.1.3).  `B_K` may have empty interior, so we remove the hypothesis
(`volumeReal_eq_integral_sq_sub_sq_of_convex`): for `ε > 0`, `K_ε := K + B̄(0, ε)` has nonempty
interior, `h_{K_ε} = h_K + ε` (`supportFn_add`, `supportFn_closedBall`) and `e⁺_{K_ε} = e⁺_K` (both are
the right derivative of the support function: `edgeMax_eq_of_supportFn_eq_add`).  Hence

    |K_ε| = ½∫(h² − e²) + ε∫h + πε²,

and `K + B̄(0, ε) = cthickening ε K` (`IsCompact.add_closedBall_zero`), whose measure tends to `|K|`
(`tendsto_measure_cthickening_of_isCompact`).

### 33.2 The tangent-cap formula

Let `a < b < a + π`, `W = v_K(a, b) = l_K(a) ∩ l_K(b)`, `C = conv(K ∪ {W})`.

* `h_C = max(⟪W, u_t⟫, h_K)` (`supportFn_convexHull_insert`: a half-plane is convex).
* On `[a, b]`, `h_K(t) ≤ ⟪W, u_t⟫`: `sin(b−a) u_t = sin(b−t) u_a + sin(t−a) u_b` with nonnegative
  coefficients, and `h_K` is sublinear (`supportFn_le_inner_vtx2`).
* On `[b, a + 2π]`, `⟪W, u_t⟫ ≤ h_K(t)`: `W = X + λ v_a = Y + μ v_b` with `X ∈ e_K(a)`, `Y ∈ e_K(b)`,
  `λ ≥ 0 ≥ μ`; then `⟪W,u_t⟫ = ⟪Y,u_t⟫ + μ sin(t−b) ≤ h_K(t)` for `t ∈ [b, b+π]` and
  `⟪W,u_t⟫ = ⟪X,u_t⟫ + λ sin(t−a) ≤ h_K(t)` for `t ∈ [a+π, a+2π]` (`inner_vtx2_le_supportFn`).

So `h_C = ⟪W, u_t⟫` on `[a, b]` (hence `e⁺_C = ⟪W, v_t⟫` a.e. there) and `h_C = h_K` on
`[b, a + 2π]` (hence `e⁺_C = e⁺_K` a.e.).  Subtracting the two classical formulas, with
`∫_a^b(⟪W,u⟫² − ⟪W,v⟫²) = ⟪W,u_a⟫⟪W,v_a⟫ − ⟪W,u_b⟫⟪W,v_b⟫` and the closed form
`2J(u^{a,b}_K) = h(b)e⁻(b) − h(a)e⁺(a) + ∫_a^b(h² − e²)` (§31.3):

    |conv(K ∪ {W})| − |K| = J(v⁺_K(a), W) + J(W, v⁻_K(b)) − J(u^{a,b}_K)      (`volumeReal_convexHull_insert_vtx2`)

(the two segment terms are `h(a)(⟪W,v_a⟫ − e⁺(a))/2` and `h(b)(e⁻(b) − ⟪W,v_b⟫)/2` by `segJ_on_line`).
The region `conv(K ∪ {W}) \ K` is exactly the region `R` enclosed by the Jordan curve `Γ` (arc +
two tangent segments) in Baek's proof of Lemma 8.2.2.

### 33.3 Lemma 8.1.6

For an injective cap `K`, `x_K` has right derivative `(1 − f⁺)u_t + (g⁺ − 1)v_t` everywhere, with
`f⁺, g⁺ > 1` on `(0, π/2)` (Def 6.1.2 (3)).  So `s ↦ ⟪x_K(s), u_φ⟫` has right derivative
`(1 − f⁺) cos(s − φ) − (g⁺ − 1) sin(s − φ) ≤ 0` on `(φ, π/2)`, and by the fencing theorem
(`image_le_of_deriv_right_le_deriv_boundary`; `antitoneOn_Icc_of_rightDeriv_nonpos`)

    ⟪x_K(t), u_φ⟫ ≤ ⟪x_K(φ), u_φ⟫ = h_K(φ) − 1      for t ∈ [φ, π/2]

(`inner_innerCorner_u_le_right`); mirror: `⟪x_K(t), u_{π−φ}⟫ ≤ h_K(π−φ) − 1` on `[0, π/2 − φ]`.
Consequence (`mem_QminusS_right`): if `⟪p, u_φ⟫ ≥ h(φ) − 1` and `⟪p, u_t⟫ < h(t) − 1` with
`φ < t ≤ π/2`, then writing `p − x_K(t) = α u_t + β v_t` (`α < 0`),
`⟪p − x_K(t), u_φ⟫ = α cos(t−φ) − β sin(t−φ)`; if `β ≥ 0` this is `< 0`, contradicting the first
hypothesis.  So `β < 0`, i.e. `p ∈ Q⁻_K(t)`.  Only the non-strict corner inequality is needed.

### 33.4 `B_K`, `D_K` and Lemma 8.1.7

`Bset φ K = K ∩ ⋂_{t∈[φ,π/2]} {h_K(t) − 1 ≤ ⟪p, u_t⟫}`, `Dset φ K` symmetric.

* The floor corners are in them: `h(t) ≤ h(0) cos t + sin t` on `[0, π/2]` (the cap lies in
  `x ≤ h(0)`, `y ≤ 1`), so `⟪h(0)u_0, u_t⟫ = h(0) cos t ≥ h(t) − 1`.  Hence `h_{B_K}(3π/2) = 0`,
  `h_{D_K}(3π/2) = 0`, and both are nonempty.
* (1), (3): `h_K(t) + h_{B_K}(π + t) ≤ 1` is the definition.
* (2) at `φ`: let `p` be the end of the chord `K ∩ b^R_K` in the direction `v_φ` (the chord contains
  `W^R_K`).  If some `⟪p, u_t⟫ < h(t) − 1`, then `t ∈ (φ, π/2)` and `p ∈ Q⁻_K(t)` (§33.3), an open
  set, so `p + ε v_φ ∈ Q⁻_K(t) ∩ F ⊆ N(K) ⊆ K` is a further point of the chord — contradiction.
  So `p ∈ B_K` and `h_{B_K}(π + φ) ≥ −⟪p, u_φ⟫ = 1 − h_K(φ)`.  **This uses `N(K) ⊆ K`**, which Baek's
  proof also uses (through "(2) of Thm 2.5.8"), although it is not part of the definition of `K_i`.
* `W^R_K, Z^L_K ∈ K` (Lemma 8.1.5) from the bottom edge `[−h(π), h(0)] × {0} ⊆ K` and
  `1 ≤ (h(0) + h(π)) cos φ`; Lemma 8.1.4 from `2 + 2 sin φ < (h(0) + h(π)) cos φ`
  (`⟪p, u_φ⟫ + ⟪p, u_{π−φ}⟫ = 2 p_1 sin φ ≤ 2 sin φ`).  Both follow from `|K| ≥ 2.2` for small `φ`.

Together: `leq_Bset_Dset : LEq φ K B_K D_K` — exactly the input of §32.

### 33.5 Lemma 8.2.2

With `B = B_K`, `W = W^R_K = v_B(π + φ, 3π/2)` (`vtx2_eq_ptWR`, from the two equalities):

* `conv(B ∪ {W}) ⊆ K` (convexity), `⊆ H̆^R_K` and `⊆ {y ≥ 0}` (all generators are).
* A point of `conv(B ∪ {W}) \ B` violates some `H^b_K(t)`, necessarily with `t ∈ (φ, π/2)`, hence
  lies in `Q⁻_K(t) ∩ F ⊆ N(K)` (`convexHull_diff_Bset_subset`).
* By §33.2 and `J(W, W_B) = 0` (both on the floor through `O`):

      J(X_B, W^R_K) − J(b_B) = |conv(B ∪ {W})| − |B| = |conv(B ∪ {W}) \ B| ≤ |N(K) ∩ H̆^R_K|.

  (`le_volumeReal_niche_right`; the left half is symmetric.)

### 33.6 What is left for Thm 8.2.4

Lemma 8.2.3 (the core region `N(K) \ H̆^R \ H̆^L`).  Plan: for injective `K`, `x_K` is differentiable
on `(0, π/2)` (no atoms of `σ_K` at `t` or `t + π/2`) with `X' = (1 − f) cos t − (g − 1) sin t < 0`, so
`x_K|[φ, π/2−φ]` is a graph `η = c(ξ)` over `[ξ_L, ξ_R]`.  Put `U = d` (the line `d^L`) left of `ξ_L`,
`U = c` on `[ξ_L, ξ_R]`, `U = b` (the line `b^R`) right of `ξ_R`, and `Ω = {0 < η < U(ξ)}`:

* `Ω ⊆ N(K) \ H̆^R \ H̆^L` (points below `x_K(t)` are in `Q⁻_K(t)`; below the segments, in
  `Q⁻_K(φ)`, `Q⁻_K(π/2 − φ)`), and `|Ω| = ∫U⁺` (`regionBetween`);
* `J(W^R, x^R) + J(x_K) + J(x^L, Z^L) = ∫_{Z}^{ξ_L} d + ∫_{ξ_L}^{ξ_R} c + ∫_{ξ_R}^{W} b` (integration by
  parts + the substitution `ξ = X(t)`), and this is `≤ ∫_Z^W U ≤ ∫ U⁺` because `U ≤ min(b, d)`
  (Lemma 8.1.6) and `Z ≤ W` (Lemma 8.1.4) — this also covers cores that dip below the floor, which
  Baek handles with an auxiliary trapezoid.

## 34. Lemma 8.2.3, Theorem 8.2.4, Theorem 8.1.8, and the reduction of (I3) to (I3′) (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 31)

`Sofa/NicheCore.lean`, `Sofa/Main.lean`.

### 34.1 The core curve of an injective cap is a `C¹` graph

Let `K` be an injective cap (Def 6.1.2).  Then `σ_K` has no atoms in `[0, π/2)` nor in `(π/2, π]`, so
`e_K(t)` is a point (`edgeLength_eq_zero_left/right`) and the arms are continuous with `f⁺ = f⁻`
(`armFp_eq_armFm`, `continuousOn_armFp`).  Hence `x_K` is differentiable on `(0, π/2)`
(`hasDerivAt_innerCorner`):

    x_K'(t) = (1 − f_K(t)) u_t + (g_K(t) − 1) v_t.

In coordinates `X = ⟪x_K, u_0⟫`, `Y = ⟪x_K, u_{π/2}⟫`:
`X' = (1 − f) cos t − (g − 1) sin t`, `Y' = (1 − f) sin t + (g − 1) cos t` (continuous), and since
`f, g > 1` on `(0, π/2)` (Thm 6.5.6 / Def 6.1.2 (3)), **`X' < 0`** there (`coreX'_neg`).

* `curveJ_core`: for `0 < a ≤ b < π/2`,
  `J(x_K|[a,b]) = (X(b)Y(b) − X(a)Y(a))/2 − ∫_a^b Y X'`  (§31's curve functional; `cross = XY' − YX'`
  and integration by parts of the `C¹` product `XY`).
* `integral_core_graph`: `X` is strictly decreasing on `[a, b]`, `X([a, b]) = [X(b), X(a)]`, and with
  `c(ξ) := Y(X|_{[a,b]}^{-1}(ξ))` (`Function.invFunOn`) the change of variables
  (`integral_image_eq_integral_abs_deriv_smul`) gives `∫_{X(b)}^{X(a)} c = −∫_a^b Y X'`.

### 34.2 The one-dimensional inequality

`coreRoof d c b ξ_L ξ_R` is `d` on `(−∞, ξ_L]`, `c` on `(ξ_L, ξ_R)`, `b` on `[ξ_R, ∞)`.  If
`c ≤ d` on `[ξ_L, ξ_R]`, `c ≤ b` there, `b ≤ d` right of `ξ_L`, `d ≤ b` left of `ξ_R`, then the roof
`U` is `≤ d` everywhere and `≤ b` everywhere (`coreRoof_le_d/b`), and for `Z ≤ W`
(`integral_pieces_le`)

    ∫_Z^{ξ_L} d + ∫_{ξ_L}^{ξ_R} c + ∫_{ξ_R}^W b  ≤  ∫_{(Z,W)} max(U, 0).

(Split at `ξ_L`, `ξ_R`; an interval `∫_Z^{ξ_L} d` with `ξ_L > W` or `ξ_L < Z` is handled by `U ≤ d`
resp. by reversing orientation — the six orderings of `Z, W, ξ_L, ξ_R` reduce to
`∫_Z^W U ≤ ∫_Z^W U⁺`.)  And `volume_underGraph`: `|{Z < ξ < W, 0 < η < U(ξ)}| = ∫_{(Z,W)} U⁺`
(`volume_regionBetween_eq_integral` through the measure-preserving `ℝ² ≃ ℝ × ℝ`).

### 34.3 Lemma 8.2.3

Fix `0 < φ < π/4` and an injective cap `K` with `h(φ) + h(π − φ) ≥ 2` and `N(K) ⊆ K`.  Set
`b(ξ) = (h(φ) − 1 − ξ cos φ)/sin φ` (the line `b^R_K = l(φ) − u_φ`), `d(ξ) = (ξ cos φ + h(π−φ) − 1)/sin φ`
(the line `d^L_K`), `c` the core graph over `[ξ_L, ξ_R] = [X(π/2 − φ), X(φ)]`, `U` the roof,
`W = W^R_K`, `Z = Z^L_K` (the floor points of `b^R`, `d^L`).

* `core_corner_right/left`, `core_roof`: `c ≤ b`, `c ≤ d` on `[ξ_L, ξ_R]` (Lemma 8.1.6: the core lies
  below `b^R` and `d^L`), and `b ≤ d` right of `ξ_L`, `d ≤ b` left of `ξ_R` (the lines are ordered
  as at the endpoints `x_K(φ) ∈ b^R`, `x_K(π/2 − φ) ∈ d^L`); `Z ≤ W` from `h(φ) + h(π−φ) ≥ 2`.
* `core_region_subset`: `Ω := {Z < ξ < W, 0 < η < U(ξ)} ⊆ (N(K) \ H̆^R) \ H̆^L`.  A point below the
  core graph at `ξ = X(t)` lies in `Q⁻_K(t) ∩ F`; a point below the segment `b^R` right of `ξ_R` lies in
  `Q⁻_K(φ)` (so also outside `H̆^R`), and symmetrically on the left (`mem_core_region`).
* `core_J_eq`: `J(W, x_K(φ)) + J(x_K|[φ, π/2−φ]) + J(x_K(π/2−φ), Z)` equals the left side of §34.2
  (segments on a line `η = mξ + k` have `J = ½∫ (mξ + k) dξ` over the reversed range — `integral_affine`;
  the curve by §34.1).

Hence (`le_volumeReal_niche_core`)

    J(W^R, x^R) + J(x_K|[φ^R, φ^L]) + J(x^L, Z^L) ≤ |(N(K) \ H̆^R_K) \ H̆^L_K|.

**No Jordan curve and no trapezoid**: Baek encloses the region by a Jordan curve and, when the core
dips below the floor, adds an auxiliary trapezoid; here the positive part `U⁺` absorbs both.

### 34.4 Theorem 8.2.4 and Theorem 8.1.8

`IsInjectiveCap.sofaArea_le_Qfun`: for `0 < φ < π/4`, `2 + 2 sin φ < (h(0) + h(π)) cos φ` and
`N(K) ⊆ K`,

    𝒜(K) = |K| − |N(K)| ≤ 𝒬(K, B_K, D_K).

`N(K)` contains the disjoint pieces `N ∩ H̆^R`, `N ∩ H̆^L` (Lemma 8.1.4: `not_mem_right_left`) and
`(N \ H̆^R) \ H̆^L`; add Lemma 8.2.2 (both sides) and Lemma 8.2.3, and close the three collinear chains
`X_B → W^R → x^R` (on `b^R`) and `x^L → Z^L → Y_D` (on `d^L`) with `segJ_on_line` (`J` is additive along
a line through three collinear points).

The width hypothesis follows from the area: a cap lies in `[−h(π), h(0)] × [0, 1]`, so
`|K| ≤ h(0) + h(π)` (`volumeReal_le_width`), and `2 + 2 sin φ < 2.2 cos φ` for `0 < φ ≤ 1/25`
(`width_of_area`; Gerver's `φ ∈ [0.039, 0.040]`).

`InL φ K B D` is Def 8.1.3 verbatim (with `K_i` = injective caps of area `≥ 2.2`, Def 8.1.1, and
"convex body" = nonempty compact convex, as in Baek §2.1).  `IsInjectiveCap.inL` is Theorem 8.1.8
(for `N(K) ⊆ K`, see §33.4).

For a balanced maximum cap `K` (`IsBalancedMaxCap.sofaArea_le_Qfun`, `.inL`): `K` is injective
(Thm 6.1.1), `N(K) ⊆ K` (Thm 2.5.8 (2)), and `𝒜(K) ≥ 2.2` is **proved**, not assumed
(`two_point_two_le_sofaArea`, `Main.lean`): the Hammersley sofa of area `π/2 + 2/π > 2.2` is a
moving sofa of area `≥ 2.2`, so its area is `≤ 𝒜(K)` for every balanced maximum cap `K` of rotation
angle `π/2` (`volume_le_sofaArea_of_bmc`: the argument of `volume_le_gerver`, i.e. Thm 1.5.1's
reduction with the maximality of `K`).

### 34.5 The chapter interface

`Main.lean` now proves (I3) `IsBalancedMaxCap.sofaArea_le_gerver` from

    (I3′)  gerver_Qfun_le : 0 < φ_G ≤ 1/25  ∧  ∀ (K, B, D) ∈ 𝓛(φ_G),  𝒬(K, B, D) ≤ |G|,

which is exactly Baek §8.4–§8.5 (Theorem 8.4.1 on the structure of Gerver's sofa, the directional
derivative computations of §8.5, and Theorem 8.5.7), together with `0 < φ_G ≤ 1/25`
(Romik's Table 1: `φ ∈ [0.039, 0.040]`).  The derivation is three lines:

    𝒜(K) ≤ 𝒬(K, B_K, D_K) ≤ |G|        (Thm 8.2.4, then (I3′) at (K, B_K, D_K) ∈ 𝓛).

### 34.6 What is left

(I3′), i.e. §8.4–§8.5:

1. Gerver's sofa `G` through Romik's ODEs, and Theorem 8.4.1 (monotone sofa; its cap `K_G`, the
   curves `x_{K_G}`, `B`, `D`, `C` and the contact structure on the intervals `I_1, …, I_5`).
   **Baek does not prove Theorem 8.4.1** (Rem 8.4.1: "implicitly assumed in [Ger92; Rom18] … a
   rigorous symbolic verification of Theorem 8.4.1 would still be worthy"; the construction comes
   within `0.0012` of breaking near `t = π/4`), and Theorem 8.4.2 (the ten ODEs) is imported from
   [Rom18].  This also needs the upstream definition of `gerversSofa` (currently a `sorry`-backed
   existence of the constants `A, B, φ, θ` in `Upstream.lean`).
2. `(K_G, B_G, D_G) ∈ 𝓛`, which needs Theorem 6.1.2 (`K_G` injective — Baek cites [Ger92, Thm 2]).
3. §8.5: `𝒬` is a quadratic form along Minkowski segments (§32 already has the polynomial structure),
   its directional derivative at `(K_G, B_G, D_G)` is `∫ (contribution at t) ≤ 0`
   (Theorems 8.5.1–8.5.6), and with the concavity of §32 this gives the global maximum on `𝓛`
   (Theorem 8.5.7) — the "concave + non-positive one-sided derivative ⇒ maximum" step is formal.

## 35. §8.5: directional derivatives, Theorem 8.5.7 and Corollary 8.5.8 (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 32)

`Sofa/DirDerivQ.lean`, `Sofa/QMax.lean`, `Sofa/Main.lean`.

### 35.1 The mixed closed form

For compact nonempty `A`, `B` and `a ≤ b` (`integral_Ioc_supportFn_sigmaK`):

    ∫_{(a,b]} h_A dσ_B = h_A(b) e⁺_B(b) − h_A(a) e⁺_B(a) + ∫_a^b (h_A h_B − e⁺_A e⁺_B).

Proof as in §31.3 (`two_mul_curveG_eq`): integrate by parts against `σ_B = d(arcFn B)`
(`integral_Ioc_stieltjes`, `g = h_A` absolutely continuous), write `arcFn B = e⁺_B + H_B`
(`H_B = ∫_0 h_B`) and `h_A' = e⁺_A` a.e., and integrate `∫ e⁺_A H_B` by parts against `dt`.
Removing the atom `h_A(b) σ_B({b})` gives the open-interval version with `e⁻_B(b)`.

* `areaB_comm`: over a full turn the boundary terms cancel (`h`, `e⁺` are `2π`-periodic), and the
  integral is symmetric: `V(A, B) = V(B, A)` for `V(A,B) = ½∫_{S¹} h_A dσ_B`.  (Baek's Thm 8.5.1
  cites this from [Sch13, (5.19)].)
* `convB_sub_convB`: for the convex-arc form `B(A, B) = ½∫_{(a,b)} h_A dσ_B` of Lemma 7.3.3,
  `B(A,B) − B(B,A) = J(v⁻_A(b), v⁻_B(b)) − J(v⁺_A(a), v⁺_B(a))` — the identity in Baek's proof of
  Thm 8.5.2 (there via Lemma 5.1.2).

### 35.2 Quadratic expansions and linear coefficients (Thm 8.5.1–8.5.5)

Along `P_λ = c_λ(P, P')`, with `h_λ = (1−λ)h + λh'`, `e⁺_λ = (1−λ)e⁺ + λe⁺'` (`edgeMax_mix`),
`v^±_λ = (1−λ)v^± + λv^±'` (`vtxP_mix`, `vtxM_mix`), `x_{K_λ} = (1−λ)x_K + λx_{K'}`
(`innerCorner_mix`), every term `T` of `𝒬` satisfies
`T(P_λ) = (1−λ)² T(P) + λ(1−λ) b_T + λ² T(P')`:

| term | `b_T` | `b_T − 2T(P)` (the directional derivative) |
|---|---|---|
| `|K|` | `2V(K', K)` | `⟨h' − h, σ_K⟩_{S¹}` (`two_mul_areaB_sub`) |
| `J(u^{a,b}_D)` | `B(D,D') + B(D',D)` | `⟨h_{D'} − h_D, σ_D⟩_{(a,b)} + J(v⁻_D(b), v⁻_{D'}(b)) − J(v⁺_D(a), v⁺_{D'}(a))` |
| `J(p, q)` | `J(p, q') + J(p', q)` | (kept as is) |
| `J(x_K|[p,q])` | `cornerB K K'` | `∫_p^q [(h'−h)(t) i_K(t) + (h'−h)(t+π/2) i_K(t+π/2)] dt + J(x_K(q), x_{K'}(q)) − J(x_K(p), x_{K'}(p))` |

For the last row, `x_K = αu + βv` with `α = h − 1`, `β = h(·+π/2) − 1`;
`2J(x_K) = ∫(α² + β² + α e⁺(·+π/2) − β e⁺)` (`two_mul_curveJ_innerCorner`, from `two_mul_curveJ_frame`
and `h' = e⁺` a.e.), `cornerB` is its polarization, and the linear coefficient follows from
`∫(α_K β_{K'})' = [α_K β_{K'}]`, `∫(β_K α_{K'})' = [β_K α_{K'}]` (`integral_deriv_mul_eq_sub`).
The densities of Baek's `ι_K` (Def 8.4.6) are `iotaV K t = h(t) − 1 + e⁺(t+π/2) = g_K(t) − 1` and
`iotaU K t = h(t+π/2) − 1 − e⁺(t) = f_K(t) − 1`.

### 35.3 Theorem 8.5.6, with concavity

`dirQ φ K B D K' B' D'` is the sum of the linear coefficients when `X_B = x^R_K`, `Y_D = x^L_K`:

    ⟨h_{K'} − h_K, σ_K⟩_{(0,2π]} + ⟨h_{D'} − h_D, σ_D⟩_{(3π/2, 2π−φ)}
      − ∫_φ^{π/2−φ} [(h_{K'}−h_K)(t) i_K(t) + (h_{K'}−h_K)(t+π/2) i_K(t+π/2)] dt
      + ⟨h_{B'} − h_B, σ_B⟩_{(π+φ, 3π/2)}.

The segment terms telescope: with `Y_D = x^L`, `X_B = x^R`, what remains is
`J(W_B, W_{B'}) − J(Z_D, Z_{D'})`, and both vanish because `h_B(3π/2) = h_D(3π/2) = 0` (the
`LEq` equalities with `h_K(π/2) = 1`) puts all four points on the floor (`segJ_eq_zero_of_line_zero`).

`Qfun_le_add_dirQ`: `𝒬(P') ≤ 𝒬(P) + dirQ(P; P')`.  Summing the expansions,
`𝒬(P_λ) = (1−λ)²𝒬(P) + λ(1−λ)S + λ²𝒬(P')`; concavity (`Qfun_concave`) at `λ = ½` gives
`½𝒬(P) + ½𝒬(P') ≤ ¼(𝒬(P) + S + 𝒬(P'))`, i.e. `𝒬(P') ≤ 𝒬(P) + (S − 2𝒬(P))`, and
`S − 2𝒬(P) = dirQ`.  Hypotheses: both `K, K'` caps with nonempty interior, `B, B', D, D'` compact
nonempty, `LEq` for both, and the matching ends at `P`.  No convex-domain structure on `𝓛` is
needed (so no Brunn–Minkowski to keep `|K| ≥ 2.2` under Minkowski combination).

`Qfun_le_of_dirQ_nonpos` (Cor 8.5.8, abstract): for `P' ∈ 𝓛` (`InL`), `dirQ ≤ 0 ⇒ 𝒬(P') ≤ 𝒬(P)`
(`InL.interior_nonempty`: a convex set of positive area has nonempty interior).

### 35.4 Caps: `⟨f, σ_K⟩_{S¹} = ⟨f, σ_K⟩_{[0,π]}`

A Stieltjes measure vanishes on an open interval where its function is constant (right continuity
at `a`, left limit at `b`).  On `(π, 3π/2)`, `h_K = −h_K(π) cos` so `e⁺_K = h_K(π) sin`
(uniqueness of the right derivative), and `arcFn K = e⁺ + ∫_0 h` is constant there; likewise on
`(3π/2, 2π)` with `h_K = h_K(0) cos`.  Hence `σ_K((π, 3π/2)) = σ_K((3π/2, 2π)) = 0`, and for
continuous `f` with `f(3π/2) = 0`, `f(2π) = f(0)` (`IsCap.setIntegral_Ioc_zero_two_pi`):

    ∫_{(0,2π]} f dσ_K = ∫_{[0,π]} f dσ_K       (the atom at 2π is the atom at 0).

Baek states Thm 8.5.1 over `[0, π]` without this remark.

### 35.5 Theorem 8.5.7 (abstract form)

`GerverData φ θ K B D` collects what Baek's proof uses about Gerver's triple:

* `0 < φ < θ < π/4`; `K` a cap with nonempty interior; `B`, `D` compact nonempty; `LEq φ K B D`;
* Thm 8.4.3 (2): `X_B = x^R_K`, `Y_D = x^L_K`, and `σ_B((π+φ, 3π/2−θ)) = 0`,
  `σ_D((3π/2+θ, 2π−φ)) = 0` (the tails `b_B = B`, `d_D = D` are traced over `[t₃, t₅]`, `[t₀, t₂]`;
  Baek's table uses this implicitly: its `h̆_B` row has entries only under `J₄, J₅`);
* Thm 8.4.3 (3): `h_K(t) + h_B(π+t) = 1` on `[π/2−θ, π/2]`, `h_K(π/2+t) + h_D(3π/2+t) = 1` on `[0, θ]`;
* Thm 8.4.5, grouped (checked interval by interval against (1)–(8) with `J₁…J₁₀` of Def 8.4.7):

      σ_K|_{[0,π/2)} = ι_K|_{[φ, π/2−φ)} + σ̆_B|_{[π/2−θ, π/2)},
      σ_K|_{(π/2,π]} = ι_K|_{(π/2+φ, π−φ]} + σ̆_D|_{(π/2, π/2+θ]},

  with `σ̆_C = (σ_C).map (· − π)` (Def 8.4.5) and `ι_K = volume.withDensity (ofReal ∘ i_K)`;
* `i_K ≥ 0` on the core (`f_K, g_K ≥ 1`).

`GerverData.dirQ_nonpos`: for `K'` a cap and `B', D'` compact nonempty with the inequalities of `𝓛`
(`h_{K'}(t) + h_{B'}(π+t) ≤ 1` on `[φ, π/2]`, `h_{K'}(π/2+t) + h_{D'}(3π/2+t) ≤ 1` on `[0, π/2−φ]`),
`dirQ ≤ 0`.  Proof, with `Δ = h_{K'} − h_K`:
`⟨Δ, σ_K⟩_{(0,2π]} = ⟨Δ, σ_K⟩_{[0,π/2)} + Δ(π/2)σ_K({π/2}) + ⟨Δ, σ_K⟩_{(π/2,π]}` with `Δ(π/2) = 0`;
by the two identities, the `ι`-parts are exactly the `ι`-term of `dirQ`; the `σ̆_B`-part is
`∫_{[3π/2−θ, 3π/2)} Δ(s−π) dσ_B(s)`, and `⟨h_{B'} − h_B, σ_B⟩_{(π+φ, 3π/2)}` reduces to the same
interval; their sum has integrand `(h_{K'}(s−π) + h_{B'}(s)) − (h_K(s−π) + h_B(s)) ≤ 1 − 1 = 0`.
Symmetrically for `D` on `(3π/2, 3π/2+θ]`.

`GerverData.Qfun_le` (Cor 8.5.8): a triple with `GerverData` maximizes `𝒬` on `𝓛`.

### 35.6 The chapter interface

`Main.lean`: (I3′) `gerver_Qfun_le` is now derived from

    (I3″) gerver_data : φ_G ≤ 1/25 ∧ ∃ K B D, GerverData φ_G θ_G K B D ∧ 𝒬_{φ_G}(K, B, D) ≤ |G|,

i.e. Baek §8.4 (Thm 8.1.8 and Thm 8.4.3–8.4.6 for the cap of Gerver's sofa), which Baek derives from
Theorem 8.4.1 (not proved in the paper, Rem 8.4.1), Theorem 8.4.2 (Romik's ODEs, [Rom18]) and
Theorem 6.1.2 (cited from [Ger92]).

### 35.7 What is left

Only §8.4: (i) state Thm 8.4.1–8.4.2 (the boundary curves `A, B, C, D, x` of `G`, the contact
structure and Romik's ODEs) and derive Thm 8.4.3, Prop 8.4.4, Thm 8.4.5 (hence `GerverData`) and
Thm 8.4.6 (`𝒬 = A(K) = |G|`; `|N(K_G)|` by the region-under-a-graph method of §34 instead of
Green's theorem); (ii) connect with the upstream definition of `gerversSofa`; (iii) the unproved
Theorem 8.4.1 itself (and Thm 6.1.2), from Romik's explicit formulas.

## 36. Theorem 8.4.3: the tails of Gerver's niche are the bottom arcs of `B_K`, `D_K` (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 33)

`Sofa/GerverFaces.lean`, `Sofa/Main.lean`.

### 36.1 Degenerate arcs

`sigmaK_Ioc_eq_zero_of_vtxP_mem`: for a compact nonempty `C` and `a < b < a + π`, if
`⟪v⁺_C(b), u_a⟫ = h_C(a)` then `σ_C((a, b]) = 0` and `v⁺_C(b) = v⁺_C(a)`.  Indeed
`0 = ⟪v⁺(b) − v⁺(a), u_a⟫ = ∫_{(a,b]} ⟪v_s, u_a⟫ dσ(s) = ∫_{(a,b]} sin(a − s) dσ(s)` (Thm 5.2.2),
and the integrand is `< 0` on `(a, b]`, so the measure vanishes (`setIntegral_pos_iff_support_of_nonneg_ae`);
then `v⁺(b) − v⁺(a) = ∫_{(a,b]} v_s dσ = 0`.

`sigmaK_Ico_eq_zero_of_vtxM_mem` (mirror): if `⟪v⁻_C(a), u_b⟫ = h_C(b)` then `σ_C([a, b)) = 0` and
`v⁻_C(b) = v⁻_C(a)`; via `v⁻ = v⁺ − ℓ v`, the atom `ℓ(a) sin(b − a) ≥ 0` and
`∫_{(a,b)} sin(b − s) dσ(s) ≥ 0` must both vanish.

### 36.2 Curves along faces

`eq_vtx_of_mem_edge`: if `c` is continuous on `[p, q]` and `c(t) ∈ e_C(α + t)` for all `t`, then
`c(t) = v⁺_C(α + t)` on `[p, q)` and `c(t) = v⁻_C(α + t)` on `(p, q]` — from
`tendsto_of_mem_edge_right/left` (points of faces `e_C(s)`, `s → t^±`, converge to `v^±_C(t)`).

### 36.3 Theorem 8.4.3

`IsCap.gerver_B_side` (for any cap `K`, `0 < φ < θ < π/4`): let `Bc` be continuous on
`[t₃, t₅] = [π/2 − θ, π/2]` with `Bc(t) ∈ B_K`, `⟪Bc(t), u_t⟫ = h_K(t) − 1` (`b_K(t)`), and
`Bc(t₃) = x^R_K`.  Then:

* (3) `h_K(t) + h_{B_K}(π + t) = 1` on `[t₃, t₅]`: `≤` is Lemma 8.1.7 (1); `≥` because
  `⟪Bc(t), u_{π+t}⟫ = 1 − h_K(t)` and `Bc(t) ∈ B_K`;
* so `Bc(t) ∈ e_{B_K}(π + t)`, and by §36.2, (1) `Bc(t) = v⁺_{B_K}(π + t)` on `[t₃, t₅)`,
  `= v⁻_{B_K}(π + t)` on `(t₃, t₅]`;
* (2) `h_{B_K}(π + φ) = 1 − h_K(φ)` (same argument at `t = φ` with `Bc(t₃) = x^R_K ∈ B_K`), so
  `v⁺_{B_K}(π + t₃) = x^R_K` lies on the supporting line of `B_K` at `π + φ`; §36.1 gives
  `σ_{B_K}((π + φ, π + t₃]) = 0` and `X_{B_K} = v⁺_{B_K}(π + φ) = x^R_K`.

`IsCap.gerver_D_side` is the mirror statement on `[t₀, t₂] = [0, θ]` (walls `d_K(t)`,
`Dc(t₂) = x^L_K`, angles `3π/2 + t`, `Y_{D_K} = v⁻_{D_K}(2π − φ) = x^L_K`,
`σ_{D_K}([3π/2 + θ, 2π − φ)) = 0`).

(Baek's statement (2) prints "`x^R_K = X_{B_K} = D(t₃)`"; it should read `B(t₃)`.)

### 36.4 The tails lie in `B_K`, `D_K`

`GerverTails φ θ K Bc Dc` records the tail part of Thm 8.4.1 and the global facts:
`0 < φ ≤ 1/25`, `φ < θ < π/4`; `K` injective (Thm 6.1.2), `N(K) ⊆ K` (monotone sofa, Thm 2.5.9),
`|K| ≥ 2.2`; (2) interior points of the tails are in `closure N(K)` but not in `N(K)`, and
`Bc(t₃) = x^R_K`, `Dc(t₂) = x^L_K`; (3) `Bc(t) ∈ b_K(t)`, `Dc(t) ∈ d_K(t)`; (4) continuous, with
right derivatives `Bc' = β v_t` (`β < 0`), `Dc' = δ u_t` (`δ > 0`).

`GerverTails.B_mem`: `⟪Bc(t), u_φ⟫` is nondecreasing (`⟪β v_t, u_φ⟫ = β sin(φ − t) ≥ 0`,
`monotoneOn_Icc_of_rightDeriv_nonneg`), so `Bc(t) ∈ H̆^R_K`; interior points are in `K` and on or
above the floor (closure of the niche), outside `N(K)`, hence in `B_K` by Lemma 8.1.6
(`mem_Bset_of_not_mem_niche`); the endpoints follow since `B_K` is closed.  `D_mem` is the mirror
(`⟪δ u_t, u_{π−φ}⟫ = δ cos(t − π + φ) ≤ 0`, antitone, ending at `x^L_K`).

`GerverTails.gerverData`: with the two measure identities of Thm 8.4.5, all fields of
`GerverData` follow (Thm 8.1.8 via `IsInjectiveCap.inL`; `i_K = g_K − 1 > 0`,
`i_K(· + π/2) = f_K − 1 > 0` by `IsInjectiveCap.one_lt_arm`).

### 36.5 The chapter interface

`Main.lean`: (I3″) `gerver_data` is now `∃ K Bc Dc, GerverTails φ_G θ_G K Bc Dc ∧ (Thm 8.4.5) ∧
𝒬(K, B_K, D_K) ≤ |G|`.  Remaining derivations of Baek that are not yet formalized: Thm 8.4.5 (from
Romik's ODEs via Prop 8.4.4) and Thm 8.4.6 (the niche area, by Green's theorem in the paper).

## 37. Theorem 8.4.6: `𝒬(K, B_K, D_K) = A(K)` for Gerver's cap, without Green's theorem (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 34)

`Sofa/GerverArea.lean`, `Sofa/Main.lean`.

Baek's proof: "By integrating the Jordan curve boundary of `N(K)` as in (2) of Theorem 8.4.1, we
have `|N(K)| = J(x|[φ^R, φ^L]) − J(B) − J(D)`."  We need only `|N(K)| ≤ …` (the other direction
is Theorem 8.2.4) and prove it by covering `N(K)` with the three explicit regions of §33–§34.

### 37.1 The niche hypothesis

`GerverNiche φ θ K Bc Dc` extends `GerverTails` (§36.4) by the niche part of Thm 8.4.1 (2):

* `core_closure`: `x_K(t) ∈ closure N(K)` for `t ∈ [φ, π/2 − φ]`;
* `below`: every `q ∈ N(K)` lies strictly below the boundary arc `D⁻¹ · x_K · B⁻¹` at its own
  abscissa: `∃ t, D(t)₀ = q₀ ∧ q₁ < D(t)₁` (`t ∈ [0, θ]`), or the same with `x_K(t)`
  (`t ∈ [φ, π/2 − φ]`), or with `B(t)` (`t ∈ [π/2 − θ, π/2]`).

This is the "⊆" half of "`N(K)` is the region enclosed by `B⁻¹`, `x|[t₁,t₄]`, `D⁻¹` and the floor
segment": since `N(K) ∩ {η > 0}` is open and the arc is a graph over the ξ-axis, no point of `N(K)`
lies on or above the arc.  (Likewise the floor endpoints `D(0)`, `B(π/2)` are not in `N(K)`: no
half-disc around them in `{η ≥ 0}` lies in the enclosed region; points of `N(K)` on the floor are
in the open segment, where the arc is strictly above the floor.)

From `B' = βv_t` (`β < 0`), `D' = δu_t` (`δ > 0`): `B₀` is nondecreasing and `B₁` nonincreasing on
`[t₃, t₅]`; `D₀`, `D₁` are nondecreasing on `[t₀, t₂]` (`monotoneOn_inner_of_rightDeriv`).

### 37.2 The three inclusions

Write `N = N(K)`, `R = H̆^R_K = {⟪·, u_φ⟫ ≥ h(φ) − 1}`, `L = H̆^L_K`, `W = W^R_K`, `Z = Z^L_K`.

(a) `N ∩ R ⊆ conv(B_K ∪ {W}) \ B_K` (`niche_inter_right_subset`).  Take `q ∈ N ∩ R` and the point
of the arc above it.
* Below `D(t)`: `D(t)₀ ≤ x^L₀`, `D(t)₁ ≤ x^L₁`, so `⟪q, u_φ⟫ < ⟪x^L, u_φ⟫ ≤ h(φ) − 1`
  (Lemma 8.1.6: `x_K(t) ∉ int R` for `t ≥ φ`), contradicting `q ∈ R`.
* Below `x_K(t)`: `⟪q, u_φ⟫ < ⟪x_K(t), u_φ⟫ ≤ h(φ) − 1`, the same contradiction.
* Below `B(t)`: `⟪q, u_t⟫ < ⟪B(t), u_t⟫ = h(t) − 1` (`sin t > 0`), so `q ∉ B_K`.  `q` lies on the
  vertical segment from `B(t)` down to `f = (q₀, max(0, b(q₀)))` where `b` is the line `b^R_K`:
  if `q₀ ≥ W₀`, `f = (q₀, 0) ∈ [W, B(π/2)]` (`W₀ ≤ q₀ ≤ B(π/2)₀`, both on the floor); otherwise
  `f` is on `b^R_K` and in `[W, x^R]` (`x^R₀ ≤ q₀`, `f₁ ≤ x^R₁`), with `f₁ ≤ q₁` because `q ∈ R`.
  All of `W`, `B(π/2)`, `x^R = B(t₃)`, `B(t)` are in the convex hull (`mem_segment_of_line`,
  `mem_segment_of_line'`: a point of a line between two points of it, ordered by one coordinate,
  is on the segment).

(b) `N ∩ L ⊆ conv(D_K ∪ {Z}) \ D_K` (`niche_inter_left_subset`): the mirror image (below `B(t)`
or `x_K(t)` contradicts `x_K(t) ∉ int L` for `t ≤ π/2 − φ`, using `B(t)₀ ≥ x^R₀`, `B(t)₁ ≤ x^R₁`).

(c) `(N \ R) \ L ⊆ Ω ∪ {η = 0}`, `Ω = {Z₀ < ξ < W₀, 0 < η < U(ξ)}` with the roof `U = d | c | b`
of §34 (`niche_diff_subset`).  For `η > 0`: `ξ ∈ (Z₀, W₀)` and `η < d(ξ)`, `η < b(ξ)` from
`q ∉ L`, `q ∉ R`; for `ξ ∈ (ξ_L, ξ_R)`, `below` gives a point of the arc above `q`, which is not
on `D` (`D₀ ≤ x^L₀ = ξ_L`) nor on `B` (`B₀ ≥ x^R₀ = ξ_R`), so it is `x_K(s)` with
`X(s) = ξ = X(t)`, hence `s = t` (`X` strictly decreasing) and `η < Y(t) = c(ξ)`.

### 37.3 Areas

* `|conv(B_K ∪ {W}) \ B_K| = J(X_B, W) − J(b_B)` and `|conv(D_K ∪ {Z}) \ D_K| = J(Z, Y_D) − J(d_D)`
  (`volumeReal_convexHull_diff_Bset/Dset`: the equalities inside Lemma 8.2.2, §33).
* `|Ω| = ∫_{(Z₀,W₀)} U⁺` (`volume_underGraph`), and `= J(W, x^R) + J(x|[φ, π/2−φ]) + J(x^L, Z)`:
  by `core_closure`, `Y ≥ 0` on `[φ, π/2 − φ]`, so `Z₀ ≤ ξ_L`, `ξ_R ≤ W₀` (from
  `⟪x^L, u_{π−φ}⟫ = h(π−φ) − 1`, `⟪x^R, u_φ⟫ = h(φ) − 1`) and `U ≥ 0` on `(Z₀, W₀)` (`d ≥ 0` right
  of `Z₀`, `b ≥ 0` left of `W₀`, `c = Y ≥ 0`); then `∫U⁺ = ∫U = ∫d + ∫c + ∫b`
  (`integral_pieces_eq`, the equality case of `integral_pieces_le`) `= J(W, x^R) + J(x) + J(x^L, Z)`
  (`core_J_eq`, §34).
* `|{η = 0}| = 0` (`volume_floor`).

### 37.4 Theorem 8.4.6

`GerverNiche.volumeReal_niche_le`: `|N| ≤ |N ∩ R| + |N ∩ L| + |(N \ R) \ L|` and §37.2–§37.3 give
`|N| ≤ [J(X_B, W) − J(b_B)] + [J(Z, Y_D) − J(d_D)] + [J(W, x^R) + J(x) + J(x^L, Z)]`; with
`X_B = x^R`, `Y_D = x^L` (Thm 8.4.3 (2), §36.3) the segment terms cancel (`J(p, q) = −J(q, p)`):
`|N(K)| ≤ J(x|[φ, π/2−φ]) − J(b_{B_K}) − J(d_{D_K})`.

`GerverNiche.Qfun_le_sofaArea`: `𝒬(K, B_K, D_K) = |K| + J(d_D) + J(Y_D, x^L) − J(x) + J(x^R, X_B) +
J(b_B) = |K| + J(d_D) − J(x) + J(b_B) ≤ |K| − |N(K)| = A(K)`.
`GerverNiche.sofaArea_eq_Qfun`: with Theorem 8.2.4, `A(K) = 𝒬(K, B_K, D_K)`.

### 37.5 The chapter interface

`Main.lean`: (I3″) `gerver_data` is now
`∃ K Bc Dc T, GerverNiche φ_G θ_G K Bc Dc ∧ (Thm 8.4.5) ∧ IsMonotoneSofa T (π/2) ∧ C(T) = K ∧
|T| ≤ |G|`, and `𝒬(K, B_K, D_K) ≤ A(K) = |T| ≤ |G|` is derived (Thm 8.4.6 and Thm 2.5.10,
`IsMonotoneSofa.sofaArea_Ccap`).  (`N(K) ⊆ K`, a field of `GerverTails`, is now redundant: it
follows from `C(T) = K` by Thm 2.5.9.)  The only derivation of Baek inside (I3″) that is not yet
formalized is Theorem 8.4.5.

## 38. Prop 8.4.4 and Theorem 8.4.5: Romik's ODEs as measure identities (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 35)

`Sofa/GerverODE.lean`, `Sofa/Main.lean`.

### 38.1 Measures determined by subintervals

`restrict_Ioc_eq_of_Ioc`: if `μ` is locally finite and `μ((a, b]) = ν((a, b])` whenever
`p ≤ a < b ≤ q`, then `μ|_{(p,q]} = ν|_{(p,q]}` (`Measure.ext_of_Ioc`, with
`(a, b] ∩ (p, q] = (max a p, min b q]`).

`restrict_Ico_eq_of_Ioc`: if moreover only `b < q` is allowed but `μ({p}) = ν({p})`, then
`μ|_{[p,q)} = ν|_{[p,q)}`: `[p, q) = {p} ∪ ⋃ₙ (p, cₙ]` with `cₙ = q − (q − p)/(n + 2)`, and
`restrict_union_congr`, `restrict_iUnion_congr`.  (The half-open shapes matter: `σ_K` has an atom
at `π/2`, the top edge, and `σ̆_{B_K}` one at `π/2`, the bottom edge of `B_K`.)

### 38.2 Prop 8.4.4

`sigmaK_Ioc_toReal_eq_integral`: let `c = v⁺_C(α + ·)` on `[a, b]`, continuous there, with right
derivatives `c'(s)` on `(a, b)` and `⟪c'(s), v_{α+s}⟫` integrable.  Then
`σ_C((α + a, α + b]) = ∫_a^b ⟪c'(s), v_{α+s}⟫ ds`.  Indeed `σ_C((α+a, α+b]) = arcFn(α+b) − arcFn(α+a)`
with `arcFn(t) = ⟪v⁺_C(t), v_t⟫ + ∫_0^t h_C`; on `[a, b]` this is
`F(s) = ⟪c(s), v_{α+s}⟫ + ∫_0^{α+s} h_C`, continuous, with right derivative
`⟪c', v⟫ + ⟪c, −u_{α+s}⟫ + h_C(α + s) = ⟪c', v⟫` since `⟪v⁺_C(t), u_t⟫ = h_C(t)`; apply
`integral_eq_sub_of_hasDeriv_right_of_le`.

Instances (Baek's Prop 8.4.4 (1)–(4)):
* (1) `IsInjectiveCap.sigmaK_Ioc_eq_left`: `c = v⁺_K` on `[a, b] ⊆ [0, π/2)`; continuity because
  `e_K(t) = 0` there (`continuousAt_vtxP_of_edgeLength_eq_zero`: `v⁺` is right-continuous and
  `v⁺(t⁻) = v⁻(t)`).
* (3) `IsInjectiveCap.sigmaK_Ioc_eq_right`: `c = v⁺_K(π/2 + ·)` on `[a, b] ⊆ [0, π/2]`,
  `⟪c', v_{π/2+s}⟫ = ⟪−C_K', u_s⟫`; at `s = a` only right-continuity is needed.
* (2) `GerverTails.sigmaBreve_Ioc_B`: `σ̆_{B_K}((a, b]) = σ_{B_K}((a + π, b + π]) = ∫_a^b (−β)`
  on `[t₃, t₅)`, where `B' = β v_t` and `B = v⁺_{B_K}(π + ·)` (Thm 8.4.3 (1)).
* (4) `GerverTails.sigmaBreve_Ioc_D`: `σ̆_{D_K}((π/2 + a, π/2 + b]) = ∫_a^b δ` on `[0, θ]`, where
  `D' = δ u_t`, `D = v⁺_{D_K}(3π/2 + ·)` on `[0, θ)`, and at `θ` also (the atom
  `σ_{D_K}({3π/2 + θ})` vanishes by Thm 8.4.3 (2), so `v⁺ = v⁻` there).

And `ι_K((a, b]) = ∫_a^b i_K` (`iotaK_Ioc_left/right`), `i_K = g_K − 1 = ⟪x_K', v⟫ > 0` on
`(0, π/2)`, `i_K(· + π/2) = f_K − 1 = ⟪−x_K', u⟫ > 0`.

### 38.3 The ODE hypothesis

`GerverODE φ θ K Bc Dc` (Thm 8.4.1 (1) + Thm 8.4.2): `v⁺_K` and `v⁺_K(· + π/2)` are
differentiable on `(0, π/2)` minus the breakpoints `t₁ = φ, t₂ = θ, t₃ = π/2 − θ, t₄ = π/2 − φ`,
with `⟪A', v⟫`, `⟪C', u⟫` integrable on `[0, π/2]`, and on `I₁ … I₅` (open intervals)

| | `⟪A_K', v_t⟫ =` | `⟪−C_K', u_t⟫ =` |
|---|---|---|
| `I₁` | `0` | `⟪D', u_t⟫` |
| `I₂` | `⟪x_K', v_t⟫` | `⟪D' − x_K', u_t⟫` |
| `I₃` | `⟪x_K', v_t⟫` | `⟪−x_K', u_t⟫` |
| `I₄` | `⟪−B' + x_K', v_t⟫` | `⟪−x_K', u_t⟫` |
| `I₅` | `⟪−B', v_t⟫` | `0` |

with `A_K' = deriv v⁺_K`, `C_K'(t) = deriv v⁺_K (t + π/2)`, `x_K' = deriv x_K`, and `B'`, `D'` the
right derivatives `derivWithin · (Ici t) t` of the tails.

### 38.4 Theorem 8.4.5

`GerverODE.sigma_left`: on each of `J₁ = [0, φ)`, `J₂ = [φ, θ)`, `J₃ = [θ, t₃)`, `J₄ = [t₃, t₄)`,
`J₅ = [t₄, π/2)` apply §38.1 with the densities of §38.2 and the ODE: `σ_K = 0`, `ι_K`, `ι_K`,
`ι_K + σ̆_B`, `σ̆_B`.  The left-end atoms: `σ_K({t}) = e_K(t) = 0`; `ι_K({t}) = 0`;
`σ̆_B({t₃}) = σ_B({π + t₃}) = 0` (inside the null interval `(π + φ, π + t₃]` of Thm 8.4.3 (2));
`σ̆_B({t₄}) = e_{B_K}(π + t₄) = 0` since `B(t₄) = v⁺ = v⁻`.  The integrability of `β` comes from
the ODE (`−β = ⟪A', v⟫ − i_K` on `I₄`, `= ⟪A', v⟫` on `I₅`).  Gluing with `restrict_union_congr`:
`σ_K|_{[0,π/2)} = ι_K|_{[φ, π/2−φ)} + σ̆_B|_{[t₃, π/2)}`.

`GerverODE.sigma_right`: on `(π/2, π/2 + φ]`, `(π/2 + φ, π/2 + θ]`, `(π/2 + θ, π − θ]`,
`(π − θ, π − φ]`, `(π − φ, π]` (Baek's `J₆, …, J₁₀`): `σ_K = σ̆_D`, `ι_K + σ̆_D`, `ι_K`, `ι_K`, `0`;
no atom conditions are needed for `(p, q]`-pieces.  Result:
`σ_K|_{(π/2,π]} = ι_K|_{(π/2+φ, π−φ]} + σ̆_D|_{(π/2, π/2+θ]}`.

`GerverODE.gerverData`: with `GerverTails`, this gives `GerverData` (§36.4).

### 38.5 The chapter interface

`Main.lean`: (I3″) `gerver_data` is now
`∃ K Bc Dc T, GerverNiche φ_G θ_G K Bc Dc ∧ GerverODE φ_G θ_G K Bc Dc ∧ IsMonotoneSofa T (π/2) ∧
C(T) = K ∧ |T| ≤ |G|`.  Every derivation of Baek's is formalized; (I3″) consists of Thm 8.4.1
(not proved in the paper, Rem 8.4.1), Thm 8.4.2 (imported from [Rom18]), Thm 6.1.2 (imported
from [Ger92]), the numerical facts `|G| ≥ 2.2`, `φ ≤ 1/25`, and the identification of the
upstream `gerversSofa` with `T`.

## 39. Gerver's constants: bounds by verified interval arithmetic (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 36)

`Sofa/IntervalArith.lean`, `Sofa/GerverConst.lean`, `Sofa/GerverFaces.lean` (`GerverAngles`),
`Sofa/Main.lean`.

### 39.1 The system

Upstream (`MovingSofa.GerversSofa.ABφθSpec`, Romik 2018 eq. 1–4): `0 ≤ φ ≤ θ ≤ π/4`, `A, B ≥ 0` and

    E₁: A(cos θ − cos φ) − 2B sin φ + (θ − φ − 1) cos θ − sin θ + cos φ + sin φ = 0
    E₂: A(3 sin θ + sin φ) − 2B cos φ + 3(θ − φ − 1) sin θ + 3 cos θ − sin φ + cos φ = 0
    E₃: A cos φ − (sin φ + ½ − ½ cos φ + B sin φ) = 0
    E₄: (A + π/2 − φ − θ) − (B − (θ − φ)(1 + A)/2 − (θ − φ)²/4) = 0.

The numerical solution is `φ ≈ 0.0391774`, `θ ≈ 0.6813015`, `A ≈ 0.0944266`, `B ≈ 1.3992037`.

### 39.2 Elimination

The equations are linear in `A, B`.  With `d = θ − φ`, `κ = 1 + d/2`,
`c₀ = π/2 − φ − θ + d/2 + d²/4` (so `E₄ ⟺ B = Aκ + c₀`), `N₁ = d cos θ + 3 sin φ − sin θ − cos θ + 1`
and `den = 3 cos φ − cos θ > 0`: `E₁ − 2E₃ = N₁ − A·den`, and

    Q̃ := N₁(cos φ − sin φ κ) − den (sin φ + ½ − ½ cos φ + sin φ c₀)
       = den (E₃ − sin φ E₄) + (cos φ − sin φ κ)(E₁ − 2E₃),
    R̃ := N₁(3 sin θ + sin φ − 2 cos φ κ) + den (3(d − 1) sin θ + 3 cos θ − sin φ + cos φ − 2 cos φ c₀)
       = den (E₂ − 2 cos φ E₄) + (3 sin θ + sin φ − 2 cos φ κ)(E₁ − 2E₃)

(`QE_RE_eq_zero`, by `linear_combination`).  So every solution has `Q̃ = R̃ = 0`.

### 39.3 Verified interval arithmetic

* `taylor_alt`: for `x ≥ 0` and all `m`, `(−1)^m (cos x − c_{m+1}(x)) ≤ 0` and
  `(−1)^m (sin x − s_{m+1}(x)) ≤ 0` (`s_n`, `c_n`: Taylor partial sums with `n` terms), by
  induction: `(C_m) ⇒ (S_m)` and `(S_m) ⇒ (C_{m+1})`, each by "vanishing at 0 with derivative
  `≤ 0` on `[0, ∞)`".  Hence `s₆ ≤ sin ≤ s₇`, `c₆ ≤ cos ≤ c₇` on `[0, ∞)`.
* `Iv`: intervals `[lo/S, hi/S]`, `lo, hi ∈ ℤ`, `S = 2⁴⁰`.  Addition exact; multiplication by the
  corner products, rounded outward (`Int` division); `mul_mem_corners` (bilinearity).
  `Iv.sin`, `Iv.cos` for intervals inside `[0, 1]`: monotonicity and the Taylor bounds; the Taylor
  polynomials are themselves evaluated by interval Horner schemes (`sin6E`, …), so the whole
  computation uses only integer arithmetic.
* `IE`: polynomial expressions with rational constants; `IE.mem_ieval` (structural induction).

### 39.4 Branch and bound

`check T d fl fh tl th` is `true` if the box `[fl, fh] × [tl, th]` (in units of `2⁻⁴⁰`) lies strictly
below the diagonal, lies inside the target `T`, or `Q̃` or `R̃` (evaluated in `IE` with variables
`φ, θ, π, sin φ, cos φ, sin θ, cos θ`) has an interval value not containing `0`; otherwise it
splits the box into four (while `d > 0`).  `check_sound`: if `check T d … = true`, every point of
the box with `φ ≤ θ` and `Q̃ = R̃ = 0` lies in `T` (induction on `d`).

The computation `check target 16 0 top 0 top = true` (`top/S ≥ 0.8`,
`target = [0.03, 0.0399] × [0.5, 0.78]`) is checked by the kernel with `decide +kernel`.  It visits
2185 boxes; checked in one piece it exceeds 7 GB of memory (the kernel caches every intermediate
term), so it is split, following a Python simulation, into 52 pieces of at most 150 boxes, glued
by `check_succ_of`.  Time: about 80 s.

### 39.5 Consequences

`ABφθSpec_mem_target`: every solution has `φ ∈ [0.03, 0.0399]`, `θ ∈ [0.5, 0.78]` (up to the
rounding `2⁻⁴⁰`).  `ABφθSpec_angles`: `0 < φ ≤ 1/25`, `φ < θ < π/4`.  `gerver_angles`: the same for
the upstream constants, via `ABφθSpec.existsUnique.choose_spec` (hence with the upstream `sorry`).

`GerverAngles φ θ` (the four inequalities) is now a separate structure extended by `GerverTails`;
(I3″) assumes it as a hypothesis (`GerverAngles φ_G θ_G → GerverNiche … ∧ GerverODE …`) and
`gerver_Qfun_le` discharges it with `gerver_angles`.

## 40. Existence and uniqueness of Gerver's constants (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 37)

`Sofa/IntervalArith.lean` (derivatives), `Sofa/GerverConst.lean` (rewritten), `Sofa/GerverUnique.lean`
(new), `Sofa/Upstream.lean` (one change), `Sofa/GerverSpec.lean` (new).

### 40.1 Statement

`spec_existsUnique : ∃! (A, B, φ, θ), Spec A B φ θ`, where `Spec` is a verbatim copy of the upstream
`ABφθSpec` (definitionally equal).  The upstream `ABφθSpec.existsUnique` is now proved by it, so the
upstream constants `A, B, φ, θ` and `gerversSofa` no longer depend on `sorryAx`.  Moreover every
solution satisfies `|φ − φ₀|, |θ − θ₀| ≤ 2⁻³⁰` with `φ₀ = 43075968132/2⁴⁰ ≈ 0.0391773648`,
`θ₀ = 749098931588/2⁴⁰ ≈ 0.6813015094` (`Spec_mem_tbox`), hence `0 < φ ≤ 1/25`, `φ < θ < π/4`
(`Spec_angles`).

### 40.2 Elimination, both directions

The identities of §39.2 hold for **all** `A, B` (`QE_eval`, `RE_eval`, `eq1_sub_eq3`, `eq4_eq`, by
`ring`).  Necessity: a solution has `Q̃ = R̃ = 0`.  Sufficiency (`spec_of_zero`): if `Q̃ = R̃ = 0` at
`(φ, θ)` with `den, N₁, κ, c₀ > 0`, then `A := N₁/den`, `B := Aκ + c₀` give `E₁ − 2E₃ = 0`, `E₄ = 0`,
so `Q̃ = den·E₃` and `R̃ = den·E₂`, whence `E₃ = E₂ = 0` and `E₁ = 0`; and `A, B ≥ 0`.

### 40.3 Localization

The branch and bound of §39.4 with the target `[φ₀ ± 2⁻⁹] × [θ₀ ± 2⁻⁹]` and `π` enclosed by Mathlib's
20-digit bounds (`pi_gt_d20`, `pi_lt_d20`): 4481 boxes, 106 kernel-checked pieces
(`Spec_mem_target`).  Memory: Lean's asynchronous elaboration keeps the memory of finished kernel
checks until the end of the file (≈ 1 MB per box); `set_option Elab.async false` runs them one after
the other (peak ≈ 4 GB).

### 40.4 Derivatives of expressions

* `IE.evalD env δ e`: forward-mode derivative of `e.eval` when the variables have derivatives `δ`.
* `IE.hasDerivAt_eval`: if `s ↦ ρ(s)(i)` has derivative `δ i` at `t` for every `i`, then
  `s ↦ e.eval (ρ s)` has derivative `e.evalD (ρ t) δ` (structural induction: `add`, `mul`, …).
* `IE.evalD_lin`: `evalD` is linear in `δ`.
* `IE.diff d e`: the derivative as an expression, `d i` the derivative of variable `i`;
