  `IE.eval_diff : (e.diff d).eval env = e.evalD env (fun i => (d i).eval env)`.

With `dφ = (1, 0, 0, cos φ, −sin φ, 0, 0)`, `dθ = (0, 1, 0, 0, 0, cos θ, −sin θ)` (variables
`φ, θ, π, sin φ, cos φ, sin θ, cos θ`), along the segment `y + τ(x − y)` every variable has derivative
`(x₁ − y₁)·dφ + (x₂ − y₂)·dθ` (`hasDerivAt_lineEnv`), so the one-variable mean value theorem gives
(`IE_mvt`)

    e(x) − e(y) = (x₁ − y₁)·∂φe(z) + (x₂ − y₂)·∂θe(z),   z on the segment,

with `∂φe = e.diff dφ`, `∂θe = e.diff dθ` — expressions that the same interval arithmetic encloses.

### 40.5 The contraction (simplified Newton / Krawczyk)

`F = (Q̃, R̃)`, `G = P F` with `P = [[−0.336, −0.063], [−0.245, −1.161]] ≈ F'(x*)⁻¹`
(`F'(x*) ≈ [[−3.0988, 0.1687], [0.6546, −0.8968]]`), `T(x) = x − G(x)` (`Tmap`).

* `contrOK_target`: on the target box, `|1 − ∂φG₁| + |∂θG₁| ≤ ½` and `|∂φG₂| + |1 − ∂θG₂| ≤ ½`
  (interval values: 0.035 and 0.24).  With `IE_mvt`: `|T_i(x) − T_i(y)| ≤ ½ max_j |x_j − y_j|`
  (`lip_of_contrOK`).
* `mapOK_true`: at the center `c = (φ₀, θ₀)`, `|G_i(c)| ≤ 2⁻³¹` (the check is `2·mag ≤ 1024` in
  units of `2⁻⁴⁰`; the interval values are 22 and 246 units of `2⁻⁴⁰`).  So `T` maps
  `tbox = [c ± 2⁻³⁰]²` into itself: `|T(x) − c| ≤ |T(x) − T(c)| + |G(c)| ≤ ½·2⁻³⁰ + ½·2⁻³⁰`
  (`maps_tbox`).
* `exists_fixed`: Banach (`ContractingWith.exists_fixedPoint'` on the complete set `tbox`).
* `fixed_unique`: two fixed points in the target box coincide (`d ≤ d/2`).
* A zero of `F` is a fixed point and conversely (`det P ≠ 0`).

### 40.6 Assembly

Existence: the fixed point `x ∈ tbox` is a zero of `F`; `spec_of_zero` (positivity of `den, N₁, κ,
c₀` on the target box by one interval evaluation, `posOK_true`) gives a solution.  Uniqueness: any
solution lies in the target box (§40.3) and is a zero of `F`, hence equals `x`; then `A, B` are
determined by `E₁ − 2E₃` and `E₄`.

### 40.7 The upstream file

`Sofa/Upstream.lean` imports `Sofa.GerverUnique` (whose dependencies are Mathlib only) and proves
`ABφθSpec.existsUnique` by `Sofa.GC.spec_existsUnique`; nothing else changed.  Consequently
`IntervalArith`, `GerverConst`, `GerverUnique` precede the upstream file in the build, and changing
them rebuilds everything; later interval tools go into new files.

## 41. Gerver's sofa is a moving sofa (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 38)

`Sofa/GerverPath.lean`, `Sofa/GerverClosed.lean`, `Sofa/GerverConn.lean` (all new).

### 41.1 The upstream definition

`gerversSofa = H₀ ∩ H₁ ∩ ⋂_{α ∈ [0, π/2]} R_α(L + p(α))`, where `L` is the hallway,
`H₀ = R_0(L_h + p(0))`, `H₁ = R_{π/2}(L_v + p(π/2))`, and Gerver's path `p` is built from the radius
function `r` (piecewise, with jumps at `φ` and `θ`) and its integrals
`x(α) = 1 − ∫_α^{π/2−φ} r cos`, `y(α) = ∫_α^{π/2−φ} r sin`:

    p₁(α) = cos α − 1                               (α ≤ φ)
          = x(π/2 − α) cos α + y(π/2 − α) sin α − 1  (α > φ)
    p₂(α) = y(α) cos α − (4x(0) − 2 − x(α)) sin α − 1 (α ≤ π/2 − φ)
          = −(4x(0) − 3) sin α − 1                   (α > π/2 − φ).

A numerical rasterization of this definition (outside Lean) gives area ≈ 2.2197–2.2198, which
agrees with Gerver's constant 2.21953… to within the discretization error: the upstream definition
describes Gerver's sofa.

### 41.2 First facts (`GerverPath.lean`)

* Enclosures of the constants: `φ, θ` to `2⁻³⁰` (§40), and `A ∈ [0.0944265581, 0.0944265636]`,
  `B ∈ [1.3992037202, 1.3992037344]` (`A_mem`, `B_mem`), from `A = N₁/(3cos φ − cos θ)`,
  `B = Aκ + c₀` and one interval evaluation on the tight box.
* `0 ≤ r ≤ 3/2`, `r` measurable, so `r` is interval integrable; `x`, `y`, `p` are continuous (the
  branches of `p` agree at `φ` and `π/2 − φ` because `x(π/2 − φ) = 1`, `y(π/2 − φ) = 0`).
* **`y(0) = 1`**: integrating the four polynomial pieces in closed form,
  `y(0) − 1 = E₃ − sin θ · E₄ = 0`.  Hence **`p(0) = 0`**.
* The motion `gmotion τ = (rotateTranslate α (p α))⁻¹`, `α = πτ/2`, is continuous (`p` is) and
  `gmotion 0 = id` (`p(0) = 0`).  Every condition of `IsMovingSofa` except connectedness is then
  immediate from the definition (`isMovingSofa_gerversSofa_of_isConnected`).

### 41.3 Closed forms (`GerverClosed.lean`)

On the pieces `[brk k, brk (k+1)]` (`brk = 0, φ, θ, π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ, π/2`) `r` is a polynomial
`ra k + rb k t + rc k t²`, so with the antiderivatives `Fs`, `Fc` of `(a + bt + ct²) sin t`,
`(a + bt + ct²) cos t`:

    y(β) = Fs_k(brk(k+1)) − Fs_k(β) + Σ_{j>k} [Fs_j]_{brk j}^{brk(j+1)}     (β in piece k),

and similarly for `x` (`y_eq_yC`, `x_eq_xC`).  Hence on each of the five pieces of `[0, π/2]` both
coordinates of `p` are explicit expressions in `α, sin α, cos α` and the constants
(`p₁_eq_closed`, `p₂_eq_closed`; the reflection `α ↦ π/2 − α` maps piece `j` to piece `4 − j`).

### 41.4 Connectedness (`GerverConn.lean`)

In the frame of the hallway at angle `α`, `z` has coordinates `ca α z = ⟪z, u_α⟫ − p₁(α)`,
`cb α z = ⟪z, v_α⟫ − p₂(α)`; `z ∈ R_α(L + p(α))` iff `ca, cb ≤ 1` and not (`ca < 0` and `cb < 0`).
So (`mem_gerversSofa_iff`)

    gerversSofa = Cvx ∖ ⋃_α Q⁻_α,
    Cvx = {x ≤ 1, 0 ≤ y ≤ 1, x ≥ 4x(0) − 3} ∩ ⋂_α {ca ≤ 1, cb ≤ 1}   (convex).

Since `⟪e₂, u_α⟫ = sin α ≥ 0` and `⟪e₂, v_α⟫ = cos α ≥ 0`, moving a point upwards never enters an
inner quadrant `Q⁻_α` (`up_mem`).  Four facts about `p`, for all `α ∈ [0, π/2]`:

* (Q1) `p₁ sin α + p₂ cos α < 9/10`: the inner corner `R_α p(α)` is below height `9/10`, and
  `Q⁻_α ⊆ {y < p₁ sin α + p₂ cos α}` (because `y = sin α ⟪z, u_α⟫ + cos α ⟪z, v_α⟫`).
  So `Cvx ∩ {y ≥ 9/10}` lies in the sofa (`mem_of_high`).
* (Q2) `p₂ ≤ (8/5) sin α` and `p₁ ≤ (7/20) cos α`: for `y ≥ 0`, `x ≤ −8/5` (resp. `x ≥ 7/20`)
  the point is not in any `Q⁻_α` (`mem_of_left`, `mem_of_right`; no division needed).
* (Q3) `(−8/5, 9/10)`, `(7/20, 9/10) ∈ Cvx` (hence the whole segment between them).

Then, for a point `z` of the sofa: if `z_y ≥ 9/10`, `z` is in the convex set `T = Cvx ∩ {y ≥ 9/10}`;
if `z_x ≤ −8/5` (resp. `≥ 7/20`) it is in the convex left (right) end piece, which meets `T`;
otherwise the vertical segment from `z` to `(z_x, 9/10) ∈ T` lies in the sofa.  So every point is
joined to `(7/20, 9/10)` inside the sofa (`isPreconnected_of_forall`): **the sofa is connected**
(`isConnected_gerversSofa`), hence **a moving sofa** (`isMovingSofa_gerversSofa'`).

### 41.5 The verified computations

(Q1)–(Q3) are seven inequalities `F(α) < 0`.  With the closed forms written as interval expressions
`IE` in the twelve variables `α, sin α, cos α, φ, θ, π, sin φ, cos φ, sin θ, cos θ, A, B`
(`p1E`, `p2E`, `eval` lemmas `p1E_eval`, `p2E_eval`), a bisection `chkA` over `α ∈ [0, π/2]`
discards a box when, for every piece it may meet (the break points are known to `2⁻³⁰`), the
interval value of the piece's expression is negative (`chkA_sound`).  `sin`, `cos` on `[0, 2]`:
`sinW` (concavity on `[0, π]` for the lower bound, monotonicity below `1.57` for the upper bound)
and `cosW` (monotone on `[0, π]`).  Kernel time 1–18 s per inequality; in all about 60 s.

Near `α = 0` (Q2, left) and `α = π/2` (Q2, right) the inequalities are tight (equality at the end
points) and are proved analytically on `[0, 1/10]` and `[π/2 − 1/10, π/2]`:
`y ≤ 1` (`y(0) = 1` and `r sin ≥ 0`) and `x(α) ≤ x(0) + (3/2) α` (`0 ≤ r ≤ 3/2`), with
`x(0) ∈ [0.19, 0.2]` (one interval evaluation).

### 41.6 The upstream statement

The upstream `isMovingSofa_gerversSofa` is stated in the upstream file, before anything that could
prove it (the proof needs the upstream definitions and our library, which imports the upstream file),
so it keeps its `sorry`; the proof is `Sofa.GP.isMovingSofa_gerversSofa'`, and
`Sofa.sofaConstant_eq_volume_gerversSofa'` now uses it.

## 42. Gerver's sofa is a monotone sofa (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 38)

`Sofa/GerverMono.lean` (new); `Sofa/Main.lean` ((I3″) restated).

### 42.1 The outer boundary curve

`x(β) = 1 − ∫_β^{π/2−φ} r cos`, `y(β) = ∫_β^{π/2−φ} r sin` (§41.1), with `r = 0` on `(π/2 − φ, π/2]`,
so `x = 1`, `y = 0` there.  On `[0, π/2]`: `x` increases from `x(0)` to `1`, `y` decreases from
`y(0) = 1` to `0` (`x_mono`, `y_anti`, `x_range`, `y_range`), and both branches of `p` are one formula
(`p₁_uniform`, `p₂_uniform`):

    p₁(γ) = x(π/2 − γ) cos γ + y(π/2 − γ) sin γ − 1,
    p₂(γ) = y(γ) cos γ − (4x(0) − 2 − x(γ)) sin γ − 1.

**Convexity** (`conv_ineq`): for `a, b ∈ [0, π/2]`,

    (x a − x b) sin b + (y a − y b) cos b = ∫_b^a r(t) sin(b − t) dt ≤ 0.

### 42.2 The contact points

`A(α) = (x(π/2 − α), y(π/2 − α))` and `C(α) = (4x(0) − 2 − x(α), y(α))` satisfy `ca α A(α) = 1`,
`cb α C(α) = 1` (`ca_ptA`, `cb_ptC`), and for all `γ ∈ [0, π/2]`:

* `ca γ A(α) ≤ 1`, `cb γ C(α) ≤ 1`: `conv_ineq` (at the reflected angles for `A`);
* `cb γ A(α) ≤ 1`, `ca γ C(α) ≤ 1`: from `1 − y(a) ≤ (3/2)(1 − cos a)` (`r ≤ 3/2`),
  `1 − cos γ ≤ sin γ`, `x(0) ≤ 1/5` and the ranges of `x`, `y`.

So `A(α), C(α) ∈ Cvx`.  They are not in any inner quadrant because each boundary point
`(x(a), y(a))` is high (`y > 9/10`, `a ≤ 9/20`) or far (`x > 2/5`, `a ≥ 9/20`) (`high_or_far`, two
interval checks with `chkA`), and §41.4 (`mem_of_high`, `mem_of_right`, `mem_of_left`; for `C`,
`x > 2/5` gives `4x(0) − 2 − x < −8/5`).  Hence `A(α), C(α) ∈ G` (`ptA_mem`, `ptC_mem`).

### 42.3 Support function, supporting hallways, monotone hull

`G` is compact (closed, inside `[4x(0) − 3, 1] × [0, 1]`).  For `α ∈ [0, π/2]`:

    h_G(α) = p₁(α) + 1,   h_G(α + π/2) = p₂(α) + 1     (supportFn_gerver, supportFn_gerver')

(`≤`: every point of `G ⊆ Cvx` has `ca, cb ≤ 1`; `≥`: the contact points).  So the supporting
hallway `L_G(α)` is the hallway `R_α(L + p(α))` of the definition (`suppHallway_gerver`), and

    I(G) = P(π/2) ∩ ⋂_{α ∈ [0, π/2]} L_G(α) = G                          (Imono_gerver)

since `P(π/2) = {0 ≤ y ≤ 1}`, `ca 0 z = z₀` (the right wall `x ≤ 1`) and
`cb (π/2) z = −z₀ − p₂(π/2)` (the left wall `x ≥ 4x(0) − 3`).  With the motion of §41.2
(`IsSofaWithAngle` with angle function `−πτ/2`) and `h_G(π/2) = p₁(π/2) + 1 = 1` (standard
position), **`G` is a monotone sofa of rotation angle `π/2`** (`isMonotoneSofa_gerversSofa`).

### 42.4 (I3″) restated

With `T = G`: `C(G)` is a cap containing its niche (Thm 2.5.9, `niche_Ccap_gerversSofa_subset`) and
`A(C(G)) = |G|` (Thm 2.5.10, `ofReal_sofaArea_Ccap_gerversSofa`).  (I3″) is now

    ∃ B D, GerverNicheFacts φ θ (C(G)) B D ∧ GerverODE φ θ (C(G)) B D,

where `GerverNicheFacts` is `GerverNiche` without the proved fields `GerverAngles` and
`N(K) ⊆ K`.  `C(G)` coincides with `Cvx` (§41.4).

### 42.5 Outlook: what (I3″) asks for `K = C(G)`

* `|K| ≥ 2.2`: `K` is convex and contains `A(0) = (1, 0)`, `A(π/2) = (x₀, 1)`, `C(0) = (3x₀ − 2, 1)`,
  `C(π/2) = (4x₀ − 3, 0)`, hence the trapezoid with these vertices, of area `3 − 3x₀ ≥ 2.4`.
* `GerverODE`: for `t ∈ (0, π/2)` the face of `K` in direction `u_t` is `{A(t)}` (`A` is continuous and
  touches every supporting line), so `v⁺_K(t) = A(t)`, `v⁺_K(t + π/2) = C(t)`, and
  `x_K(t) = p₁ u_t + p₂ v_t`.  `A' = r(π/2 − t) v_t`, `C' = −r(t) u_t`,
  `x_K' = (p₁' − p₂) u_t + (p₁ + p₂') v_t`, and `p₁'' + p₁ + 1 = r(π/2 − t)`,
  `p₂'' + p₂ + 1 = r(t)`.  The ten ODEs reduce to `r = 1/2` on `(0, φ)`, `r = 0` on
  `(π/2 − φ, π/2)` and two identities for
  `F(t) = [y(t) − y(π/2 − t)] cos t + [x(t) + x(π/2 − t) + 2 − 4x₀] sin t − 1`:
  `F = r` on `(θ, π/2 − φ)` and `F = 2r − 1` on `(φ, θ)` (checked numerically to `10⁻⁴⁰`).
  In closed form: `−Fs_k(t) cos t + Fc_k(t) sin t = P_k(t) − 2c_k` and
  `Fs_k(s) sin s + Fc_k(s) cos s = P_k'(s)` (`P_k` the polynomial of `r` on piece `k`, `c_k` its
  `t²`-coefficient), so on each piece `F − r` (resp. `F − 2r + 1`) is `c₁ cos t + c₂ sin t` with
  constants `c₁`, `c₂`; sympy gives `c₂ = −E₂ + 2 cos θ · E₄` on `[θ, π/2 − θ]` (`c₁ = 0` there),
  `c₁ = sin θ · E₄`, `c₂ = −E₂ + 3 cos θ · E₄` on `[π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ]`, and
  `c₁ = −sin θ · E₄`, `c₂ = −E₂ + 3 cos θ · E₄` on `[φ, θ]` — so the identities follow from
  Romik's equations by `linear_combination`.
* Tails: `B(t) = p₁ u_t + p₁' v_t`, `D(t) = −p₂' u_t + p₂ v_t` (envelopes of the inner walls),
  `B' = (r(π/2 − t) − 1) v_t`, `D' = (1 − r(t)) u_t`.

## 43. The cap of Gerver's sofa: `|K| ≥ 2.2`, Romik's ODEs, the tails (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 39)

`Sofa/GerverCap.lean`, `Sofa/GerverRomik.lean`, `Sofa/GerverEnds.lean`, `Sofa/GerverTailDeriv.lean`
(new); `Sofa/Main.lean` ((I3″) reduced).

### 43.1 The cap `K = C(G)`

`K = Ccap gerversSofa (π/2)` (`Kg`) is a cap (Thm 2.4.1 for the monotone sofa `G`, §42), and by
`supportFn_eq_of_subset_Ccap` its support function on `J = [0, π]` is that of `G` (§42.3):

    h_K(α) = p₁(α) + 1,   h_K(α + π/2) = p₂(α) + 1,   x_K(α) = p₁(α) u_α + p₂(α) v_α   (α ∈ [0, π/2]).

Moreover `K = Cvx` (`Ccap_gerver`): `P(π/2) = {0 ≤ y ≤ 1}`, and the outer walls at `α = 0` and
`α = π/2` are the right and left walls of `Cvx`.

### 43.2 `|K| ≥ 2.2`

The contact points `C(π/2) = (4x₀ − 3, 0)`, `A(0) = (1, 0)`, `A(π/2) = (x₀, 1)`, `C(0) = (3x₀ − 2, 1)`
(`x₀ = x(0)`) lie in `Cvx` (§42.2), which is convex, so the trapezoid
`{0 < y < 1, 4x₀ − 3 + (1 − x₀) y < x < 1 − (1 − x₀) y}` lies in `K` (`pt_mem_Cvx_of_trap`).
Its area is `∫₀¹ (4 − 4x₀ − (2 − 2x₀) y) dy = 3 − 3x₀ ≥ 2.4` (`volume_trap`: `regionBetween` in the
swapped coordinates `(y, x)`, `Measure.measurePreserving_swap`).  Hence `11/5 ≤ |K|` (`area_Kg`).

### 43.3 Calculus of Gerver's path

Away from the break points `r` is continuous (`continuousAt_r_of_good`, `good` =
`(0, π/2) ∖ {φ, θ, π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ}`, open and symmetric under `t ↦ π/2 − t`), so by the fundamental
theorem of calculus `x' = r cos`, `y' = −r sin`, and with `p₁'(t) = −x(π/2 − t) sin t + y(π/2 − t) cos t`,
`p₂'(t) = −y(t) sin t − (4x₀ − 2 − x(t)) cos t` (`dp₁`, `dp₂`; the terms with `r` cancel):

    p₁'' = r(π/2 − t) − p₁ − 1,   p₂'' = r(t) − p₂ − 1.

### 43.4 Vertex curves and inner corner

`edgeMax K t` is the right derivative of `h_K` at `t` (`hasDerivWithinAt_supportFn_Ioi`), so for
`t ∈ good`: `edgeMax K t = p₁'(t)`, `edgeMax K (t + π/2) = p₂'(t)`, and

    v⁺_K(t) = (p₁ + 1) u_t + p₁' v_t = (x(π/2 − t), y(π/2 − t)) = A(t),
    v⁺_K(t + π/2) = (p₂ + 1) v_t − p₂' u_t = (4x₀ − 2 − x(t), y(t)) = C(t)        (vtxP_Kg, vtxP_Kg_add)

with `A' = r(π/2 − t) v_t`, `C' = −r(t) u_t` and `x_K' = (p₁' − p₂) u_t + (p₁ + p₂') v_t`.

### 43.5 The tails

`B = p₁ u + p₁' v` and `D = −p₂' u + p₂ v` (`Bg`, `Dg`) are the envelopes of the inner walls
`⟪z, u_t⟫ = h_K(t) − 1` and `⟪z, v_t⟫ = h_K(t + π/2) − 1` (`Bg_wall`, `Dg_wall`); they are continuous and
`B' = (r(π/2 − t) − 1) v_t`, `D' = (1 − r(t)) u_t` on `good`.

* **End points** (`Bg_start`, `Dg_end`): `B(π/2 − θ) = x_K(φ)`, `D(θ) = x_K(π/2 − φ)`.  In closed form
  these are identities between values of `x`, `y` at the break points.  They hold on the "relaxed"
  variety (treat `sin φ, cos φ, sin θ, cos θ, φ, θ, π, A, B` as independent variables subject to
  `E₁, …, E₄` and the two Pythagorean relations; checked numerically at random points), and sympy
  finds certificates for the components along `u_φ`, `v_φ` (resp. `u_{π/2−φ}`, `v_{π/2−φ}`):
  `(sin φ/2) E₁ + (cos φ/2) E₂ − cos(θ − φ) E₄ + …` and `(cos φ/2) E₁ + (sin φ/2) E₂ − 2 cos θ sin φ E₄ + …`
  plus multiples of `sin² + cos² − 1` (`vec_eq_of_proj`: two components in an orthonormal frame).
* **One-sided derivatives at the break points** (`Bg_deriv`, `Dg_deriv`, Thm 8.4.1 (4)): `r` is
  continuous from the left (`continuousWithinAt_r_Iic`), so the right derivative of `B` at `t` is
  `(r(π/2 − t) − 1) v_t` with `r(π/2 − t) < 1` for `t ∈ [π/2 − θ, π/2)`; the right derivative of `D`
  at `t ∈ [0, θ)` is `(1 − r⁺(t)) u_t` with the right limit `r⁺` (`rR`; `r⁺(φ) = (1 + A)/2`).  Both by
  `hasDerivWithinAt_Ici_of_tendsto_deriv` on an interval `(t, c)` free of break points.

### 43.6 Romik's identities

With `F = p₂ − p₁' = [y(t) − y(π/2 − t)] cos t + [x(t) + x(π/2 − t) + 2 − 4x₀] sin t − 1` and
`p₁ + p₂' = F(π/2 − ·)`, the ten ODEs of `GerverODE` become

* `r(π/2 − t) = 0` on `(0, φ)` (v1), `r(t) = 0` on `(π/2 − φ, π/2)` (u5), `r = 1/2` (u1, v5);
* `F = r` on `(θ, π/2 − φ]` (u3, u4, and v2, v3 at `π/2 − t`), `F = 2r − 1` on `(φ, θ]` (u2, and v4).

On each piece the closed forms give `−Fs_k(t) cos t + Fc_k(t) sin t = P_k(t) − 2c_k` and
`Fs_k(s) sin s + Fc_k(s) cos s = P_k'(s)`, so `F − target = c₁ cos t + c₂ sin t` with constants
`c₁, c₂`, and sympy expresses them through Romik's equations: on `[θ, π/2 − θ]` the certificate is
`−sin t · E₂ + 2 cos θ sin t · E₄ + (A − φ + t + 1)(sin² t + cos² t − 1)`; on `[π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ]`
`−sin t · E₂ + (sin θ cos t + 3 cos θ sin t) · E₄ + q(sin² t + cos² t − 1)`; on `[φ, θ]`
`−sin t · E₂ + (−sin θ cos t + 3 cos θ sin t) · E₄ + (A − φ + t + 1)(sin² t + cos² t − 1)`
(`F_piece2`, `F_piece3`, `F_piece1`, each one `linear_combination`).  At `t = π/2 − θ` the two
formulas agree by `E₄`.  Hence `gerverODE_Kg : GerverODE φ θ K B D`: **Thm 8.4.1 (1) and Romik's
Thm 8.4.2 hold for the upstream sofa.**

### 43.7 (I3″) now

`gerver_data : GerverNicheFacts φ θ (C(G)) B D` with the explicit tails; what is left is Thm 6.1.2
(injectivity: `σ_K` absolutely continuous on `[0, π/2)` and `(π/2, π]`, the arm functions continuous,
`f⁺_K, g⁺_K > 1`, i.e. `F > 0` on `(0, π/2)`) and the niche (Thm 8.4.1 (2): the tails and the core
lie in the closure of `N(K)` but not in `N(K)`, and `N(K)` lies below them).

## 44. Theorem 6.1.2 for Gerver's sofa: `C(G)` is an injective cap (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 40)

`Sofa/GerverInj.lean` (new); `Sofa/Main.lean` ((I3″) reduced to Thm 8.4.1 (2)).

Baek proves Theorem 6.1.2 in one line by citing Theorem 2 of [Ger92] ("`G` is a balanced maximum
sofa").  We verify Def 6.1.2 directly for `K = C(G)` (§43.1: `h_K = p₁ + 1` on `[0, π/2]`,
`h_K(· + π/2) = p₂ + 1`).

### 44.1 `p₁`, `p₂` are `C¹`

Off the break set `{φ, θ, π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ}` (`brkSet`, countable) `p₁`, `p₂` are differentiable with
the continuous derivatives `p₁'`, `p₂'` of §43.3.  `integral_eq_of_hasDerivAt_off_countable` gives
`p(b) − p(a) = ∫_a^b p'` for all `a, b` (`p₁_sub`, `p₂_sub`), hence `p = p(0) + ∫_0^· p'` and by
`integral_hasDerivAt_right` `HasDerivAt p (p'(t)) t` for **every** `t` (`hasDerivAt_p₁'`,
`hasDerivAt_p₂'`).  Also `0 ≤ p₁ + 1 ≤ 2`, `0 ≤ p₂ + 1 ≤ 4` on `[0, π/2]` (from `0 ≤ x, y ≤ 1` and
the bounds on `x₀`), and since `p₁'' = r(π/2 − ·) − p₁ − 1`, `p₂'' = r − p₂ − 1` off the break set
with `0 ≤ r ≤ 3/2`, `|p'(b) − p'(a)| ≤ 5(b − a)` on `[0, π/2]` (`dp₁_lip`, `dp₂_lip`: the same FTC
for `p'` and `norm_integral_le_of_norm_le_const`).

### 44.2 The edges

`edgeMax K t` and `edgeMin K t` are the right and left derivatives of `h_K` (§11), so
`edgeMax K t = p₁'(t)` on `[0, π/2)`, `edgeMin K t = p₁'(t)` on `(0, π/2]`, `edgeMax K (t + π/2) = p₂'(t)`
on `[0, π/2]`.  At the ends: `K = Cvx ⊆ {0 ≤ y ≤ 1, x ≤ 1}` with `(1, 0) ∈ K`, so `h_K(s) = cos s` on
`[−π/2, 0]` (`supportFn_Kg_neg`) and `edgeMin K 0 = 0` (`edgeMin_Kg_zero`); similarly
`h_K(s) = (4x₀ − 3) cos s` on `[π, 3π/2]` (`supportFn_Kg_pi`) and `edgeMax K π = 0`.  In particular
`p₁'(0) = 0 = p₂'(π/2)` and the edge of `K` in direction `0` has length `0` (`edgeLength_Kg_zero`).

### 44.3 `σ_K` is absolutely continuous (Def 6.1.2 (1))

`arcFn K b − arcFn K a = (edgeMax K b − edgeMax K a) + ∫_a^b h_K` (`arcFn_Kg_sub`), so by §44.1–44.2
`arcFn K` is `7`-Lipschitz on `[0, c]` for `c < π/2` and `9`-Lipschitz on `[π/2, π]`
(`arcFn_Kg_lip_left`, `arcFn_Kg_lip_right`).  The principle `sigmaK_restrict_ac` (§25) turns this
into `σ_K|(0, c] ≪ λ` and `σ_K|(π/2, π] ≪ λ`; with `σ_K{0} = edgeLength K 0 = 0` and
`[0, π/2) = {0} ∪ ⋃_n (0, π/2 − 1/(n+1)]` this gives `σ_K|[0, π/2) ≪ λ` (`sigmaK_Kg_ac_left`,
`sigmaK_Kg_ac_right`).

### 44.4 The arms (Def 6.1.2 (2), (3))

By §44.2, `f⁻_K(t) = h_K(t + π/2) − edgeMin K t` and `f⁺_K(t) = h_K(t + π/2) − edgeMax K t` are both
`p₂ + 1 − p₁' = F(t) + 1`, and
`g⁺_K(t) = h_K(t) + edgeMax K (t + π/2) = p₁ + 1 + p₂' = F(π/2 − t) + 1` (§43.6), continuous on
`[0, π/2]` (`armFm_Kg`, `armFp_Kg`, `armGp_Kg`).  So `f⁺, g⁺ > 1` on `(0, π/2)` is `F > 0` on
`(0, π/2)` (`F_pos`):

* `(φ, θ]`: `F = 2r − 1 = A + t − φ > 0` (`F_eq_two_r`);
* `(θ, π/2 − φ]`: `F = r > 0` (`F_eq_r`, `r_pos_mid`; on `(π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ]`,
  `r = B − s(1 + A)/2 − s²/4` with `0 ≤ s = π/2 − t − φ < θ − φ`, positive by the bounds on `A, B, φ, θ`);
* `(0, φ]`: `r = 1/2` gives `x = x₀ + sin/2`, `y = (1 + cos)/2` there (`x_piece0`, `y_piece0`), and
  `F = (3 − 3x₀) sin t − (1 − cos t)/2 ≥ (5/2 − 3x₀) sin t > 0` (`1 − cos t ≤ sin t`);
* `(π/2 − φ, π/2)`: `F = (3 − 3x₀) sin t − cos t/2 − 1` with `sin t ≥ 1 − φ²/2`.

Hence **`isInjectiveCap_Kg : IsInjectiveCap (C(G))`** — Theorem 6.1.2 for the upstream sofa.

### 44.5 (I3″) now

`GerverNicheFacts` has lost the field `injective`: (I3″) is exactly Thm 8.4.1 (2), the description of
the niche — the tails `B|(π/2 − θ, π/2)`, `D|(0, θ)` and the core `x_K|[φ, π/2 − φ]` lie in
`closure N(K)`, the tails do not lie in `N(K)`, and every point of `N(K)` lies strictly below a point
of `D|[0, θ] ∪ x_K|[φ, π/2 − φ] ∪ B|[π/2 − θ, π/2]` with the same `x`-coordinate.

## 45. Theorem 8.4.1 (2): the niche of Gerver's sofa (2026-09-23, Opus 5.5, round 41)

`Sofa/GerverArch.lean`, `Sofa/GerverNiche.lean` (new); `Sofa/Main.lean` (`gerver_data` proved: **the
main theorem is `sorry`-free**).

### 45.1 The niche as a union of quadrants

For `K = C(G)` and `s ∈ [0, π/2]`, `h_K(s) − 1 = p₁(s)` and `h_K(s + π/2) − 1 = p₂(s)`, so
`Q⁻_K(s) = {ca s < 0, cb s < 0}` with `ca s z = ⟪z, u_s⟫ − p₁(s)`, `cb s z = ⟪z, v_s⟫ − p₂(s)`, and
`N(K) = {y ≥ 0} ∩ ⋃_{0 < s < π/2} Q⁻(s)` (`mem_niche_Kg`).  The arch is
`D|[0, θ] ∪ x_K|[φ, π/2 − φ] ∪ B|[π/2 − θ, π/2]`, with `D(θ) = x_K(π/2 − φ) =: J_L` and
`B(π/2 − θ) = x_K(φ) =: J_R` (§43.5).  The main lemma is: **no point of the arch lies in any
`Q⁻(s)`**.

### 45.2 Tools

* `min_le_of_hasDerivAt_mul`: if `f' = ω k` on `(a, b)` with `ω > 0`, and `k` stays `≤ 0` once it
  is negative (e.g. `k` antitone), then `min(f(a), f(b)) ≤ f` on `[a, b]` (monotone up to the sign
  change of `k`, antitone after it).
* Monotonicity from a derivative of constant sign off a countable set (FTC with countably many
  exceptions): the tails are differentiable off the break points.
* Romik's `F = p₂ − p₁'` is increasing on `[φ, π/2 − φ]` (`F = A + t − φ` on `[φ, π/2 − θ]`,
  `F = B − σ(1 + A)/2 − σ²/4` with `σ = π/2 − t − φ` on `[π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ]`, equal at `π/2 − θ` by
  `E₄`), `G = p₁ + p₂' = F(π/2 − ·)` is decreasing, both `≥ A > 0`; `x_K' = −F u + G v`.
* The reflection `R(x, y) = (4x₀ − 2 − x, y)`: `p₂(π/2 − s) = p₁(s) − (4x₀ − 2) cos s` gives
  `ca s ∘ R = cb (π/2 − s)`, `cb s ∘ R = ca (π/2 − s)`, `R ∘ D(π/2 − ·) = B`, `R ∘ x_K(π/2 − ·) = x_K`.
* `s** = π/2 − φ − (√((1 + A)² + 4(B − 1)) − (1 + A)) ∈ (π/2 − θ, π/2 − φ)`: `r ≤ 1` on `(−∞, s**]`,
  `r ≥ 1` on `[s**, π/2 − φ]`.

### 45.3 The left tail

`d/dτ cb s (D τ) = −(1 − r(τ)) sin(s − τ)` and `d/dτ ca s (D τ) = (1 − r(τ)) cos(s − τ)` off the break
points, and `cb s (D s) = 0` (the envelope touches its wall).

* `s ≤ s**`: for `τ` between `s` and `t` (all `≤ s**`, so `1 − r ≥ 0`) the derivative of
  `cb s (D ·)` has the sign of `τ − s`, so `cb s (D t) ≥ cb s (D s) = 0` (`cb_Dg_nonneg_low`).
* `s ∈ [θ, π/2]`: `cb s (D ·)` decreases on `[0, θ]`, so `cb s (D t) ≥ cb s (J_L)` (`cb_Dg_ge_theta`).
* `s ∈ [π/2 − φ, π/2]`: `s − τ ∈ [π/4, π/2]` for `τ ∈ [0, θ]` (`φ + θ < π/4`), so
  `ca s (D ·) + cb s (D ·)` decreases and is `≥ ca s J_L + cb s J_L` (`ca_add_cb_Dg_ge_theta`).

### 45.4 The junction `J_L`

* **(J1)** `cb s J_L ≥ 0` for `s ∈ [0, π/2 − φ]`: for `s ≤ s**` by 45.3 with `t = θ`.  For
  `s ∈ [s**, π/2 − φ]` (**Sturm**): `f(s) = cb s J_L` has `f' = −⟪J_L, u_s⟫ − p₂'`,
  `f'' + f = 1 − r ≤ 0`, `f(s**) ≥ 0`, `f(π/2 − φ) = cb (π/2 − φ) (x_K(π/2 − φ)) = 0`.  With `c` the
  midpoint and `W = f' cos(s − c) + f sin(s − c)`, `W' = (1 − r) cos(s − c) ≤ 0`, and
  `(f / cos(· − c))' = W / cos²(· − c)`, so `f / cos(· − c)` is quasi-concave and `f ≥ 0`
  (`cb_JL_sturm`).  (At `s = θ`, `f` has a double zero: `J_L = D(θ)` is the envelope point.)
* **(J4)**, **(J5)** for `s ∈ [π/2 − φ, π/2]`: `p₁ = x₀ cos s + (sin s − 1)/2`, `p₂ = (3 − 4x₀) sin s − 1`
  there (`r(π/2 − s) = 1/2`, `r(s) = 0`), `J_L = p₁(π/2 − φ) u + p₂(π/2 − φ) v` with
  `p₁(π/2 − φ) ≤ 1/100`, `p₂(π/2 − φ) ≥ 119/100`; `ca s J_L` and `ca s J_L + cb s J_L` vanish at
  `π/2 − φ` and have positive derivative (`ca_JL_nonneg_top`, `ca_add_cb_JL_top`).
* **(J2)**, **(J3)** at `J_R = R(J_L)`: `ca s J_R ≥ 0` for `s ≥ φ`, `cb s J_R ≥ 0` for `s ≤ φ`.

### 45.5 The core and the right tail

For `t ∈ [φ, π/2 − φ]`:

* `t ≤ s`: `τ ↦ ca s (x_K τ)` has derivative `cos(s − τ)(G tan(s − τ) − F)` with the bracket
  decreasing on `[φ, min(s, π/2 − φ)]`.  So it is quasi-concave and `≥ min(ca s J_R, 0 or ca s J_L) ≥ 0`
  (`ca_xKg_nonneg`).
* `s ≤ t`: `τ ↦ cb s (x_K τ)` has derivative `cos(τ − s)(G − F tan(τ − s))` on
  `[max(s, φ), π/2 − φ]` and is `≥ min(0 or cb s J_R, cb s J_L) ≥ 0` (`cb_xKg_nonneg`).

The right tail follows from the left tail by the reflection (`Bg_not_mem`).  Outside the arch the
`x`-axis is not reached: `cb s (D 0) ≥ 0` (for `s ≥ π/2 − φ` directly, `cb s (D 0) = 1 − (1 − x₀) sin s`)
and `ca s (B(π/2)) ≥ 0`.

### 45.6 Theorem 8.4.1 (2)

* **Not in the niche**: `B(t)`, `D(t)` avoid every `Q⁻(s)` (`Bg_not_mem_niche`, `Dg_not_mem_niche`).
* **In the closure**:
  - `D(t) + δ e₀ ∈ Q⁻(t)` for small `δ > 0` (`ca = −G + δ cos t`, `cb = −δ sin t`), and `D_y ≥ 0`
    (`D_y' = (1 − r) sin ≥ 0`, `D_y(0) = 0`);
  - `B(t) − δ e₀` is the mirror image of this;
  - `x_K(t) − δ(u_t + v_t) ∈ Q⁻(t)`, with `x_K(t)_y > 0`: `(x_K)_y' = cos(G − F tan)` is quasi-concave
    and positive at the ends (`x_K(π/2 − φ)_y ≥ 1.19 sin φ − 0.001 > 0`).
* **Below the arch** (`niche_below`): let `q ∈ Q⁻(s)`, `q_y ≥ 0`. By the last paragraph of 45.5,
  `x(D(0)) ≤ q_x ≤ x(B(π/2))`. By the intermediate value theorem on the three pieces (continuous
  `x`-coordinates; `x(D(θ)) = x(x_K(π/2 − φ))`, `x(x_K(φ)) = x(B(π/2 − θ))`) some arch point `P` has
  `P_x = q_x`.  If `P_y ≤ q_y` then `ca s P ≤ ca s q < 0` and `cb s P ≤ cb s q < 0`, which
  contradicts 45.3–45.5.

Hence `gerver_data : GerverNicheFacts φ θ (C(G)) B D`: **(I3″) is proved, and
`sofaConstant_eq_volume_gerversSofa' : sofaConstant = volume gerversSofa` depends only on `propext`,
`Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`.**
